# Firefox Nova port progress

## Goal and status

Port textfox to Firefox Nova while preserving the TUI appearance, existing configuration, accessibility, and browser functionality. Support is not complete. Do not enable Nova for textfox users or remove the README warning until the checks below pass.

Baseline: repository commit `79f8391`, Firefox 159.0 on Linux, and Nix 2.20.6 through rootless nix-portable. The working tree contains uncommitted inspection-tool changes. Nix-portable uses proot and cannot prove NixOS VM or activation behavior that requires mount namespaces or a real Nix daemon.

## Completed

- Reviewed the plan independently with DeepSeek and Grok. Both identified the Nova preference, existing profile migration, CSS delivery order, and live verification as priorities.
- Confirmed that Firefox release source defines `browser.nova.enabled` as `true` and uses it in browser chrome as well as new-tab content. The earlier assumption that Nova was limited to new-tab was wrong.
- Confirmed that textfox sets `browser.nova.enabled` to `false` in `user.js` and `nix/pkgs/wrapTextfox.nix`. Home-manager reads `user.js`; manual installation of that file is optional.
- Updated `tools/firefox-chrome/firefox-live.sh` to set `MOZ_REMOTE_ALLOW_SYSTEM_ACCESS=1` for the disposable browser and to accept `--nova on` or `--nova off`. Firefox 159 rejected chrome-context access without the environment variable, despite the existing command-line flag.
- Updated `tools/firefox-chrome/ff_inspect.py` to report the effective Nova preference.
- Added `tools/firefox-chrome/test-firefox-live.sh`. It passed against Firefox 159 in headless mode and verified that `#nav-bar` exists with the requested Nova preference in both states. This is not a full theme test.
- Ran `nix shell nixpkgs#nixfmt -c sh -c 'find . -type f -name "*.nix" -not -path "./.git/*" -exec nixfmt --check {} +'`. It passed. The documented `nix run nixpkgs#nixfmt-tree -- --check` failed because the current `treefmt` rejects `--check`.

## Current investigation

A disposable Firefox 159 headless profile loads `about:newtab`, but its `#root` has no children after waiting. This occurs with Nova on and off, including when browser chrome opens a trusted new tab. The page reports `readyState` as `complete`, is visible, and has a nonzero viewport. Browser chrome reports delayed startup complete. The browser console showed telemetry client-ID errors but no established cause for the empty new-tab root.

A second headless probe used an unthemed profile with Nova on and opened `about:home`. After 15 seconds, its `#root` was also empty, although `javascript.enabled` was true and the document contained the expected new-tab script elements. This isolates neither headless mode nor Marionette as the cause, but shows that textfox CSS is not necessary to reproduce the observation. No `DISPLAY`, `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, Xvfb, or xvfb-run was available in this session. The temporary probes and their JSON output are under `/tmp/textfox-*` and may disappear.

A four-way headless browser-chrome comparison found `#nav-bar` in vanilla and textfox profiles with Nova both on and off. The effective preference matched the requested state. Textfox computed a transparent background and a 2px solid border in both states, unlike vanilla. This validates only that one element and its computed styles, not layout or interaction.

The empty root was caused by the automation baseline, not established as a theme defect. Firefox 159's bundled `chrome/remote/content/shared/RecommendedPreferences.sys.mjs` sets `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.testing.shouldInitializeFeeds` to `false` and disables widgets for automation. Its new-tab bundle returns no content until `App.initialized` is true. In a disposable vanilla profile, setting `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.testing.shouldInitializeFeeds` to `true` before startup produced a populated `about:home` root in headless Firefox. Setting the feeds preference and `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.widgets.enabled` to `true` produced populated `about:newtab` roots in textfox profiles with the browser Nova preference requested both on and off. These probes do not verify the effective new-tab Nova state or visual correctness. The `.nova-enabled` element appeared in both runs, so do not use the browser preference alone as proof that the new-tab implementation switched.

A headed Firefox session on a disposable headless Sway/Wayland compositor also produced an empty root with the original automation preferences. Disabling all recommended remote preferences caused a Marionette timeout in one attempt; use targeted preference overrides for further tests instead. The temporary probes are under `/tmp/textfox-*` and may disappear.

`firefox-live.sh --newtab` now probes initialized new-tab content in a disposable profile and reports Firefox build, effective preferences, selector presence, and computed styles. `--nova on` and `--nova off` explicitly set both the browser-wide preference and the separate `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.nova.enabled` preference. The feed and widget overrides apply only to this disposable probe. These profiles may make Firefox's normal new-tab network requests.

On Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the four-way headless probe reported the requested effective preferences and the expected `.nova-enabled` or `.classic-enabled` container. Both themes populated the new tab. For `.tile`, textfox computed `border-radius: 0px` and `box-shadow: none` in both states; vanilla computed 12px with Nova and 16px with classic, both with shadows. This confirms a limited userContent style difference, not layout, contrast, or interaction correctness. The test and JSON probe are repeatable; temporary results are in `/tmp/tf-newtab-*` when available.

A headed probe on a disposable Sway headless compositor at 1280×800 repeated the four aligned-state checks on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. All four new-tab roots initialized and exposed the expected Nova or classic container. Textfox tiles computed a 0px radius and no shadow in both states; vanilla tiles computed 12px with Nova and 16px with classic, both with shadows. JSON results are in `/tmp/tf-headed.A5PtCN/` and may disappear. This was a headed rendering environment, but the probe did not inspect screenshots, layout, focus, keyboard interaction, or menus.

A further headed Sway probe requested all four combinations of browser and new-tab Nova preferences, each with vanilla and textfox profiles. All eight new-tab roots initialized; the observed Nova or classic container followed the requested new-tab preference regardless of the requested browser preference. This probe did not read back effective preferences. After a body click, its first eight Tab presses reached the same sequence of element types and classes in vanilla and textfox for each preference combination. This is a limited focus-order observation, not proof of visible focus, complete keyboard access, or equivalent behavior inside shadow roots and iframes. The probe and JSONL are `/tmp/tf-matrix-probe.py` and `/tmp/tf-matrix.Y5QPPF/results.jsonl`; they may disappear. New-tab widgets can vary with profile and network state.

A repeat of the eight headed combinations read back both effective Nova preferences; each matched its requested value. In each profile, the probe programmatically focused one widget menu button, sent Enter, observed a visible `role=menu`, then sent Escape and observed it close with focus on the button. It tested the timer menu in classic and the widgets header menu in Nova, not menu item navigation or activation. Results are `/tmp/tf-menu.6s2yfx/results.jsonl`; the temporary probe may disappear. Its first attempt failed because it called a nonexistent Marionette API; the probe was corrected before this run.

In another headed run, Enter opened the same widget menus, and Arrow Down focused the first menu item's shadow-DOM button in all eight combinations. Screenshots show a visible focus outline on that item in the inspected textfox Nova and classic cases. The textfox Nova screenshot also shows the ASCII logo overlapping the search field and the Wikipedia shortcut label wrapping mid-word; the vanilla Nova screenshot does not show those two effects. These are observations at this viewport and profile, not yet root-caused or tested at other sizes. Screenshots and JSONL are in `/tmp/tf-focus.7aoGGZ/` and may disappear. Menu item activation was not tested.

A follow-up headed Nova probe compared computed geometry. In textfox, `.wordmark` is 294×64 CSS pixels but its `::before` renders the five-line `--tf-newtab-logo` at 14px; vanilla uses a 105×64 wordmark without generated text. The textfox logo/search screenshot shows apparent overlap, but the probe did not measure pseudo-element ink bounds, so the precise overlap and cause remain unverified. Both vanilla and textfox shortcut title boxes are 75px wide; the computed title font differs: Adwaita Sans in vanilla and SF Mono/Consolas/monospace in textfox. This supports font width as a hypothesis for the screenshot's mid-word wrapping, not a confirmed cause. Geometry and screenshots are in `/tmp/tf-geometry.Sc9uNJ/`; they may disappear.

A headed Nova font intervention measured the Wikipedia shortcut text via DOM range rectangles. With textfox's default font it occupied two lines (70.4px and 8.8px); changing only `--tf-font-family` to Adwaita Sans at runtime reduced it to one 67.9px line. The shortcut title has `word-break: break-word`, and `chrome/content/about.css` applies the configured font to all about-page elements. This confirms the font choice causes this observed wrap in that profile and viewport; changing the font globally would defeat the user's configured font. Earlier attempts to override individual elements did not win the user stylesheet's `!important` rule. Results are `/tmp/tf-font.LtBtjf/results.jsonl` and may disappear.

A temporary headed probe reproduced the logo text as a hidden DOM element with the pseudo-element's computed font and whitespace settings. Its estimated last line bottom was 112.5px, below the search wrapper's 96.25px top, while the wordmark starts at 22.5px. This supports a vertical overflow cause, but is an approximation rather than a measurement of the actual pseudo-element ink or proof of painted-pixel overlap. The same probe widened the outer shortcut `.title` to 90px; its computed width changed, but Wikipedia still occupied two lines, so widening that element alone is insufficient. Results are `/tmp/tf-layout.u2XFWp/results.jsonl` and may disappear.

Next, build a repeatable failing layout check, trace the shortcut's nested width constraints and the logo's Nova layout, test menu item activation and broader focus behavior, then inventory other components. These targeted probes do not establish visual or accessibility correctness.

## Remaining work

- [ ] Pin the target Firefox release and build for each delivery path. Record the effective Nova preference and its default.
- [ ] Establish a working live baseline for Nova new-tab and browser chrome. Capture vanilla and textfox behavior with Nova on and, while supported upstream, off.
- [ ] Inventory current theme selectors and Firefox-owned variables against Firefox source and live DOM. Cover tabs, sidebar, toolbar, URL bar, menus, panels, findbar, split view, private windows, new-tab, and supported `about:` pages.
- [ ] Port verified gaps in component CSS. Preserve existing `--tf-*` overrides, TUI styling, visible focus, keyboard access, contrast, forced colors, reduced motion, and RTL behavior.
- [ ] Check CSS delivery separately for manual installation, home-manager, and NixOS. Compare `@import` order with the fixed concatenation order in `nix/pkgs/wrapTextfox.nix`; verify user config precedence, icons, and NixOS restart behavior. Do not infer activation success from evaluation alone.
- [ ] Test existing configuration options against computed styles. If adding a `--tf-*` option, update `chrome/defaults.css`, `nix/modules/options.nix` option definitions, and generated `configCss` together.
- [ ] Design and verify preference migration for fresh and existing profiles across all delivery paths. Removing `user_pref(..., false)` alone may leave a persisted user preference. Flip or retire the preference only after Nova UI checks pass.
- [ ] Exercise the interaction and accessibility matrix on real Firefox. Record platform and environment limits. Update README support and migration guidance only after verification.
- [ ] Run the live regression test, relevant Nix checks, `git diff --check`, and independent review before claiming support.

## Pause checkpoint

`tools/firefox-chrome/test-nova-layout-live.py` now creates a disposable profile, sets both Nova preferences, inserts a deterministic Wikipedia shortcut label, and reports DOM geometry. With Firefox 159.0, its headed 1280×800 run and a separate headless run both reported an estimated logo/search overlap and a wrapped shortcut. Logo line boxes are reconstructed from pseudo-element styles, not measured painted pixels. The check assumes a usable shortcut and default font; the disposable new-tab profile may make network requests. Run it with `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-nova-layout-live.py`; it currently exits 1 for those two observations.

Firefox's Nova wordmark has a 64px fixed height; textfox's generated logo exceeds it. Testing Nova-scoped `height: auto` increased the wordmark to 90px but left search at the same position and did not clear the estimated overlap. That experimental change was removed. Trace the surrounding grid and responsive placement before another fix. Nova's shortcut label and ancestor tile are each 75px wide; widening only the outer title did not prevent wrapping. Do not replace the configured monospace font or assume labels can overflow neighboring tiles safely. No theme CSS fix has been applied.

A delivery audit found that NixOS concatenation order differs from manual imports. The README also says the install script keeps existing `config.css` in place, but the script moves the whole old `chrome/` directory to a timestamped backup and creates a new one without restoring that config. Existing `user.js` is overwritten if the optional copy is accepted. These are source-level observations; update and interruption behavior require safe migration design and tests under T5. A Firefox 159 disposable-profile probe confirmed that the former update branch's recursive removal deleted an unrelated `chrome/config.css`; a deliberately failed copy also left the directory absent. The probe exercised equivalent `nsIFile` operations, not generated autoconfig or NixOS activation. As an interim containment, the branch now reports an error and leaves existing profile files untouched when the current store marker is missing. This deliberately blocks updates and installation into existing chrome directories; existing users retain old styles, but their icons may disappear if Nix garbage-collects the old store path referenced by those styles. A failed first install can leave a partial chrome directory that this guard also refuses on retry. Preserve the old store generation and back up the entire profile before any manual recovery; no recovery procedure is verified yet. `node tools/firefox-chrome/test-nixos-update.js` exercises the extracted branch with mocked Firefox file APIs, including fresh, current and update paths. This is not a real Firefox autoconfig or NixOS activation test. Design and verify a safe ownership, migration and retry protocol before restoring updates. Do not delete profile files to work around the block. Manual reinstall backs up the old chrome directory but does not restore its config automatically.

`nix eval --impure --expr 'builtins.typeOf (import ./nix/modules/nixos.nix {})'` failed on an undefined `policies` variable. Removed that unbound assignment; the same import now returns `"lambda"`. A full offline `nixosSystem` evaluation timed out after 90 seconds in this environment. The import check does not prove module evaluation, wrapper build, policies, or activation work.

Nova's sidebar occupies a fixed 150px grid track while the default generated logo is approximately 294px wide. Search is in a neighboring column with overlapping row spans. Headless runtime interventions moving search or widening grid tracks avoided the estimated collision at one viewport but altered Firefox layout and lack responsive, RTL, and interaction validation; none was retained. Independent review found the layout probe can miss clipped text or misestimate pseudo-element geometry. Its success case now reports inconclusive rather than passing, and it checks the disposable profile identity before opening a tab. Preserve the Nova warning and preference until the full matrix passes.

Working tree at pause: modified `tools/firefox-chrome/ff_inspect.py` and `tools/firefox-chrome/firefox-live.sh`; untracked `tools/firefox-chrome/ff_newtab.py`, `tools/firefox-chrome/test-firefox-live.sh`, `tools/firefox-chrome/test-newtab-live.sh`, `NOVA-PORT.md`, and pre-existing `.pi/`. These files are not committed. Preserve existing changes and `.pi/`. `git diff --check` passed at pause; it does not check untracked files. Temporary probe scripts and screenshots under `/tmp` are not durable.

## T1 continuation

Firefox found on PATH is `$HOME/.local/bin/firefox`, linked to `$HOME/opt/firefox/firefox`. On this Linux host it reports Firefox 159.0; the disposable vanilla new-tab probe reports build `20261022150525`. With neither Nova preference explicitly set by the probe, it reports both effective preferences as `true` and displays `.nova-enabled`. This is an effective-value observation under Marionette and targeted feed/widget overrides, not an independent reading of Firefox's default preference branch.

On this run, `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-firefox-live.sh` and `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-newtab-live.sh` exited successfully. They exercise both aligned Nova states, not all independent preference combinations or actual installation paths.

Manual installation targets the user's selected Firefox profile and does not choose a browser build. Home-manager enables `programs.firefox` but this module does not pin its Firefox package; host configuration determines it. NixOS defaults `textfox.package` to `pkgs.firefox-unwrapped` and permits an override. The flake lock identifies nixpkgs revision `0726a0ecb6d4e08f6adced58726b95db924cef57`, but does not pin user-supplied packages. An offline evaluation of that revision's `x86_64-linux.firefox-unwrapped.version` exceeded 60 seconds and was stopped; the cause is unknown. Nix delivery versions, build IDs, and actual delivery-path baselines remain unverified. T1 remains in progress.

A fresh disposable Firefox 159.0 profile reported `true` for both Nova preferences on the default branch and effective branch, with no user value for either. `ff_newtab.py` now reports `defaultPreferences` separately from effective `preferences`; the new-tab regression checks both Nova defaults are booleans in all four aligned theme/state runs. The new assertion failed before implementation with a missing `defaultPreferences` key and passed afterward. A vanilla run confirmed both defaults as `true`, while automation overrides feed and widget defaults from `false` to `true`. A separate offline `nix flake metadata` also timed out after 20 seconds, while `nix eval --offline --impure --expr '1 + 1'` succeeded. The flake timeout cause remains unknown.

## Parallel T1 continuation

`firefox-live.sh --nova off --newtab-nova on --newtab` can request browser and new-tab Nova preferences independently. `test-newtab-live.sh` now checks all eight vanilla/textfox and preference combinations in headless Firefox, including effective values, initialized content, and both Nova/classic container selectors. The new test initially failed on the missing flag, then passed after implementation. `ff_newtab.py` checks the connected profile path and launched process before opening the new tab; a disposable wrong-profile check returned the expected error. At that point the default Marionette port could collide, and browser-chrome inspection did not yet verify profile identity; see the later browser-chrome probe identity section. This matrix does not prove layout, accessibility, or installed delivery paths.

The `flake.lock` GitHub input and `flake.nix` channel tarball declaration differ. At the locked nixpkgs revision `0726a0ecb6d4e08f6adced58726b95db924cef57`, the upstream `pkgs/applications/networking/browsers/firefox/packages/firefox.nix` declares Firefox 150.0. This is source-level evidence, not a verified Nix evaluation or build; host package overrides can differ. The mismatch may contribute to offline evaluation timeout, but cause is not established. No lock file was changed.

## Layout and selector continuation

The Nova layout probe now checks shortcut text rectangles against the viewport and overflow-clipping ancestors. A deliberately clipped DOM fixture checks that this detection is exercised. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the fixture was detected as clipped, while the Wikipedia shortcut occupied two lines and had no detected clipping. The probe exited 1 for that wrap and the estimated logo/search overlap. It still cannot prove painted pseudo-element geometry, visible focus, or responsive and RTL behavior; no CSS fix was applied.

A bounded source inventory of URL bar and new-tab selectors found that current Firefox release source emits `.urlbarView-results` where textfox targets `.urlbarView-body-inner` and `.urlbarView-body-outer`. Firefox's one-offs separator condition tests `[noresults]` on `#urlbar`, whereas textfox tests it on `.urlbarView`. These are source-level discrepancies, not confirmed visual defects. Inspect the live results DOM, slot assignment, and computed borders before changing them. New-tab `.logo-and-wordmark`, `.top-site-outer`, and `--newtab-background-color-secondary` remain present in release source; parent hover and focus states and forced-color cascade still need live checks. Searchfox's release branch is moving and was not independently pinned to the investigated build. Other components remain uninventoried.

Delivery-path inspection confirms manual installation uses the selected profile and does not select a Firefox build, home-manager uses the host's Firefox package, and NixOS accepts a `textfox.package` override. Per-path package builds, effective preferences, actual installation and real NixOS activation remain unverified. The manual and NixOS CSS source orders differ; browser processing must be tested before drawing conclusions about precedence.

## URL bar fix and further source inventory

In disposable headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, open classic URL bar results had a 1px top border because textfox's old `.urlbarView-body-inner` selector no longer matched the results element. Nova results had no top border. A narrow reset for the current direct-child `.urlbarView-results` removes the classic separator while retaining the configured outer border. `test-urlbar-live.py` checks both browser Nova states, profile identity, the current selector, and non-default accent and border width. A negative-control run without the reset failed on the classic 1px border; the updated probe passed both states, including under `python -O`. This covers computed styles for populated results, not painted appearance, accessibility, or delivery paths. A visible one-offs or true `noresults` state was not obtained; its selector discrepancy remains unverified and unchanged.

Additional moving-release source inventory identified potential stale tab color variables, `#vertical-pinned-tabs-container`, and `#sidebar-main` selectors in `chrome/tabs.css` and `chrome/sidebar.css`. Current Firefox source uses different tab color tokens, a `#pinned-tabs-container`, and a `sidebar-main` element; live values, matching, and sidebar shadow boundaries need verification. In `chrome/menus.css`, Firefox source paints panels using `--panel-background-color` and exposes panel content through a shadow part, while textfox sets `--panel-background` and targets ordinary descendant selectors. Actual panel appearance and cascade remain unverified. Findbar source retains the local textbox and checkbox selectors, but focus, contrast, split view, and interaction need live checks. These source observations are not confirmed defects or a complete T2 inventory.

## Latest pause: tab and app-menu investigations

A disposable headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` probe enabled horizontal tabs and set `--tf-bg: rgb(20, 40, 60)`. Selected and pseudo-hovered textfox tab backgrounds computed transparent with browser Nova both on and off, while vanilla tabs had distinct fills. In these profiles, the Firefox variables used by the textfox tab color definitions, `--toolbar-field-color` and `--toolbar-bgcolor`, computed empty. Hover used Firefox pseudo-class locking, not pointer interaction. An experimental `--tf-bg`-derived fill produced black selected-tab text on dark blue in screenshots, so it was reverted. No tab fix was retained. A safe fix must preserve configured colors and verify foreground contrast; headed rendering, forced colors, other themes, and delivery paths remain unverified. Temporary probes `/tmp/tf-tab-investigate.py` and `/tmp/tf-tabs-test-experiment.py`, and screenshots `/tmp/tf-tabs-paint-*.png`, may disappear. Experimental screenshots require the reverted experiment and cannot be regenerated from the current checkout alone.

A separate disposable headless app-menu probe opened `#appMenu-popup` in vanilla and textfox profiles with browser Nova on and off, checking profile identity and effective preference. With textfox `--tf-bg: rgb(23, 47, 71)`, `--tf-accent: rgb(19, 83, 137)`, `--tf-border-width: 5px`, and `--tf-rounding: 7px`, the shadow content slot inherited the configured `--panel-background` but used `--panel-background-color: Field` and computed white. Its border and radius followed the configured values. This is a computed-style/configuration discrepancy, not proof of painted appearance or contrast; keyboard interaction, other panels, platforms, and delivery paths remain unverified. The reproduction is retained in the subagent output at `$HOME/.pi/agent/sessions/<project>/subagent-artifacts/outputs/3cae61a1-001d-4341-9e35-5259a18d54c6/task-2.md`; that artifact may be subject to retention.

A parallel URL bar no-results investigation stopped before producing findings when `jev-guard` blocked a scout's command. The guard was not bypassed. Reassess the blocked command and investigate safely before retrying; do not change guard configuration as a workaround. No tracked code changes remained from these investigations. T1 through T4 remain incomplete, and Nova support remains unclaimed.

## URL bar one-offs selector

Firefox release `view-proton.css` tests `[noresults]` on `#urlbar`, while textfox tested it on `.urlbarView`. A disposable Firefox 159.0 probe opened populated results, temporarily removed the one-offs `hidden` attribute, and toggled `#urlbar[noresults]`. The old rule computed a 3px separator in both states; `test-urlbar-live.py` failed on the no-results state. The corrected selector computes the configured 3px separator with results and 0px without results in both browser Nova states. The one-offs element still computed `display: none` in the fixture and the no-results attribute was simulated; this proves the selector response, not a naturally occurring visible one-offs interaction or painted appearance. Existing V5 and V6 cover this class; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. T2 and T4 remain open.

## Manual and Nix default configuration

`tools/firefox-chrome/check-config-defaults.py` evaluates Nix defaults and compares every `--tf-*` default against the manual CSS in two disposable Firefox profiles, including computed new-tab logo pseudo-content. The check first failed because Nix's default logo differed in spacing, characters and trailing newline from `chrome/defaults.css`. The Nix default now matches the manual logo; the comparison passed on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. The probe uses cached nixpkgs and disposable new-tab feeds that may make network requests. It compares computed defaults, not full option behavior, home-manager installation or actual NixOS activation. Existing V4 and V9 cover this class; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. T6 remains open.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/check-config-defaults.py /nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source`, substituting an available nixpkgs source.

## Nix icon URL generation

Auditing every icon URL in the built Nix CSS found `chrome://global/skin/icons/close.svg` corrupted into a URL containing the Nix store path. The wrapper's `sed` pattern `./icons` treated `.` as a wildcard and replaced part of Firefox's `skin/icons`. The pattern now matches the literal `./icons/` prefix. The generated-CSS probe failed on the corrupt reference, then rebuilt and verified 90 distinct referenced SVG files plus the intact Firefox chrome URL. This checks generated files, not Firefox rendering of every icon or store garbage-collection behavior. Existing V2 and V9 cover icon preservation; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. T5 remains open.

## Recent-activity card selectors

`probe-newtab-cards.py` creates five visited bookmarks in each disposable profile, enables recent-activity feeds, disables top sites for the fixture, and toggles the bookmark-inclusion preference to trigger a feed refresh after the section initializes. The enabled feeds may make network requests. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, all 16 `about:newtab`/`about:home`, independent browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and vanilla/textfox combinations produced a real bookmarked recent-activity card. In the eight themed samples, its outer border computed 7px solid and the outer, card, and preview top-left radii computed 3px, matching non-default configuration. The nested `.card-context` contained `Bookmarked` but computed `display: none` in textfox; in vanilla it computed `display: flex` with nonzero geometry. Tagged Firefox 159 [card source](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/extensions/newtab/content-src/components/Card/Card.jsx) renders the context label inside that element.

A follow-up reads Firefox's Marionette accessibility properties for the card link and its descendants. In all 16 profiles the link role was `link` and its computed label contained the hostname and test title but not `Bookmarked`. The direct context-label accessibility-properties request returned null in both vanilla and textfox samples; the inspected link subtree exposed no descendant label containing `Bookmarked`. This does not establish why Firefox omitted that label or whether equivalent information appears elsewhere in the accessibility tree. The probe does not establish screen-reader behavior, painted readability, other card types, or installed delivery. No production fix was applied. The initial fixture seeded bookmarks without visited history and found no cards; tagged `NewTabUtils.sys.mjs` requires `last_visit_date > 0` for recent bookmarks. A second fixture confirmed Places returned visited bookmarks but the feed needed a refresh after section initialization. Existing V5 and V6 cover readability and real UI behavior; no new invariant is proposed without establishing user impact. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-newtab-cards.py`.

## Additional about-page fonts

`test-about-font-live.py` now also samples `about:firefoxview`, `about:protections`, `about:processes`, `about:memory`, and `about:robots`. In headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, both Nova states computed the configured monospace font on each page body and the probe's selected light-DOM controls; sampled Firefox View shadow controls also computed monospace. The profiles were disposable and the probe did not activate page controls. Empty or default content limits coverage of populated states, other shadow roots, interaction, painted readability, and delivery paths.

An exploratory `about:logins` run initially found no controls because the probe's element-name list omitted Firefox's custom login hosts. Tagged [`aboutLogins.html`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/aboutlogins/content/aboutLogins.html) defines `login-list`, `login-item`, and related hosts. After adding those hosts, an empty disposable profile produced five sampled hosts and 15 direct shadow controls with the configured monospace font in each Nova state. The initial empty result was a probe coverage gap, not an established theme defect. A follow-up inserts one synthetic login into each verified disposable profile before navigating to `about:logins`. In both Nova states, the populated login row and its title and username nodes inside a nested shadow root computed monospace. The probe checks only a boolean match for the synthetic username and does not read a real profile or print the synthetic password. An initial follow-up run failed because populated `about:logins` adds a login GUID fragment to its URL while the fixture compared the whole URL; matching the base URL resolved this probe bug. Other populated states, deeper shadow trees, interaction, and painted output remain untested. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-about-font-live.py`.

## About downloads font

Tagged Firefox 159.0 maps `about:downloads` to [`contentAreaDownloadsView.xhtml`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/downloads/content/contentAreaDownloadsView.xhtml), a XUL `window` rather than an HTML document with a body. `test-about-font-live.py` now checks the body when present or the document element otherwise, and verifies the downloads root and list fixture. In both browser Nova states on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the root and sampled `richlistbox` computed the configured monospace font. The disposable download list was empty; this does not cover download entries, actions, shadow internals, painted text, or delivery paths. The first exploratory probe assumed an HTML body and failed on this XUL document; that was a fixture assumption, not a product defect. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-about-font-live.py`.

## About-page index and font probe

`test-about-font-live.py` now includes `about:about`, checks its link hosts, and records its `about:` link targets. In two headless runs on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, both browser Nova states listed 45 targets and the index body and sampled links computed the configured monospace font. The index is Firefox's exposed list in these profiles, not a declaration that every target is supported by textfox or that hidden, parameterized, privileged, or platform-specific pages are covered. Existing page samples and their shadow-control limits remain unchanged.

During probe development one `about:preferences` run returned empty computed font strings on several `moz-button` hosts and their shadow buttons; a later run passed. The cause was not captured. The probe now waits up to ten seconds only while all differing values are empty, and fails on persistent empties or any nonempty mismatch. It reports DOM connection, display, and geometry on failures rather than treating missing values as success. Two subsequent runs passed all six pages in both Nova states. This bounds an observed fixture instability, not proof that its cause is fixed. Existing V6 covers reliable runtime evidence. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-about-font-live.py`.

## Home and new-tab preference matrix

`firefox-live.sh --newtab --newtab-page about:home` now inspects home content; the default remains `about:newtab`. `test-newtab-live.sh` checks both pages across all independent browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and vanilla/textfox combinations. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, all 16 disposable profiles initialized the requested page and expected Nova/classic container; themed tiles computed a 2px solid border, zero radius, and no box shadow. Effective preferences and boolean defaults were read back. The probe enables disposable feeds and widgets, which may make network requests. It does not establish painted layout, interactions, accessibility, or installed delivery behavior.

During test development Marionette returned `null` once while content navigated, and the inspector raised `TypeError`. The bounded wait now treats that transitional result as not initialized and reports the last result on timeout. This was an inspector failure, not an established product defect; existing V6 and V8 cover reliable state evidence. Invalid page values and `--newtab-page` without `--newtab` are rejected. Run `sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-newtab-live.sh`.

## New-tab border configuration

The actual Nix-generated `userContent.css` probe found shortcut tile border computed `0px none` with `--tf-border-width: 7px`. `chrome/content/newtab.css` referenced Firefox's `--border-width` rather than textfox's setting and derived `--newtab-border-color` from `--newtab-wallpaper-color`, which was unset without wallpaper. The invalid color made the whole border declaration invalid. New-tab borders now use `--tf-border-width`, and color mixes fall back to Firefox's `--newtab-background-color` when no wallpaper color exists. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the generated CSS now gives Nova tiles a 7px solid border both without wallpaper and with a runtime wallpaper-color override; manual-import regression checks default 2px tile borders across all browser/new-tab Nova combinations. The original wallpaper check changed only a CSS variable. A follow-up configured Firefox's solid-color wallpaper preference and verified the body received `#14283c`; tile borders remained 7px solid with and without content forced colors. The generated CSS also produced a 7px solid classic-new-tab border without wallpaper and with the solid wallpaper plus forced colors; effective new-tab Nova state was checked. This does not cover uploaded or image wallpapers or painted contrast. The manual new-tab regression initially raced tile initialization in one run; `ff_newtab.py` and its launcher now support an explicit selector wait, and the matrix passes after waiting for `.tile`. A disposable content forced-colors run also reported a 7px solid tile border with and without the wallpaper-color override; the media query was verified active. A stress fixture setting both source colors to `Canvas` made the tile border compute to the same color as the forced-colors canvas; the red check normalized both colors through a canvas pixel. The new-tab stylesheet now uses system `CanvasText` for its border token when content forced colors are active, and that check passes. The color comparison includes a Canvas pixel negative control. Initial attempts to override the tile or body token from author CSS lost to user-origin `!important`; the test uses a canvas-color negative control instead. This uses temporary style overrides to exercise the failure, not a real OS theme. Painted accessibility, home-manager and actual NixOS activation remain unverified. Existing V4–V6 cover this class; a §B entry requires approval under spec backprop protocol. T3–T6 remain open.

## Nix-generated CSS build and Firefox probe

This host's nix-portable can run its `bwrap` runtime; a trivial build and `tools/firefox-chrome/check-nixos-build.nix` both built offline. The latter evaluates the real NixOS module and builds the real wrapper CSS derivation, while replacing `wrapFirefox` with `writeText` to capture generated autoconfig. `tools/firefox-chrome/test-nixos-generated-live.py` reads that autoconfig's CSS store path, copies the generated CSS into a disposable Firefox profile, and checks a configured 7px navbar border. It passed in headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. The generated `userChrome.css` still contains `@import` lines after earlier CSS rules; the current build does not copy their target files to the profile. A follow-up inserted a valid leading import as a positive control and provided sentinel `browser.css` and `config.css` files in the disposable profile. Firefox loaded the leading control but neither late import sentinel; the generated 7px border remained active. This confirms those late imports were ignored in that Firefox run, not full manual/Nix precedence or recovery behavior. A source-marker check also found each manual component import represented in the generated CSS; markers do not prove full contents or equivalent cascade. The probe also confirms generated CSS names the generated store's `icons/book.svg` and that the store file contains SVG data; it does not establish that Firefox can render that path outside nix-portable or after garbage collection. A follow-up also verified the generated `userContent.css` applies a configured monospace font to `about:preferences` in this assembled profile. A precedence fixture set `--tf-border-width` to 7px through the structured option, 9px in `extraConfig`, 11px in `extraUserChrome`, and 13px in `extraUserContent`; Firefox computed 11px for the navbar and 13px for a Nova tile. The same generated CSS checks passed with browser Nova enabled and new-tab Nova both enabled and disabled; preferences were read back. This tests one property and generated concatenation, not every CSS cascade interaction. The actual Firefox wrapper, autoconfig execution, NixOS VM and activation are not tested. Cached nixpkgs source is not proven to be the flake lock. T5 and V7 remain open.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-nixos-generated-live.py /nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source`, substituting an available nixpkgs store path. This invokes nix-portable with `NP_RUNTIME=bwrap`.

## Nix custom-logo escaping

`textfox.config.newtabLogo` previously escaped backslashes and newlines but not double quotes, and emitted `\A` without a terminator. A quoted logo therefore generated invalid CSS; a following hexadecimal character could also be consumed as part of the CSS escape. The Nix option now escapes quotes, carriage returns, and backslashes, and terminates newline escapes with a space. `check-nixos-eval.nix` failed on the old generated string and passes with quotes, backslash and a newline followed by `1`. A subsequent carriage-return fixture exposed another unescaped CSS string terminator; the evaluation check failed before that fix and Firefox now reports the expected escaped carriage return. `test-nix-logo-live.py` evaluates that Nix configuration, copies its generated CSS into a disposable profile, and checks the computed pseudo-element content in Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. Firefox serializes newlines as CSS `\a ` escapes; the initial test incorrectly expected JSON newline serialization and was corrected. This validates generated CSS in a manually assembled profile, not home-manager or NixOS activation. It requires the cached nixpkgs path noted below and probe-only feeds may make network requests. Existing V4 covers configuration; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. T6 remains open.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-nix-logo-live.py /nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source`, substituting an available nixpkgs store path.

## Home Manager evaluation and assembled profile

Home Manager source revision `095fef2f265c3c0da4fe315efba8f8a04495ab34` was fetched into `/nix/store/5d6k9kgxfpazzb88scxx3sgcp9arq51h-source` for a disposable evaluation against the cached Firefox 159 nixpkgs source. `check-home-manager.nix` evaluates the actual textfox and Home Manager modules with one profile, but substitutes the checkout for the flake's built textfox package. `test-home-manager-live.py` materializes the resulting Firefox files in a temporary profile without running Home Manager activation. Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` reported browser Nova disabled, a configured 7px navbar border and configured monospace `about:preferences` font. The first attempt to evaluate the actual package derivation tried to build missing toolchain dependencies and failed downloading bash despite offline mode; the checkout substitution avoids that build and is not proof of actual delivery. The copied Nix store directory was read-only, so the fixture explicitly made its temporary copy writable before writing generated config. No existing home or profile was changed. A subsequent attempt to build the full Home Manager activation package with the cached nixpkgs failed because the closure was uncached and a fixed-output ACL source download timed out; no activation ran. Home Manager activation, package builds, upgrades, file ownership and migration remain unverified; T1, T5 and T6 remain open.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-home-manager-live.py /nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source /nix/store/5d6k9kgxfpazzb88scxx3sgcp9arq51h-source`, substituting available sources.

## Offline NixOS module evaluation

`tools/firefox-chrome/check-nixos-eval.nix` evaluates the actual NixOS module against a supplied nixpkgs source, checks a non-default border reaches `configCss`, and captures the wrapper's generated autoconfig string to check that Nova remains disabled and the existing-directory refusal is present. With locally cached nixpkgs source `/nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source`, rootless nix-portable evaluation passed and reported `firefox-unwrapped` version `159.0`. This source is not established as the flake's locked input or any user's package; the wrapper substitutes a fake CSS derivation path and a `writeText` package to make evaluation bounded. No package, Firefox build ID, generated CSS output, real autoconfig execution or NixOS activation was verified. The first expression used an unavailable `lib.nixosSystem`; importing nixpkgs's NixOS evaluator worked. `nixfmt --check` initially found formatting differences in the new test; formatting and recheck passed. No new spec invariant is needed: V7 already distinguishes evaluation from real activation. T1 and T5 remain open.

Run `nix eval --offline --impure --json --expr 'import ./tools/firefox-chrome/check-nixos-eval.nix { nixpkgsPath = /nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source; }'` where that cached path exists.

`tools/firefox-chrome/test-nova-no-search-live.py` verifies effective preferences and initializes Nova new-tab with `showSearch=false` in disposable vanilla and textfox profiles. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, both browser Nova states rendered the centered-logo layout and shortcuts with nonzero geometry and no search wrapper. A follow-up made the generated logo transparent and compared screenshots. In the textfox profiles, the intervention changed 3052 pixels across the screenshot but none inside the first shortcut rectangle; control screenshots had zero changes there. This tests one shortcut and one viewport, not general logo/shortcut separation, interaction, clipping or accessibility. The probe requires ImageMagick. Feeds and widgets are probe-only overrides and may make network requests. T3 and T4 remain open.

## Split-view keyboard resize

`tools/firefox-chrome/test-split-view-live.py` creates a split view in disposable profiles, verifies a visible focusable splitter with separator role and `aria-controls` pointing to the first panel, and sends Arrow Left. In headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the first panel shrank and `aria-valuenow` changed in vanilla and textfox profiles with both browser Nova states. The test programmatically focuses the splitter; it does not verify tab order, painted focus, pointer resizing, forced colors or delivery paths. A follow-up covered Firefox's bidi pseudo-locale in all four configurations. The initial test expected Arrow Left to shrink the first panel in RTL and failed even in vanilla; Arrow Right is the shrink direction in that fixture. With direction-specific arrows, all eight Nova/theme/direction combinations passed, including a nonzero computed focus outline after keyboard input. Repeating with `ui.useAccessibilityTheme=1` and verified `(prefers-contrast)` passed the same checks for 16 total cases. This does not prove the outline is painted or sufficiently contrasted. The first run showed textfox panel bounding width exceeding `aria-valuenow` by about 4 CSS pixels. Firefox release `toolkit/content/widgets/tabbox.js` derives that value from `clientWidth`, not bounding width. A follow-up measured 2px borders on both sides of the themed panel and confirmed `aria-valuenow` equaled `clientWidth` after resizing in all four cases. This explains the measured difference; it does not establish complete accessibility. A follow-up opened a findbar in each split panel through Firefox's API and reversed the split view. Both findbars retained nonzero visible geometry before and after in all four cases, the two panel bounding boxes did not overlap after reversal, and exactly one split footer had visible nonzero geometry; this does not test findbar search, painted overflow or keyboard traversal inside split view. T2 and T4 remain open.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-split-view-live.py`.

## Horizontal tab token intervention

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-tab-colors.py` tests two temporary static themes and a matching configured `--tf-bg` in disposable profiles. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, selected horizontal-tab backgrounds computed transparent in both Nova states, while `--toolbar-field-color` and `--toolbar-bgcolor` were empty. Firefox's `--tab-background-color-selected` was present as a 40%-alpha white color in these profiles. Setting textfox's selected-background variable on the selected background element made that fill compute, but did not change the theme-provided text color. A root inline override failed to beat textfox's user-origin `!important`; the element-level intervention was used to test the token. A discarded screenshot-difference attempt to infer text contrast had widespread changes between control captures and did not isolate glyph pixels, so it provided no contrast evidence. The result is not a safe fix: the translucent fill's painted backdrop and foreground contrast are unverified, as are hover, forced colors, other themes and delivery paths. A temporary candidate outside the checkout replaced the missing inputs with `currentColor` and `--tf-bg`. In the same two matched light/dark themes and both Nova states, selected and pseudo-hovered horizontal tabs computed nontransparent, distinct fills while text stayed black or white respectively. Contrast ratios calculated from those opaque computed foreground/background values exceeded 10:1 in the sampled profiles; this is not a painted-pixel measurement. With `ui.useAccessibilityTheme=1`, `(prefers-contrast)` matched and these computed values remained the same in the four sampled cases; `(forced-colors: active)` remained false in Linux chrome. A deliberate mismatch between theme text and custom `--tf-bg` produced computed selected and hover contrast ratios below 1.5:1 in both Nova states. This does not prove the candidate alone creates that mismatch, but confirms it cannot guarantee readable combinations. Default themes, painted contrast, pointer hover, Windows forced-colors chrome and other modes remain unverified, so no CSS change was retained. The first attempt used a nonexistent Firefox API for hover; `InspectorUtils.addPseudoClassLock` worked. T2 and T4 remain in progress.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-tab-colors.py`.

On headless Linux Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, accepting `user.js` set browser Nova to false as a user preference; declining left it at its true default. New-tab Nova remained true in both profiles. Declining used a pre-existing `user.js` that enabled custom stylesheets, since the installer does not enable them when `user.js` is skipped. A follow-up checked `about:preferences` computed `font-family: monospace` from the installed custom config in both profiles. This is a limited fresh-profile manual-install baseline, not an update, migration, accessibility, visual or full delivery test. Search suggestions may make network requests. T1 remains in progress.

## Themed sidebar launcher selector

Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` exposes the launcher as `sidebar-main` without an ID, so the old `#sidebar-main` rule never matched. `test-sidebar-theme-live.py` installs a temporary static theme only inside each disposable profile and checks the launcher with non-default `--tf-bg`. The original selector failed the dark-theme baseline because its host background remained transparent. Replacing it with `sidebar-main` made the host background follow configured dark and light colors in both browser Nova states; computed launcher foreground matched the corresponding white or black theme text. A follow-up enabled Firefox's expand-on-hover mode; the root state was active and computed launcher-container foreground matched the test theme in all four cases. Nova computed a themed background image, while classic computed none. A Marionette pointer move from the collapsed launcher triggered expansion in all four cases. This does not prove painted output, keyboard access or real desktop pointer behavior. The temporary themes deliberately pair backgrounds and text; this does not guarantee contrast for arbitrary user-specified combinations, forced colors, or other delivery paths. Built-in dark theme did not set `lwtheme` in this Firefox baseline, so the test uses a temporary extension theme that does. Existing V4 and V5 cover this class; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. T2 and T4 remain open.

`tools/firefox-chrome/test-sidebar-tools-live.py` checks `--tf-display-sidebar-tools` with vertical tabs and sidebar revamp enabled. ## Nova logo painted-pixel probe

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-nova-logo-paint.py` captures two control screenshots, then makes only the generated logo text transparent and captures another. It reports ImageMagick pixel differences inside `.search-inner-wrapper`; a zero full-image difference means the intervention did not visibly affect pixels and cannot establish a negative. Search geometry must stay unchanged. On headless Linux Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, a 1280px requested window (1244px content viewport) had identical control crops, but changed search-inner pixels with the logo made transparent, for default 14px font, 18px font, and bidi pseudo-locale. This is evidence that the logo affects painted search-region pixels in those profiles, not proof of overlap for all layouts or of actual text legibility. At a 700px requested window the wordmark computed `display: none`; intervention changed no pixels, so that case is inconclusive. Network and dynamic widgets may make control screenshots differ. The probe requires ImageMagick and enables disposable new-tab feeds that may make network requests. No layout fix was retained; responsive, real RTL locale, interaction, focus, and clipping checks remain open.

A probe-only `--experiment` changes the grid's first row to `auto`, confines the start sidebar to that row, and lets the wordmark height grow. At the same 1280px requested width, it eliminated detected search-region pixel differences in LTR, bidi pseudo-locale, and 18px-font runs; control crops were unchanged. It also shifted search down and changed grid geometry. At requested widths 700px and 1000px the wordmark was hidden, yet the intervention still changed grid geometry; at 1600px the visible-logo search crop showed no intervention difference. A follow-up also detected no logo-caused pixel differences inside the opposite sidebar crop at 1280px for 14px, 18px and bidi cases, with unchanged controls. In another headless run, clicking the new-tab search handoff focused the browser URL bar with the experimental CSS at 700px and 1280px, including 18px font and bidi at 1280px. A further `--long-logo --font-size 18` intervention used a 100-character custom `--tf-newtab-logo`; pixels changed in the opposite sidebar crop in both LTR and bidi runs while control crops were unchanged. At the 1244px content viewport, a repeat measured 630 changed pixels in the opposite sidebar with or without the grid experiment in LTR, versus zero control changes; bidi with the experiment measured 618 changed pixels. These are pixel differences caused by making the logo transparent, not element hit-testing or proof that a particular widget is obscured. The candidate does not resolve this existing custom-logo overflow and is not safe to ship as-is. These are bounded observations, not proof of widget layout or keyboard focus. A second probe-only `--experiment-wrap` uses a `minmax()` first row and wraps the wordmark within a container-relative width, retaining the configured font. At 1280px requested width, default 14px LTR and long 18px bidi samples had zero changed pixels in search and opposite-sidebar crops with stable controls; clicking search still focused the URL bar. At 700px and 1000px, search and opposite-sidebar geometry matched baseline, but start-sidebar height changed from 136.25px to 62.5px even with the wordmark hidden. Empty-logo controls confirmed the start-sidebar height also changes at 700px and 1280px even though no logo pixels change; search and opposite-sidebar rectangles stayed equal in those samples. In the sampled 700px and 1280px profiles the start sidebar had only its logo heading; the 1280px sample had one visible opposite-sidebar control. This does not cover layouts with navigation or other sidebar children. Additional 1280px runs forced either reduced motion or content forced-colors through disposable Firefox preferences and verified the corresponding media query; default LTR and long-logo 18px bidi cases showed zero changed search and opposite-sidebar pixels with stable controls, and search click focused the URL bar. This does not establish visual contrast or real OS accessibility-theme behavior. With a synthetic tall page fixture and a 900px content viewport, scrolling 200px left search sticky at the same top position as baseline and its click focused the URL bar for default LTR and long-logo 18px bidi cases. This does not exercise real feed behavior or sticky transitions. A separate `--experiment-column` widened the start grid track instead of changing row placement. It eliminated the sampled default LTR search-pixel difference, but with a long 18px logo in bidi mode the search inner wrapper extended outside the viewport; the probe refused an invalid crop. Do not ship that candidate. A `--centered-logo` fixture disables stories, highlights and widgets and verifies Firefox's `logo-in-content` class. At 1280px requested width the row experiment also changed the centered logo's wordmark height and moved search; default LTR and long-logo 18px bidi runs showed no changed search or opposite-sidebar pixels and search click focused the URL bar. This is one centered configuration, not proof for other configurations. The wrapping/row experiment remains unverified for other start-sidebar contents, custom-logo clipping, painted focus, actual RTL locales and other configurations. A prior intervention changing the sidebar row and wordmark height without changing the grid's fixed row size left the paint difference unchanged.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-nova-logo-paint.py --width 1280` with optional `--rtl`, `--font-size 18`, `--long-logo` or `--empty-logo`, `--centered-logo`, `--reduced-motion`, `--forced-colors`, `--experiment`, `--experiment-wrap`, or `--experiment-column`. `--height 1000 --scroll 200` appends a disposable tall fixture before scrolling.

## Sidebar resize control

Firefox release `browser/themes/shared/sidebar.css` gives `#sidebar-splitter` focus and visible focus styling. Textfox hid it unconditionally, preventing sidebar resizing through that control. In disposable headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, `test-sidebar-live.py` failed before removing that rule because the open bookmarks sidebar's splitter had `display: none`. After removal, the splitter was visible and focusable in both browser Nova states; Arrow Right increased sidebar width and Arrow Left decreased it. The test also checks LTR and Firefox's bidi pseudo-locale in both states, including a nonzero computed focus outline after keyboard input. With the disposable `ui.useAccessibilityTheme` preference set, `(prefers-contrast)` matched and keyboard resizing still worked. The regression now covers both sidebar positions across Nova, bidi and contrast preference states, 16 cases total; it reads back direction and position and chooses the appropriate arrow. One immediate focus-outline check failed once and passed on rerun; the test now waits up to five seconds for the focus state instead of assuming it settles synchronously. The cause of that intermittent observation is not established. An earlier attempt incorrectly expected `(forced-colors: active)` in Linux browser chrome and failed; Firefox documents that chrome and content use different contrast regimes. This is not an OS high-contrast or forced-colors test. The probe does not prove tab order, painted focus indication or contrast, mouse drag, real RTL locales, other platforms, or delivery paths. Existing V5 covers keyboard access and V6 requires real interaction checks. A §B entry should record the root cause under the spec backprop protocol; that spec edit requires approval. T4 remains in progress.

## Browser-chrome probe identity

`firefox-live.sh` now passes its disposable profile path and launched process ID to `ff_inspect.py` for chrome queries. Before inspecting an element, the inspector rejects a mismatched Marionette profile or an exited process. `test-inspector-profile.py` exercises both rejections and a valid profile with real headless Firefox. A listening port can still belong to another Firefox, and establishing a Marionette session itself occurs before the identity check; use disposable instances and avoid sharing the port. The PID liveness check does not prove process ancestry. The launcher now selects an ephemeral port by default, while `--port` and `FF_MARIONETTE_PORT` still override it. A real-Firefox concurrent-inspector regression failed with the former fixed port and passed with ephemeral ports. The port is released before Firefox binds it, so a bind race remains; the identity check limits misleading results but cannot reserve the port. T1 remains in progress because delivery-path builds and baselines are not verified.

## App-menu color intervention

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-panel-colors.py` reproduces the configured app-menu background discrepancy in disposable profiles and temporarily sets Firefox's current `--panel-background-color` on the open panel. On headless Linux Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, both browser Nova states changed from white background with black text to the configured dark blue background while retaining black text. This demonstrates that setting the background alone risks unreadable text; no CSS fix was retained. The probe reports computed slot styles, not painted pixels or menu item contrast, and does not exercise forced colors, other operating systems, or delivery paths. Firefox release `toolkit/themes/shared/popup.css` uses `--panel-background-color` and `--panel-text-color` for the content part. Further work must establish a safe foreground pairing and accessibility behavior before changing the theme. Existing V4 and V5 already cover configuration and contrast; no new invariant is needed for this observation. T2 and T4 remain in progress.

A follow-up probe read an empty root `--lwt-text-color` and `currentColor` for `--toolbar-color` in this profile, so neither observation establishes a usable contrasting foreground for arbitrary `--tf-bg` overrides. Setting `browser.display.document_color_use` to `2` did not activate `(forced-colors: active)` in the browser chrome on this headless Linux baseline. An initial check expecting it to do so failed; Firefox's accessibility documentation distinguishes content high-contrast settings from chrome styling. This is not a forced-colors test. V5 and V6 already require accessibility and effective-state verification, so no new invariant is needed.

Run with `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-panel-colors.py`. It checks both browser Nova states and two document color preference values; it makes a temporary runtime override, not a persistent theme change.
