```sh
firefox --version
nix --version
sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-firefox-live.sh
sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-newtab-live.sh
MOZ_HEADLESS=1 sh tools/firefox-chrome/firefox-live.sh --vanilla --nova on --newtab
MOZ_HEADLESS=1 sh tools/firefox-chrome/firefox-live.sh --nova on --newtab
MOZ_HEADLESS=1 sh tools/firefox-chrome/firefox-live.sh --nova on nav-bar -- --computed border,background-color
git status --short
```

The untracked `.pi/` directory was already present during this work and has not been modified intentionally. Do not remove it as part of this port.

## NixOS interrupted-install mock checks

`node tools/firefox-chrome/test-nixos-update.js` exercises the extracted autoconfig branch with a stateful in-memory profile. Injected failures before directory creation, each CSS copy, and marker creation leave the expected completed operations; no failure triggers a restart. A retry after directory creation is refused and preserves the partial files, while a failure before directory creation can retry the fresh install. Existing user files and an old store marker are preserved by the refusal. A negative control with the refusal guard removed failed the retry assertion. These mocks do not simulate partial writes within an operation, Firefox file APIs, actual autoconfig execution, or NixOS activation. Refusing a partial install is containment, not automatic recovery. T5 and V2 remain open.

## App-menu foreground pairing

On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, `probe-panel-colors.py` confirmed that overriding the app-menu content slot background with `--tf-bg` left its black foreground unchanged in both browser Nova states and with document color preferences 0 and 2. Chrome forced colors remained inactive. A separate temporary probe at `/tmp/tf-panel-matrix.py` tested matching light and dark static themes and a deliberately mismatched dark theme with a light configured background, in both Nova states. After the background-only override, the mismatched fixture computed rgb(232, 240, 248) background with rgb(251, 251, 254) foreground, approximately 1.11:1 contrast. This is a negative control for the proposed intervention, not evidence that the existing baseline has that defect. Computed slot colors do not establish painted text contrast or child menu-item colors. The temporary probe may disappear; other panels, keyboard interaction, actual forced colors, platforms and delivery paths remain unverified. No CSS change was made. T2 and T4 remain open.

## Disposable NixOS VM boot

A minimal NixOS VM built from cached nixpkgs source `/nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source` and reached its serial login prompt under QEMU using nix-portable with the bwrap runtime. The first build failed in the NixOS HTML manual derivation because `cp` could not set permissions on `highlightjs`; disabling `documentation.nixos.enable` for this disposable VM allowed the build to finish. The boot was stopped by a 120-second timeout after the login prompt, producing QEMU shutdown errors; no guest login or graceful shutdown was tested. No Firefox or textfox module was included in this first VM, so this is not a delivery or activation test. The VM output is `/nix/store/d4vnhsa7y8m0w4y0i3nij45yacdv9psv-nixos-vm`, and the disk and boot log are under `/tmp/textfox-nixos-vm.E7ztKR`; both may be removed by garbage collection or temporary-file cleanup. The `~/.local/bin/nix` wrapper forces proot even when `NP_RUNTIME` is set; this boot used `NP_RUNTIME=bwrap $HOME/.local/bin/nix-portable nix shell --offline <VM output> --command run-nixos-vm` directly. T1 and T5 remain open.

A second VM used the actual `nix/modules/nixos.nix` module and the real `wrapTextfox` package with the same cached nixpkgs source. Its build fetched Firefox 159.0 and completed, and its serial console reached the NixOS login prompt. The guest was stopped by timeout after boot; Firefox was not launched, so neither autoconfig execution nor effective preferences or profile behavior were verified. The VM output is `/nix/store/6nxbmv4x9dkgnj0k0zggx4sb8ra5bw45-nixos-vm`; its disposable disk and boot log are in `/tmp/textfox-nixos-theme-vm.9W9gjm`. These paths are subject to garbage collection and temporary-file cleanup.

## NixOS VM serial diagnosis

A disposable VM with a root serial autologin and a separate unprivileged Firefox service allowed guest inspection. After more than one minute, `systemctl status` showed the wrapped Firefox 159.0 process running; its child command line included build ID `20261022150525`. `ss -lnt` showed no listening TCP sockets, and the requested fresh guest profile had no `chrome/` directory. Thus the host Marionette timeout is not explained solely by a host port-forwarding rule. The service log contained headless and graphics messages but no established cause for incomplete startup; no effective preferences or autoconfig execution were verified. The diagnostic VM definition and serial log are temporary at `/tmp/textfox-vm-live.nix` and `/tmp/textfox-vm-console.I2FBPb/boot.log`. Autologin was used only in this isolated diagnostic guest. The QEMU timeout terminates the guest ungracefully. T1 remains open.

## NixOS autoconfig import investigation

In a disposable NixOS VM, the same Firefox 159.0 package started without textfox and opened Marionette on port 2829, while the textfox-wrapped process remained running without a listening port or `chrome/` directory. The Firefox 159 archive contains `modules/FileUtils.sys.mjs`; `wrapTextfox.nix` imports the older `FileUtils.jsm`. A temporary copy of the wrapper changed only that import to `ChromeUtils.importESModule("resource://gre/modules/FileUtils.sys.mjs")`. In a new guest profile, that candidate created both CSS files and the store marker, then exited as expected for the autoconfig restart. The test service originally stopped on that successful exit; changing only the temporary service to restart on exit led the candidate to reopen Firefox and listen on port 2828. This supports the outdated import as the startup blocker in this fixture, but no effective preference or rendered style was verified and the production wrapper remains unchanged. The VM definition and logs under `/tmp/textfox-vm-candidate*` may disappear. Existing V2 and V7 cover this failure; a backprop record and a durable real-autoconfig regression are still needed before a fix is complete.

## NixOS autoconfig import fix

The NixOS wrapper now imports `FileUtils.sys.mjs` using `ChromeUtils.importESModule`. `tools/firefox-chrome/test-nixos-autoconfig-vm.sh` builds a disposable NixOS VM with the real textfox module and starts wrapped Firefox in a fresh guest profile. It checks both generated CSS files, the store marker and a listening Marionette socket after the initial restart. With cached Firefox 159 nixpkgs, the fixed wrapper passed. A separate copy with the old import recorded guest FAIL; QEMU timed out during its shutdown, so its process exit was not a clean shutdown. The test service deliberately restarts Firefox after its initial successful exit. It does not read effective preferences, validate painted CSS, or prove safe upgrades and recovery. The test uses nix-portable bwrap and may download missing build inputs; VM artifacts remain under the temporary path it prints. Existing profiles are not touched. T1 and T5 remain open.

## NixOS VM effective preference baseline

A disposable NixOS VM built with the actual textfox module and the fixed wrapper ran a guest-local Marionette probe against `/home/probe/profile`. Firefox reported version 159.0 build `20261022150525`. `browser.nova.enabled` reported effective false, default true, and a user value; `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.nova.enabled` reported effective true, default true, and no user value. Marionette automation may modify preferences, so the source of the browser Nova user value was not established. The guest probe read both values directly from Firefox after the autoconfig restart; it did not verify visual behavior or non-automation defaults. A host-forwarded Marionette attempt timed out despite the guest VM passing its socket check, so the successful probe ran inside the guest and wrote its result through the VM shared directory. The temporary probe files under `/tmp/textfox-guest-prefs.py` and `/tmp/textfox-vm-guest-prefs.nix` may disappear. NixOS activation was exercised in this VM, but Home Manager delivery and other T1 checks remain open.

## Home Manager activation in NixOS VM

A disposable NixOS VM imported Home Manager source `/nix/store/5d6k9kgxfpazzb88scxx3sgcp9arq51h-source`, the actual textfox Home Manager module, and the real textfox package derivation against cached nixpkgs `/nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source`. The Firefox service required and followed `home-manager-probe.service`, so it started only after that service succeeded. A guest-local Marionette probe connected to `/home/probe/.config/mozilla/firefox/test`, reporting Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, a configured 7px navbar border, browser Nova effective false/default true/user value present, and new-tab Nova effective true/default true/no user value. The profile and activation were confined to the guest. Automation may affect preference state; this test does not establish preference migration, existing-profile safety, full option coverage, or visual accessibility. The guest was stopped after collecting results. The VM expression and probe are temporary at `/tmp/textfox-hm-vm.nix` and `/tmp/textfox-hm-prefs.py`; results are under `/tmp/textfox-hm-vm.E9PAAV` and may disappear. This is a NixOS-hosted Home Manager integration baseline, not every standalone Home Manager environment. T1 remains open.

The Home Manager activation baseline is now repeatable with `sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-home-manager-vm.sh /nix/store/771lrblshx04rd5hgbg69pxkfzm9ncqh-source /nix/store/5d6k9kgxfpazzb88scxx3sgcp9arq51h-source`, substituting available nixpkgs and Home Manager sources. It builds a disposable VM, requires successful Home Manager activation before starting Firefox, and checks the actual profile, browser Nova state and configured 7px navbar border through guest-local Marionette. The VM test passed. A separate temporary fixture configured 3px instead; Firefox computed 3px and the test reported FAIL. The check does not assert a particular Firefox build or new-tab preference default, nor does it test visual layout or upgrades.

## Repeatable NixOS and Home Manager preference baselines

Both VM tests now run `probe-vm-preferences.py` inside the guest and report Firefox version, build, both Nova effective values, default-branch values and user-value presence. The NixOS test also checks copied CSS files and marker after autoconfig restart; the Home Manager test checks activation and a configured 7px navbar border. On cached Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, both tests passed and reported browser Nova false with default true, and new-tab Nova true with default true. Both report a browser Nova user value; automation may influence that state. The manual fresh-profile test and vanilla/textfox browser and new-tab preference probes also passed on the same host build. These checks do not verify new-tab visual behavior in either VM, actual upgrade behavior, or arbitrary package overrides; the cached nixpkgs source is not established as the flake lock. T1 remains open.

## Flake input mismatch at T1 verification

With nix-portable bwrap and `--no-write-lock-file`, `nix flake metadata` resolved the `flake.nix` channel tarball to revision `1d060086d60cb76a40cf37109f979d88a5bd6396`; `nix eval` of that resolved input reported `firefox-unwrapped.version` as `159.0.1`. Nix warned that it would replace the existing GitHub-based locked input `0726a0ecb6d4e08f6adced58726b95db924cef57`. The lock file was not changed. The VM tests above used a different cached source reporting 159.0 and therefore do not establish a baseline for this newly resolved channel input. Manual, Home Manager, and NixOS users may also select their own Firefox packages. T1 remains open until the intended input and relevant actual builds are resolved and tested.

The NixOS autoconfig and Home Manager activation VM tests also passed against the newly resolved channel source `/nix/store/wa9kz0gcy4wgakdv28p7i4jc7xkcbbky-source` at revision `1d060086d60cb76a40cf37109f979d88a5bd6396`. Both reported Firefox 159.0.1 build `20261102144711`, browser Nova effective false/default true, and new-tab Nova effective true/default true; Home Manager applied the configured 7px navbar border. Both reported a browser Nova user value under automation. These are actual VM Firefox results for that resolved source, not a persisted lock change, full new-tab visual coverage, or proof for user-supplied package overrides.

## Locked GitHub input baseline

The exact GitHub revision in `flake.lock` was fetched with its recorded NAR hash as `/nix/store/75bkaivfbwq3x8cs7155hag7hs1chjcx-source` and evaluated to Firefox 150.0. Both real delivery VM probes launched Firefox 150.0 build `20260415192539`, but failed their Nova default checks: `browser.nova.enabled` had effective false and a user value but no boolean default on the default branch. The new-tab Nova preference reported effective and default false. Home Manager activation succeeded and its configured navbar border computed 7px. This does not establish Firefox 150 as a supported Nova target; textfox targets the latest release. The lock file was not changed. The VM probes now record missing preferences as null and retain an error file instead of losing all observations to a JavaScript exception. Both VM probes still pass with resolved-channel Firefox 159.0.1. The input mismatch remains unresolved, and T1 remains open.

## Flake lock aligned with channel

With operator approval, `flake.lock` now records the channel tarball declared by `flake.nix` at revision `1d060086d60cb76a40cf37109f979d88a5bd6396`. Offline flake evaluation with `--no-update-lock-file` returned that revision, source `/nix/store/wa9kz0gcy4wgakdv28p7i4jc7xkcbbky-source`, and Firefox 159.0.1. Both NixOS and Home Manager VM checks passed again against that exact source and reported build `20261102144711`; effective and default Nova values matched the earlier channel tests. Home Manager itself remains an externally supplied source, and package overrides and other platforms can select different Firefox builds. This aligns the flake input; it does not establish visual Nova support or update safety.

## Paired vanilla and textfox NixOS VM baseline

The NixOS VM regression now runs a second unthemed Firefox profile alongside the wrapped textfox profile and reads both using guest-local Marionette. Against locked Firefox 159.0.1 build `20261102144711`, the test passed: vanilla reported both Nova effective/default values true and a 1px navbar border; textfox reported browser Nova effective false/default true, new-tab Nova effective/default true, and a 2px navbar border. The vanilla profile had no user values for either Nova preference; the textfox profile reported a browser Nova user value under automation. This is a computed-style and preference baseline, not proof of painted appearance or full new-tab initialization. The Home Manager activation VM test also passed after the shared probe gained profile and port arguments.

## T2 inventory baseline

T2 completion requires accounting for every generated rule record and non-`--tf-*` custom-property identifier against the Firefox 159.0 release source and relevant live DOM states, grouped by component. Record source-only evidence separately from live matches, computed style, interaction, and painted evidence. Keep untested conditional, platform-specific, shadow-DOM, and state-dependent rules explicit; an empty source search or a missing default-state DOM match does not prove removal. Use the moving latest-release tree to flag potential drift, but identify its revision separately from the tested build. Unavailable-platform and painted accessibility checks can remain explicit T8 gates; unresolved local source or live inventory keeps T2 open. The generated syntax list is a starting point, not a completed assessment.

The manual entry points import 14 tracked CSS files, including the entry points themselves. Component rules also occur in `icons.css` and `overwrites.css`, so a component inventory cannot rely on its namesake stylesheet alone. `content/about.css` targets every `about:` URL rather than an enumerated list; five pages have bounded live evidence in the checkpoint below. No project document was found that declares a complete supported-page list. Treat untested pages and shadow controls as unresolved rather than silently excluding them.

`tools/firefox-chrome/inventory-css.py` enumerates tracked CSS rule preludes, nesting contexts, and custom-property identifiers with `tinycss2` 1.4.0. The current checkout yields 377 rule records across 14 files. Nested selectors are kept as written, not expanded; property identifiers include definitions and references rather than only Firefox-owned variables. The output is a source inventory, not evidence that a selector matches or a property is used at runtime. A fixture checks nested and conditional rules and ignores identifiers in comments and strings. To reproduce without changing the Firefox probe environment, install `tinycss2==1.4.0` into a temporary target, then run `PYTHONPATH=<target> python3 tools/firefox-chrome/test-inventory-css.py` and `PYTHONPATH=<target> python3 tools/firefox-chrome/inventory-css.py > inventory.json`. Generated output remains unreviewed until compared with source and live states.

The local Firefox reports 159.0, with previously recorded build `20261022150525`. The GitHub Firefox mirror resolved `FIREFOX_159_0_RELEASE` to `9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402`; the moving `release` branch resolved to `7b58e9811f74cf2d098fe7b7d8f05c6e2b1a2406`. Searchfox queries below used the moving `firefox-release` tree. The branch lookup does not prove Searchfox served that revision or that the tag corresponds to the installed build. Subsequent inventories must identify the source revision or explicitly label moving-source evidence.

Firefox 159.0 tag `9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402` defines `#TabsToolbar`, `.toolbar-items`, `#tabbrowser-tabs`, and `#pinned-tabs-container` in [`navigator-toolbox.inc.xhtml`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/navigator-toolbox.inc.xhtml). A moving Searchfox CSS-only query for `toolbar-items` returned no matches, despite its presence in that tagged markup. Search scope matters and an empty CSS-only search cannot establish that a DOM selector is absent. This markup confirms those elements exist in source, not that textfox's complete selectors match in every live state.

Initial source searches found `#pinned-tabs-container` in `browser/base/content/navigator-toolbox.inc.xhtml` and no occurrence of `#vertical-pinned-tabs-container`. The same tree defines `#sidebar-tools-and-extensions-splitter` and `.buttons-wrapper` in `browser/components/sidebar/sidebar-main.mjs` and `.css`; the existing live sidebar test exercises both. `#permissions-granted-icon` and `.titlebar-spacer` appear in `navigator-toolbox.inc.xhtml`. Searches found no `--urlbar-toolbar-height` occurrence, while `--urlbar-container-height` occurs in `browser/components/urlbar/content/SmartbarInput.mjs`. Absence in a search is not proof that a rule has no runtime effect. The pinned-tab multi-item state, toolbar variable behavior, other selectors, and matched Firefox 159 source remain unresolved.

The component-by-component ledger is [`T2-INVENTORY.md`](T2-INVENTORY.md). It records source and live evidence alongside unresolved states; it is incomplete until all generated rule groups and non-`--tf-*` properties are accounted for.

## T2 coverage checkpoint

This index summarizes bounded evidence above, not a complete selector or variable inventory. Live results refer to disposable Linux Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` unless otherwise stated. Searchfox `firefox-release` moves independently of that build.

| Component | Existing evidence | Remaining T2 inventory |
|---|---|---|
| Tabs | `probe-tab-colors.py` found empty inputs to textfox's selected and hover color tokens. The sampled backgrounds were transparent. The vertical strip check is described below. | Verify other tab selectors, vertical and pinned states, and color tokens across themes. |
| Sidebar | `test-sidebar-theme-live.py`, `test-sidebar-tools-live.py`, and `test-sidebar-live.py` cover launcher, tools, and resize samples. Firefox release defines `sidebar-main` and styles `#pinned-tabs-container`; textfox still targets `#vertical-pinned-tabs-container`. The classic header gap is described below. | Inventory remaining sidebar selectors and states. A single pinned tab did not establish a pinned-container defect. |
| Toolbar | `test-firefox-live.sh` checks `#nav-bar` in both browser Nova states. Manual and VM baselines check configured borders. Navigation, bookmarks, and window-control probes are described below. | Inventory remaining buttons, other window-control states and platforms, and Firefox-owned variables in live states. |
| URL bar | `test-urlbar-live.py` checks populated results and a simulated no-results condition. `probe-urlbar-colors.py` checks selected-result tokens and background in sampled profiles. | Verify naturally visible one-offs and remaining selectors, variables, and painted states. |
| Menus and panels | `probe-panel-colors.py` shows app-menu content uses `--panel-background-color`, not the configured `--panel-background`, in sampled profiles. Firefox release `toolkit/themes/shared/popup.css` uses `--panel-background-color` for popup background. | Inspect other panels and menus, including active-item states, shadow parts, foreground pairing, and actual forced colors. |
| Findbar | `test-findbar-live.py` covers keyboard search, no-match feedback, recovery, and computed focus outline in both Nova states, two directions, and two contrast preferences. The no-match color difference is described below. | Inventory remaining selectors and confirm painted focus and contrast. |
| Split view | `test-split-view-live.py` covers keyboard resizing, findbar geometry, and configured active-panel styling. `test-split-fullscreen-live.py` samples window fullscreen styling. A real-dialog clipping gap is described below. | Inventory remaining selectors and variables, DOM fullscreen, painted layout, and focus. |
| Private windows | `test-private-window-live.py` covers URL bar results and one local navigation. `probe-private-indicator.py` found a missing indicator in a horizontal-tab configuration, described below. | Verify a safe indicator fix, other private-window chrome, and other platforms. |
| New-tab | `test-newtab-live.sh` checks independent Nova preference combinations. Widget menu, shortcut hover, search handoff, and logo probes cover bounded interaction and layout samples. Shortcut-menu and search-handoff selector gaps are described below. | Complete selector and variable inventory, logo overflow, responsive layout, and accessibility checks. |
| `about:` pages | `about:preferences` font was checked in manual and assembled Nix profiles. `test-about-font-live.py` samples five page bodies and selected light-DOM control hosts as described below. | Identify supported pages and inspect remaining controls, shadow roots, and computed configuration. |

On this continuation, `test-firefox-live.sh` and `test-findbar-live.py` passed again in headless Firefox. `probe-panel-colors.py` repeated the app-menu background discrepancy in both browser Nova states; forcing its background alone retained black foreground. These observations do not establish visual accessibility. T2 remains open.

## Vertical tab title and hover styles

`test-sidebar-tools-live.py` now samples the collapsed vertical tab strip with a non-default accent and uppercase text transform. In both Nova states on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the tab container and selected tab label computed uppercase, and the strip's generated title computed `"tabs"` with `display: flex`. After locking `:hover`, the strip border and title color reached the configured accent. An immediate border read initially failed while the CSS transition was still in progress; the probe now waits up to five seconds for the computed color and always removes the pseudo-class lock. This tests computed styles, not real pointer hover, keyboard focus, painted title readability, expanded mode, or other platforms. Existing V6 and V8 cover reliable state evidence; no new invariant or product change was made. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-sidebar-tools-live.py`.

## Horizontal tab toolbar hover

`probe-tab-colors.py` now locks `:hover` on `#TabsToolbar` with a non-default accent and waits for its border transition. In both browser Nova states and temporary light/dark themes on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the toolbar border and generated `"tabs"` title computed the configured accent; the title computed `display: flex`. The same assertions passed with the accessibility-theme preference and deliberately mismatched background probe variants. The probe removes the lock, then moves a Marionette pointer over the toolbar and away; the toolbar matched real `:hover` and reached the configured border color in those samples. This does not test a physical pointer, toolbar focus, title painting, contrast, or other platforms. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-tab-colors.py`.

## Tab toolbar selectors and text transform

`probe-tab-colors.py` now checks the selected tab's `<tabs>` ancestor and `#TabsToolbar .toolbar-items` in disposable profiles configured with `--tf-text-transform: uppercase`. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the tab container and selected `.tab-label` computed uppercase and `.toolbar-items` computed a 3px margin for both browser Nova states and temporary light/dark themes. The same assertions passed with the probe's accessibility-theme preference and its deliberately mismatched background option. These are computed styles in horizontal-tab fixtures, not painted uppercase text, vertical-tab coverage, or proof of contrast. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-tab-colors.py`, optionally adding `--high-contrast` or `--mismatch`.

## Firefox View button in tab strip

`probe-tab-colors.py` now places Firefox View in the tab strip through Firefox's customization API in disposable horizontal-tab profiles, clicks it, and verifies `about:firefoxview`, the button's `open` attribute, `aria-pressed="true"`, and nonzero icon geometry. In both Nova states and temporary light/dark themes on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the icon background computed transparent and `--tab-selected-bgcolor` computed empty. Setting that variable only on the icon to a diagnostic color changed the computed background in all four profiles. This verifies the active Firefox View selector's variable response, not painted focus, contrast, keyboard activation, other placements, or safe replacement colors. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-tab-colors.py`.

## Tab-strip adjacency selectors

`probe-tab-colors.py` and `probe-private-indicator.py` now inspect the two `chrome/tabs.css` selectors that require a toolbar button or palette item immediately before `#tabbrowser-tabs`. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the sampled four non-private themed horizontal-tab profiles and six private vanilla/textfox horizontal/vertical profiles had no preceding element sibling and no matching adjacency selector. Both probes temporarily inserted a disposable toolbar button immediately before the tab strip as a positive control; the corresponding normal or private selector matched once, then the button was removed. The root private and `firefoxviewhidden` attributes were checked for the control. This inventories sampled default layouts, not custom toolbar layouts, Firefox View hidden states, painted borders, or other platforms. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-tab-colors.py` and the same command with `probe-private-indicator.py`.

## Toolbar context bookmark-toggle selector

With expanded selector inventory enabled, `probe-extension-icon.py` finds the dynamically created bookmarks-toolbar toggle matching textfox's full toolbar-context selector with visible geometry in all 16 independent browser Nova and icon-preference combinations. The selector's legacy media condition remains false in those samples. This does not establish painted icon visibility or all unified-extension-menu branches.

## Help-view report-site selector

Tagged Firefox's [panel command handler](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/customizableui/content/panelUI.js#L906) uses `appMenu_help_reportBrokenSite`. In four vanilla/textfox and browser-Nova profiles, `probe-app-menu-subviews.py` finds that item in the open Help view with visible geometry, disabled on the non-reportable `about:robots` fixture, and computed `list-style-image: none`. Both IDs targeted by textfox's report-site icon rule are absent in that sampled view. The fixture does not enable telemetry or submit a report; it does not establish behavior for reportable URLs, painting, or a safe fix.

## Unsampled context-menu IDs

The tagged `browser/` and `toolkit/` atom search finds no exact textual occurrence of `context-pocket`, `context-savelinktopocket`, or `context-keywordfield`. In the existing four-profile `probe-context-icon.py` output, none appears among the sampled link, page, or input popup menu item IDs. These are bounded source and DOM absences, not proof the IDs cannot be generated in another state or source tree. No selectors were removed.

## App-menu zoom pseudo-element

In eight themed Firefox profiles, `probe-panel-colors.py` finds the real `#appMenu-zoom-controls` host with nonzero geometry and a computed `::before` content URL for textfox's screenshot SVG; the resource loads. This separately inventories one branch of the grouped pseudo-element rule. Host geometry and a loadable URL do not establish pseudo-element geometry, painting, contrast, the alternate zoom ID, or the other grouped branches.

## URL-bar placeholder pseudo-element

`test-urlbar-live.py` samples an empty URL bar with a visible placeholder in both browser Nova states. With `--tf-text-transform: uppercase` configured, its computed `::placeholder` transform is uppercase; a temporary root-token control changes it to lowercase and is restored. Expanded-selector snapshots intentionally do not query pseudo-elements, so this direct computed-style evidence is separate from their unobserved count. Painted text and other placeholder hosts remain unverified.

## T2 inventory closeout

T2's bounded inventory is complete. All 377 current rule records are routed by component, with 313 structural matches in the supplied snapshot union and 64 separately dispositioned unobserved records; source atom searches and direct controls are recorded without treating absence as removal. All 40 non-`--tf-*` custom-property names are reconciled against textfox syntax and searched in tagged Firefox `browser/` and `toolkit/`. The operator-selected `about:about` index is covered by the eight-profile offline matrix below, including two documented HTTPS redirects that could not load offline. The underlying evidence ledger, selector records, source searches, and about-page report are linked from `T2-INVENTORY.md`.

Completion means inventory and recorded gaps, not a Firefox Nova support claim. T3/T4 still need verified fixes and interaction testing; T6/T7 cover configuration and preference migration; T8 retains painted accessibility, real RTL, other platforms, signed-in/real-extension states, delivery paths, and independent review. Firefox's tag-to-tested-build correspondence remains unproven. Production Nova remains disabled, the README warning remains, and the proposed §B entry was not added.

## Full indexed about-page inventory

The operator selected every entry in Firefox's `about:about` index as T2's about-page scope and authorized changing only T2's status cell when its gate is satisfied. Tagged [aboutAbout.js](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/toolkit/content/aboutAbout.js) derives the index from `AboutPagesUtils.visibleAboutUrls`. `probe-about-index-live.py` navigated all 45 observed entries in eight disposable vanilla/textfox profiles across independent browser and new-tab Nova settings, inside a bubblewrap network namespace. Forty-three internal documents reached ready state `complete` in every profile. `about:credits` and `about:rights` redirected to Mozilla HTTPS URLs and reached offline error pages; remote destination content was not fetched or verified. Every sampled themed document/error-page root computed `monospace`, and selected visible button/input-like hosts and one level of open shadow controls had no different computed font. Zero sampled controls, `readyState=complete`, and a root font do not prove asynchronous initialization or visual accessibility; the two redirect rows describe error documents, not destination fonts. [Per-page results](T2-ABOUT-INDEX.md) retain these limitations. Earlier populated-login and initialized-newtab checks remain separate.

Reproduce with `bwrap --unshare-net --ro-bind / / --bind /tmp /tmp --dev /dev --proc /proc --setenv HOME /tmp/textfox-about-home --setenv MOZ_HEADLESS 1 --setenv DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS unix:path=/nonexistent -- "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-about-index-live.py > /tmp/textfox-t2-about-index-matrix.jsonl`, after creating `/tmp/textfox-about-home`; then `python3 tools/firefox-chrome/summarize-about-index.py /tmp/textfox-t2-about-index-matrix.jsonl > T2-ABOUT-INDEX.md`. The probe refuses a network namespace with non-loopback interfaces; the negative control outside isolation failed as intended.

## Unobserved selector dispositions

`T2-UNOBSERVED-DISPOSITION.md` assigns each of the 64 records absent from the expanded-selector snapshot union to bounded direct evidence, a verified mismatch, or an explicit untested state. It distinguishes pseudo-elements, signed-in account states, real extensions, Floorp, and platform/paint follow-up. `check-unobserved-disposition.py` checks location coverage and duplicates; it does not validate the accuracy or sufficiency of the prose. Run it and `test-unobserved-disposition.py` after regenerating snapshots. This is an accounting aid, not a support claim or proof all selector branches work.

## Source search for unobserved selector atoms

`T2-UNOBSERVED-SOURCE.md` lists ID/class atoms heuristically extracted from the 64 records not observed in expanded-selector snapshots, with textual matches across 23,190 tagged Firefox `browser/` and `toolkit/` files. Counts include unrelated contexts, tests, and comments; attributes, tag names, pseudo-elements, conditions, and complete relationships are not checked. Generated extension IDs need not occur literally in source, and no source hit is not proof of removal. The script rejects stale selector text and incomplete tagged checkouts. Reproduce using `python3 tools/firefox-chrome/selector-atoms-source.py /path/to/firefox /path/to/expanded.json T2-SELECTOR-EVIDENCE.md > T2-UNOBSERVED-SOURCE.md`; run `python3 tools/firefox-chrome/test-selector-atoms-source.py` for matching fixtures.

## Classic extension sidebar icon selectors

`probe-extension-sidebar-icons.py` installs two synthetic sidebar actions using Bitwarden and Video DownloadHelper IDs in four themed headless Firefox profiles, with `sidebar.revamp=false` and both browser Nova and extension-icon preference states. Textfox hides the classic sidebar header, so the probe temporarily reveals it to open the real switcher menu, then restores its inline style. Both extension menu items are visible: the unconditional Video DownloadHelper icon applies; the Bitwarden icon under the inactive legacy query does not, while a modern-query control follows the preference. Opening each action produces a `moz-extension://` icon URL with a profile-specific hostname, and neither fixed-hostname switcher selector matches these fixture profiles. Tagged Firefox's [sidebar controller](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/sidebar/browser-sidebar.js#L1999) constructs switcher IDs from the command. This does not test real extensions, revamp mode, paint, or prove how every existing profile chooses extension hostnames.

## Context-menu child structure

Tagged Firefox 159 [menu widget source](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/toolkit/content/widgets/menu.js#L204) constructs `.menu-icon` and `.menu-text`. `probe-context-icon.py` uses `InspectorUtils.getChildrenForNode` on the real page-menu Save Page item in four Linux Nova/preference profiles: the visible `.menu-text` computes a 21px start margin, while the icon has zero geometry; `.menu-iconic-left` and `.menu-iconic-text` are not present in the sampled light DOM. This is one item and computed geometry, not proof about other menus, painted spacing, or a safe replacement for textfox's old child selectors.

## Extension context selector fixtures

`probe-extension-context-matrix.py` now adds four synthetic extensions whose IDs and link-menu item IDs exercise the uBlock, Simple Translate, Clean URL, and Video DownloadHelper selectors alongside the nested Bitwarden fixture. Across four browser-Nova/icon-preference profiles, all four root items have visible geometry and compute no background image under the inactive legacy query. The original rules, temporarily wrapped in the modern preference query, apply the expected four icon resources when enabled; those resources load with the existing missing-resource negative control. Expanded snapshots match all four full selectors, but their `.menu-iconic-icon` descendants are absent from the sampled light DOM and open shadow roots. A follow-up uses `InspectorUtils.getChildrenForNode` to inspect internal children: each sampled item exposes a loaded 16×16 `.menu-icon`, with computed opacity `1` both before and while the modern-query replacement-icon rules are applied. Tagged [Firefox menu widget source](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/toolkit/content/widgets/menu.js#L204) constructs `.menu-icon`, not `.menu-iconic-icon`. This is a computed native-icon suppression gap for the synthetic fixtures, not proof of painted overlap, real-extension behavior, or a safe fix.

## Page-context navigation selector mismatch

Tagged Firefox 159 [context markup](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-context.inc.xhtml#L22) places Back, Forward, Reload, Stop, and Bookmark under `#context-navigation` on non-macOS builds; textfox requires each to be a direct child of `#contentAreaContextMenu`. `probe-context-icon.py` checks the real page context menu in four Linux Nova/preference profiles. All five have `#context-navigation` as parent and fail the direct-child selector; four have visible geometry and Stop is hidden. A temporary modern-query control using the original direct relationship does not apply its diagnostic icon, while a descendant-selector control does when the preference is enabled. The markup has a separate macOS branch, not tested here. This is independent of the legacy media-query failure; no production fix or §B edit was made.

## Private link context menu

`test-private-window-live.py` now opens a real link context menu in a private window for both browser Nova states and both context-icon preference states. Normal-tab and private-window link commands have visible geometry and computed `background-image: none` in all four themed headless profiles; the legacy media query is false. A temporary modern-query control applies the private icon only when enabled. The probe does not activate the command or establish painted icon visibility. A URL-bar fixture initially retained an autofill suffix after synthetic Ctrl+A; using Firefox's `gURLBar.select()` before typing removed that harness race, and the probe still verifies local private-window navigation. Run with `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` for expanded selector snapshots.

## Protection and identity panel icons

`probe-site-panel-icons.py` serves a loopback page, stores a disposable localStorage value, and opens Firefox's protection and identity panels in vanilla and textfox profiles across both browser Nova states. The fixture explicitly sets `browser.urlbar.trustPanel.featureGate=false`; tagged Firefox 159 [defaults that gate to true](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/app/profile/firefox.js#L515), and its older protections handler returns early when enabled. In themed profiles the fixture also sets `--tf-display-urlbar-icons: flex`. In all four samples, the settings, protection report, and clear-site-data targets were visible inside their respective panels; their three textfox `list-style-image` resources loaded in each themed profile with a missing-resource negative control. Expanded queries match those three icon records. Four additional profiles explicitly enable the trust-panel gate: the real `trustpanel-popup` opens, Clear Cookies and Privacy links have visible geometry, and both compute `list-style-image: none`. The three older IDs are absent from the sampled documents; the newer controls do not match those legacy ID selectors. The themed profiles still have `--tf-display-urlbar-icons: flex`, but the old shield container has zero geometry in both themes with the trust panel enabled. This is an explicitly controlled gate, not proof of effective defaults for every delivery path. The probe does not activate any target, establish painted appearance, or infer production support. Initial attempts that clicked the legacy shield with the trust-panel gate at its default did not open the old popup; forcing the gate off was a diagnostic fixture, not a production change.

`probe-bookmarks-menu-icons.py` now records expanded icon-selector snapshots while the bookmarks menu and its toolbar submenu are open in four Nova/preference profiles. Seven bookmarks-popup records match the sampled popup DOM; the eighth conditional record matches only after the temporary `collapsed="true"` attribute intervention, which is restored. The mobile-bookmarks item is present but has no visible geometry in those samples. A separate two-bookmark toolbar-folder fixture now exposes the open-in-tabs selector with visible geometry; its computed background is `none`, and activation opens both local pages in all eight sampled profiles. This is structural evidence, not proof of enabled legacy media queries or painted icons.

The extension-toolbar and nested extension-context matrix probes and the bookmarks-toolbar test now accept `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY`. In four Nova/preference profiles the 11 synthetic extension toolbar targets all matched with visible geometry; the Bitwarden-style root and submenu children matched in four profiles, while the nested `.menu-iconic-icon` did not match sampled light DOM. Eight themed bookmark-toolbar profiles add expanded queries for bookmark and folder Places menus, Other Bookmarks, the folder icon, and the overflow popup. A matching selector may still be hidden or under an inactive preference query; these synthetic add-ons are not the real extensions. With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` set, `probe-download-icons.py` queries every expanded icon rule while the completed local download's context menu is open. All 11 downloads-context records structurally matched in four Nova/preference profiles; six records had a visible match.
