## Unified-extension menu states

Tagged Firefox 159's [unified-extension test helper](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/extensions/test/browser/head_unified_extensions.js) opens the panel item's menu button; its [context-menu tests](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/extensions/test/browser/browser_unified_extensions_context_menu.js) exercise the move commands. `probe-unified-extension-menu.py` installs two no-code temporary add-ons and uses the real panel and menu in both browser Nova states and both context-icon preference states. In all four headless samples, Manage, Remove, Report, and Move Down had visible geometry and computed `background-image: none`; Move Up was hidden. Moving the first fixture down changed Firefox's add-ons-area widget order; after explicitly closing and reopening the popup, Move Up was visible and also computed `none`. Moving it up restored the original order. A temporary modern-preference-query Manage-icon control followed the preference; the legacy query was false in each sample. The explicit popup close avoids assuming headless synthetic-click dismissal. This does not test extension removal, real add-ons, painted icons, or all menu states. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-unified-extension-menu.py`.

## Per-record selector snapshot reconciliation

`T2-SELECTOR-EVIDENCE.md` enumerates all 377 expanded rule records and their matching labeled JSON paths across 30 existing output sets. The current union has 313 structural matches and 64 not observed in those snapshots; it includes synthetic controls and lazy DOM, and does not determine whether media conditions are active, whether grouped branches individually match, or whether any result paints. The count excludes separately documented direct-selector controls and pseudo-element measurements. `T2-INVENTORY.md` maps labels to probe scripts. `selector-evidence.py` rejects stale selector text and observation inputs containing no selector records; `test-selector-evidence.py` tests those guards. The source JSONL files were temporary and may disappear; regenerate by running the documented probes with `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` and the other listed options before rebuilding the report. With the named outputs present, run `sh tools/firefox-chrome/reconcile-selector-evidence.sh /tmp > T2-SELECTOR-EVIDENCE.md`. This per-record gap list does not by itself finish T2.

## Pinned Firefox variable source search

`T2-FIREFOX-VARIABLES.md` records a reproducible exact-name search for all 40 non-`--tf-*` property names in 23,190 tracked files under `browser/` and `toolkit/` of Firefox `FIREFOX_159_0_RELEASE` (`9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402`). Fourteen names have no textual match in those directories; textfox itself defines some of them. Counts are matching lines, including comments and tests, not proven consumers. The script requires a clean tagged checkout with every tracked file under both directories present. The wider search found `--newtab-border-color` in Firefox's non-handoff search suggestion CSS; that local declaration and its consumers were outside the earlier narrower search. `probe-newtab-search-input.py` now seeds local form history with remote suggestions disabled and opens the non-handoff suggestion table in eight profiles. A body-level token intervention leaves the table's token and one-offs border unchanged; an intervention directly on the table changes the computed border. Both inline changes are restored. This shows local override behavior in sampled computed styles, not painted contrast or all suggestions. It does not search all of Firefox, prove runtime cascade, or establish tag-to-tested-build correspondence. Reproduce with `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/textfox-t2-css-parser python3 tools/firefox-chrome/inventory-firefox-variable-source.py /path/to/firefox --markdown`; `tinycss2==1.4.0` must be available. Run `python3 tools/firefox-chrome/test-firefox-variable-source.py` for its matching and report tests.

## New-tab selector states

Expanded-selector queries cover all 18 non-pseudo `newtab.css` records across default pages, saved history cards, two solid wallpaper classes, shortcut-tile pointer hover, and the in-page Nova search input. The live shortcut menu is a `panel-list` without `.context-menu` in eight sampled profiles; a separate temporary `div.context-menu` control matches the rule and receives its configured border and rounding. The search/input and hover records match the sampled light-DOM `moz-urlbar` input when browser Nova is on; sampled handoff controls use shadow DOM and do not match those records. Each interaction result is bounded to its fixture; grouped branches, the logo `::before`, image wallpapers, other menu variants, and painted accessibility remain separate. An initial control that altered the live menu raced its replacement and could not read it; the final probe uses a removable independent control, not a production change.

## Shared overwrite selector records

With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` set, `probe-container-colors.py` queries the complete `overwrites.css` records after creating Firefox's ten current container identities. Across eight vanilla/textfox, horizontal/vertical, and Nova profiles, the root and seven named identity records matched real DOM; the `turquoise` record did not match those sampled identities. A temporary XUL element with `data-color="turquoise"` matched it and, with textfox applied, received the same nonempty color/icon/tab tokens; the element was removed afterward. All nine records therefore have bounded structural matches in a real state or explicit control, not proof that Firefox offers `turquoise`, that the colors meet the spec constraint, or that indicators paint correctly.

## Toolbar selector states

`test-bookmarks-toolbar-live.py` moves a synthetic pointer over the navigation and bookmarks toolbars in eight themed profiles; both hover branches match and their border and title colors settle to the configured accent. `probe-tab-colors.py` checks the full titlebar-menubar selector in four temporary-theme profiles: the sampled root lacked `tabsintitlebar`, while temporarily adding it made the selector match and applied `fit-content`/3px values; the original attribute was restored. Together with the default-DOM, identity, bookmark, and titlebar controls, the 23 non-pseudo `navbar.css` rule records have bounded structural matches in sampled states or explicit interventions. Five pseudo-element records, grouped-selector branches, natural titlebar/platform variants, inactive legacy media rules, and painted behavior remain separate checks.

## Menu selector scopes

With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` set, `probe-menu-active.py` queries the full enabled-active and disabled-hover menu selectors in real or synthetic popup states, and queries `.menupopup-arrowscrollbox` inside the popup's open shadow root. `probe-panel-colors.py` separately confirms `slot[part="content"]` in the app-menu shadow root. Eight of the ten `menus.css` rule records have bounded structural matches across these samples; the nested `#appMenu-popup #shadow-root` and its child rule do not match the sampled actual shadow structure. The active and disabled states were exercised in both Nova modes; this count is not proof of painted focus, every menu, or platform behavior.

## URL-bar selector records

The expanded-selector probe structurally matched all five `urlbar.css` rule records with URL-bar results open in each browser/new-tab Nova combination. `test-urlbar-live.py` now also evaluates the complete one-offs selector while temporarily varying the real `noresults` and `hidden` attributes: it matched only the populated/unhidden control and the configured border followed that match in both browser Nova states. This is bounded DOM/computed-style evidence; the attribute intervention is not a naturally produced no-results case and does not establish painted layout or keyboard behavior for every provider.

## Sidebar selector scopes

`probe-selector-dom.py` now exposes an explicit open-shadow-root query mode to callers without pretending selectors cross shadow boundaries. With the expanded inventory, `test-sidebar-tools-live.py` finds the splitter, wrapper, and pseudo-class-locked wrapper-hover records inside `sidebar-main`'s open shadow root in both Nova states. `test-sidebar-theme-live.py` confirms the full `:root[lwtheme] sidebar-main` relationship in four temporary-theme profiles. Across these and earlier sidebar fixtures, 13 of 14 non-pseudo `sidebar.css` records have a bounded structural match; the remaining `#sidebar-select-box` is a Floorp-specific rule not found in the sampled Firefox light DOM. The grouped pinned-tab selector matches its `.tab-stack` branch while its old ID branch remains absent in the sampled state. Four pseudo-element records, closed shadow roots, Floorp behavior, and painting are not covered by that count.

## Tab selector state coverage

With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` set, `probe-tab-colors.py` queries the full `tabs.css` records in horizontal, synthetic-pointer toolbar hover, pseudo-class-locked unselected-tab hover, and Firefox View states. `test-sidebar-tools-live.py` queries the same records during pseudo-class-locked vertical-strip hover and collapsed pinned-tab states. Together with the existing temporary adjacent-button controls for normal/private roots, all ten non-pseudo tab rule records have a structural match in sampled states or explicit controls; four pseudo-element records are not counted by this query. The grouped adjacency and Firefox View rules contain multiple branches, so a record-level match does not prove every branch or painted styling. Physical-pointer, other tab states, platform behavior, and contrast remain open.

## Findbar selector state coverage

With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` set, `test-findbar-live.py` queries expanded `findbar.css` selectors while the bar is open, after a synthetic pointer hover, and after Escape hides it. Across 16 vanilla/textfox, Nova, bidi pseudo-locale, and accessibility-preference profiles, all five non-pseudo rule records have structural matches across those states; the two pseudo-element records are not queried. Pointer hover reached the bar in all samples, and themed border/title colors settled to the configured accent. The existing headless-Sway focus-pixel check still passed. Physical-pointer behavior, real RTL/forced colors, and complete painted title/contrast checks remain open.

## Browser selector state coverage

With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` set, `test-split-fullscreen-live.py` queries the expanded `chrome/browser.css` selectors in windowed split, synthetic-pointer hover, window fullscreen, and content-requested DOM fullscreen states in both Nova modes. The two hover rules matched after the pointer reached the selected panel, and configured accent border/title colors settled. Those split samples structurally match 15 of the file's 16 non-pseudo rule records; the remaining `:root[lwtheme]` browser-root rule matches in four temporary-theme samples from `probe-tab-colors.py`, with a class-removal control. The five pseudo-element records are not counted by the selector query. This is bounded structural and computed-style evidence, not physical-pointer, painted, cross-platform, or complete pseudo-element verification.

## Bookmark creation with hidden URL-bar icons

`probe-bookmark-action.py` exercises the actual app-menu Bookmark This Page action on disposable `about:robots` and local-file pages. With Firefox's default `browser.bookmarks.editDialog.showForNewBookmarks=true` (also explicitly set and verified), default textfox hides `#star-button-box`. In all four Nova/page combinations, the URL-bar star gained `starred`, but the edit popup stayed closed and the Places bookmark lookup remained empty for 10 seconds. The sampled `BookmarkingUI.anchor` getter threw `Error: PageActions: No anchor node for bookmark`. Vanilla profiles and textfox profiles with the temporary `--tf-display-urlbar-icons: flex` control opened the edit popup and saved after its Done button was clicked. With the edit-dialog preference temporarily disabled, default textfox saved the bookmark despite the missing anchor. Tagged [bookmark code](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-places.js) marks a new bookmark with an unsaved GUID before opening the edit panel, and tagged [page-action code](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-pageActions.js) throws when no candidate anchor has geometry. This is a verified local functionality gap in the sampled configuration, not a production fix; actual public pages, persistence across restart, and other entry points remain unverified. Existing spec-edit approval boundary remains in force.

## App-menu subview icons

Tagged [app-menu markup](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/appmenu-viewcache.inc.xhtml) defines the bookmarks, history, more-tools, and help subviews. `probe-app-menu-subviews.py` opens each using Firefox's actual menu buttons in vanilla and textfox profiles across both Nova states. In themed samples, 4 bookmark, 5 history, 1 more-tools, and 6–7 help ID candidates had visible geometry with non-`none` icon URLs; every sampled URL loaded with a missing-resource negative control. The more-tools view contained eight visible developer entries with non-`none` URLs that also loaded. Inserting a disposable bookmark changed the menu item's `starred` state and themed icon from hollow to filled in both Nova states; this was a seeded state, not a verified bookmark-creation interaction. With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` set to the expanded selector JSON, the probe additionally queries all icon-rule candidates in each subview and confirms the full starred selector matches. These are sequential structural samples; earlier lazily built views may remain in the DOM, and media conditions and painting remain separate checks. A separate bookmark-action probe traced the earlier inconclusive click to the hidden-anchor gap documented below; this seeded-state check is not counted as a passing creation action. Painted icons, all subview states, and bookmark action persistence remain open.

## Default-state selector sample

`expand-rule-selectors.py` expands nested selector branches into bounded DOM-query candidates, with fixture checks for commas, nesting markers, and unsupported escaped/quoted markers. `probe-selector-dom.py` queries those candidates in the default browser chrome and initialized `about:newtab` document across independent browser/new-tab Nova settings. In each of four headless Firefox 159.0 samples, 156 of 357 chrome records had a light-DOM structural match, 175 did not, and 26 involved pseudo-elements and were not queried; the 20 content records yielded 9, 10, and 1 respectively. There were no invalid queries in those samples. Results record media conditions separately: a structural match does not mean a conditional rule is active. A follow-up queries the same chrome candidates sequentially with the app menu, URL-bar results, bookmarks sidebar, findbar, and link context menu open. In each profile, the union across the default and those states contains 180 structurally matched chrome records, 62 with nonzero visible geometry in at least one state; 24 structural matches were new beyond the default snapshot. Later states retain lazily created DOM from earlier ones. The query does not cross shadow roots, check computed declarations, open every UI state, or prove painting; missing default-state matches are not removal claims. Tagged source correspondence and per-state review remain open. Reproduce with `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/textfox-t2-css-parser python3 tools/firefox-chrome/expand-rule-selectors.py > /tmp/textfox-t2-expanded.json`, then run `probe-selector-dom.py /tmp/textfox-t2-expanded.json` with the live-tool venv. New-tab feeds may make network requests.

## Nested extension context selectors

`probe-extension-context-matrix.py` installs a synthetic extension using the Bitwarden selector ID, creates one root and eight named submenu items, and opens the real link context menu and submenu in both Nova and extension-context preference states. All eight children had visible geometry in four profiles; the legacy media query remained false. A temporary sheet built from the actual textfox media block with only its preference syntax modernized applied the expected root and eight child icon URLs when enabled, including the overlapping autofill variants. All nine resources loaded with a missing-resource negative control. The synthetic root exposed no `.menu-iconic-icon` through its light DOM or open shadow root, so its separate opacity rule remains unverified; this is not proof that Firefox paints no native icon. Real Bitwarden behavior, sidebar-action selectors, and painted output remain open.

## Extension toolbar identity matrix

`probe-extension-toolbar-matrix.py` installs 11 separate synthetic browser-action extensions using the IDs named in textfox's toolbar-icon rules, pins them in disposable profiles, and verifies the ID/icon pairs against `chrome/icons.css`. Across both Nova states and both toolbar-icon preference values, every fixture produced a visible toolbar-icon target; the legacy media query remained false. A temporary modern-query control applied each corresponding textfox image when enabled, including the two 1.3 scale overrides. All 11 SVG resources loaded, with a missing-resource negative control. These are synthetic IDs and browser-action widgets, not tests of the real extensions, `.eom-addon-button`, the separate Bitwarden image selector, or painted icon appearance.

## Extension toolbar context icons

Tagged [Firefox popup markup](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/main-popupset.inc.xhtml) defines the toolbar-context extension classes used by textfox. `probe-extension-icon.py` now right-clicks its pinned disposable extension, independently varies all three icon preferences and both Nova states, and samples the real toolbar context menu. In 16 profiles, five candidate items had visible geometry and computed `background-image: none`; the legacy menu-icon query was false, and a modern-query control applied an icon to Manage Extension only when its own preference was enabled. Clicking Customize Toolbar entered Firefox's customization mode in all 16 profiles. Move-to-panel and remove-from-toolbar items were hidden in this fixture; unified-extension menus, other extensions, and painted icons remain unverified.

## Bookmarks menu conditional selector

`probe-bookmarks-menu-icons.py` places Firefox's bookmarks-menu button in the navigation bar, opens its real popup and toolbar submenu, and samples both toolbar visibility states across both Nova and icon-preference values. In all four profiles, `setToolbarVisibility(..., false)` produced `PersonalToolbar.collapsed === true` with an empty `collapsed` attribute, not `collapsed="true"`. Consequently textfox's `#main-window:has(#PersonalToolbar[collapsed="true"]) #BMB_bookmarksPopup #BMB_viewBookmarksToolbar` selector did not match. Temporarily setting the same attribute to the literal string `true` made it match; restoring the original empty value restored the prior state. The legacy icon query was false, and a separate modern-query control reached the visible submenu item when enabled. Clicking that item showed the previously collapsed toolbar in all four profiles; the probe then used Escape to close both popups. The logically collapsed toolbar still measured 12px high in these themed headless samples; no vanilla or painted comparison establishes its impact. This is source/live evidence, not a production fix.

## Bookmark toolbar icon targets

Tagged [browser Places code](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-places.js) dynamically builds `#OtherBookmarks`. `test-bookmarks-toolbar-live.py` now exercises a real toolbar folder and an Other Bookmarks item in eight themed Nova/alignment/icon-preference profiles. The folder's icon matched textfox's selector and computed `display: none`; a temporary display control gave it 16×16 geometry. The Other Bookmarks button computed the configured icon background, hidden label child, zero image margins, and 25px width; its SVG loaded and its popup opened with the disposable bookmark. The probe waits for Firefox localization before sampling its `label` attribute. The same probe adds 35 disposable toolbar bookmarks to produce actual overflow. In all eight profiles, `#PlacesChevron` had visible geometry, its configured SVG loaded, and pointer activation opened an overflow popup containing visible fixture entries. A separate attempt to query the chrome XUL Other Bookmarks element's computed accessibility label through Marionette timed out; that is a probe limitation, not proof of a missing accessible name. Painted icons, screen-reader behavior, vanilla comparison, and other folder states remain open.

## Rule-record routing

`check-rule-ledger.py` routes all 377 current syntactic CSS rule records to [T2 ledger groups](T2-INVENTORY.md#rule-record-routing), including nine distinct icon scopes. It preserves each original file, line, enclosing context, and selector in JSON, and rejects unmapped files, icon media contexts, or conditional icon anchors. Its fixture tests cover nested scopes, unknown media, and the bare container-link type selector. Group routing is not selector-level proof or a T2 completion claim; several groups explicitly retain local and platform gaps.

## New-tab wallpaper weather token

Tagged [customize-menu SCSS](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/extensions/newtab/content-src/components/CustomizeMenu/_CustomizeMenu.scss) uses `--newtab-weather-background-color` for the classic personalize button under wallpaper classes. `probe-newtab-wallpaper-tokens.py` tests two real solid-color wallpaper preferences, both browser and new-tab Nova states, and vanilla/textfox: 16 disposable profiles. After disabling the button's transition for measurement, themed classic buttons computed a transparent background; a target-level token control changed it in all eight classic samples. The eight Nova samples used a different `moz-button`; its inner button's background did not change under the same control, although the token itself did. Pointer activation opened the customization dialog and changed the actual button's `aria-expanded` from false to true in all 16 profiles; for Nova that attribute is on the shadow button, not the host. This does not establish behavior for image wallpapers, real OS forced colors, painted contrast, or other weather-token consumers. Disposable new-tab feeds may make network requests.

## New-tab secondary background consumer

Tagged [Firefox card SCSS](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/extensions/newtab/content-src/components/Card/_Card.scss) uses `--newtab-background-color-secondary` on `.card-outer`. `probe-newtab-cards.py` now measures that token and the background of a real bookmarked activity card. In all 16 `about:newtab`/`about:home`, vanilla/textfox, and independent browser/new-tab Nova samples, a temporary card-level token intervention changed its computed background to the control color. The sampled themed card retained textfox's computed `color-mix` token. With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` set to the expanded inventory, the same 16 samples also confirm full structural matches for `.card-outer`, `.card`, `.card-preview-image-outer`, and `.card-context`; those were absent in the earlier default new-tab snapshot. This establishes one consumer and these sampled selectors, not wallpaper-image variants, all card types, or painted contrast. The disposable new-tab feed may make network requests.

## Icon fill-opacity input

Textfox's app-menu and context-menu icon rules reference `--toolbarbutton-icon-fill-opacity`. A moving Searchfox query found no occurrence in its searched Firefox-release tree; this is not proof of absence elsewhere or at runtime. `probe-root-variables.py` found the root value empty in vanilla and textfox across both Nova states. In themed profiles, `probe-panel-colors.py` sampled the private-window item's `::before`, and `probe-context-icon.py` sampled the link-menu private-window item: each target's token was empty and computed `fill-opacity` was `1`. A temporary target-level `0.37` input changed each computed fill opacity to `0.37`. The panel probe covered eight samples and the context probe four; neither establishes actual SVG pixel opacity, all icon targets, other themes, or a safe replacement.

## Custom-property syntax reconciliation

`inventory-variables.py --non-textfox --check-ledger` enumerates declaration and `var()` reference locations and verifies that the [T2 ledger](T2-INVENTORY.md#custom-property-syntax-reconciliation) names every non-textfox property found by the independent syntax inventory. The current checkout has 40 names, 55 declaration records, and 63 reference records. This exposes previously implicit names such as `--panel-background`, `--toolbarbutton-icon-fill-opacity`, and the two URL-bar height variables. A property assigned but not referenced by textfox can still be consumed by Firefox. Source provenance, actual cascade, and live consumers remain separate evidence; this reconciliation does not complete T2. Both inventory fixture tests pass with tinycss2 1.4.0.

## Secure and mixed-content identity selectors

`probe-identity-live.py` creates a disposable CA trusted only by each temporary profile and serves local HTTPS and mixed-content fixtures on loopback. In both Nova states, the secure page reached `pageproxystate="valid"` with `verifiedDomain`; the mixed page reached both `verifiedDomain` and `mixedActiveBlocked`, did not execute the HTTP script, and made no request to the fixture HTTP server. Tagged [site-identity code](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-siteIdentity.js) adds those classes together for blocked active mixed content. With textfox configured to `--tf-display-urlbar-icons: none` or `flex`, the permission box and granted-permissions icon computed that display value in both states. Vanilla comparisons used Firefox's own display values. The natural mixed fixture has both classes. A temporary DOM intervention removes `verifiedDomain`, leaving `mixedActiveBlocked`: the themed permission box and icon still follow the configured display. Removing both classes changes at least the icon's computed display; the probe then restores the original classes. This isolates the selector branch diagnostically, not a naturally occurring identity state. A follow-up starts a real geolocation watch after activating Firefox's permission prompt. Its provider returns a fixed synthetic coordinate from loopback, with Wi-Fi scanning disabled; no real location is used. Across six vanilla/textfox and Nova profiles, the `hasSharingIcon` attribute and `image[sharing="true"]` sibling appeared and the permission box had nonzero geometry. Textfox computed `-4px` sibling margin and kept the sharing box visible with either icon setting; the granted icon followed `none`/`flex`. Vanilla computed `0px` margin. A second disposable tab requests Firefox fake media streams through the real permission prompt and stops every track after measurement. Across six profiles per mode, camera+microphone and camera-only produced `sharing="camera"`; microphone-only produced `sharing="microphone"`. Neither matched textfox's sibling selector `image[sharing="true"]`, and its margin stayed `0px`. A temporary selector control accepting any `sharing` attribute produced `-4px` in themed profiles. Run the default combined fixture or set `TEXTFOX_FAKE_MEDIA_KIND=camera` or `microphone` for individual modes. With `TEXTFOX_SHARING_PAGE=mixed`, the same geolocation and three fake-media modes also ran on blocked-mixed pages across six profiles each. Both identity classes remained present, the geolocation sibling matched `sharing="true"`, and camera/microphone siblings did not; no mixed-script request reached the local server. With `TEXTFOX_SELECTOR_INVENTORY` pointing to the expanded selector JSON, the same probe also queries every navbar rule in each identity state. Across six profiles each for secure and mixed sharing, the full geolocation sibling selector matched the secure branch and, on mixed pages, the mixed branch; neither matched the fake-media state. This is a selector observation, not a recommendation to change spacing or proof of a painted defect. Real devices, other permission states, painted output, and public sites remain unverified. The probe requires `openssl` and `certutil`; it does not alter the user's Firefox profile.

## Panel and menu variable consumers

Tagged [Firefox popup CSS](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/toolkit/themes/shared/popup.css) uses `--panel-background-color` and `--panel-border-radius`; tagged [menu CSS](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/toolkit/themes/shared/menu.css) uses `--panel-separator-color` for a separator's generated border. With distinct configured background, border, and rounding, `probe-panel-colors.py` found textfox's `--panel-background`, `--panel-border-radius`, and `--panel-separator-color` values on the app-menu panel in eight samples. Its content slot computed the configured 7px radius, while `--panel-background-color` remained `Field` and the slot background was white; the existing temporary `--panel-background-color` control changes it. `probe-menu-active.py` adds a visible synthetic separator: its generated border followed a non-default `--tf-border` in both Nova states, with vanilla comparisons. Its `--builtin` variant programmatically opens Firefox's toolbar context menu and observes the same relationship on one visible built-in separator in each profile. A temporary separator-level token intervention changes that generated border in both vanilla and textfox profiles. These are computed results, not proof of all panel consumers, real OS forced colors, painted contrast, or a safe production change.

## Extension badge preference control

`probe-extension-icon.py` now sets a `7` badge on its temporary uBlock-ID fixture and varies the toolbar and context icon preferences independently across both Nova states. In all eight headless Firefox 159.0 samples, the badge had nonzero geometry and computed `display: block`, even with the toolbar preference enabled. The corresponding legacy media query was false. A temporary modern-query sheet containing the existing blanket `.toolbarbutton-badge { display: none; }` rule hid the badge only when the toolbar preference was enabled; the context preference did not control that intervention. The fixture's actual badge text is in `textContent`, not the XUL-style `value` property initially assumed by the probe. This demonstrates the rule's sampled effect, not whether hiding notification badges is desirable or whether the badge is painted and legible. Only this synthetic extension is covered; no production preference or stylesheet was changed.

## Container link context icon selector

Tagged [browser context markup](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-context.inc.xhtml) defines `<menuitem id="context-openlinkincontainertab">`. In `chrome/icons.css`, the corresponding selector is instead the bare type selector `context-openlinkincontainertab`; it is not included in the earlier simple-ID candidate count. `probe-container-context-icon.py` opens a real link menu inside a disposable container tab, verifying both tab and content principal identity. In both Nova states and both icon-preference values, the visible target was a `menuitem`, matched the ID selector, and did not match the bare type selector. A temporary modern-preference sheet reproducing the original selector list did not apply its icon; a separate temporary ID-selector control applied an icon when the preference was enabled. This is an independent selector mismatch in addition to the legacy media-query issue, not a production fix or proof of painted icon behavior. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-container-context-icon.py`.

## Password-field context icon

`probe-context-icon.py` right-clicks a synthetic password field and activates Reveal Password twice by pointer, checking the field's privileged `revealPassword` state and the menu item's checked state. In both Nova states and both icon-preference values, the first activation revealed the fixture and the second concealed it; the item was unchecked and then checked when each menu opened. Nine simple icon-ID candidates had visible geometry in each state and computed `background-image: none`. Adding this context to the prior sampled union brings it to 54 distinct candidates. An exploratory assertion incorrectly expected the HTML `type` to change; tagged [Firefox context-menu code](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/actors/ContextMenuChild.sys.mjs) toggles `revealPassword` instead, and the corrected probe verifies `type` stays `password` on reveal. This synthetic fixture does not test saved-login, keyboard activation, security guarantees, or painted icons.

## Findbar Match Case keyboard path

`test-findbar-live.py` now starts from the focused find field, reaches Match Case by Tab, and presses Space twice. Across 16 headless Firefox 159.0 samples spanning both Nova states, vanilla/textfox, bidi pseudo-locale, and the accessibility-theme preference, four Tab presses reached the checkbox; it matched `:focus-visible`, enabled case-sensitive not-found feedback, and restored the match when disabled. The checkbox host's computed `outline-style` was `none` in both vanilla and textfox samples. Tagged [checkbox CSS](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/toolkit/themes/shared/checkbox.css) instead applies `outline-style: auto` to its `.checkbox-check` child on `:focus-visible`. The probe found that child at nonzero geometry with a computed 3px auto outline in all 16 profiles; Tab away changed its outline to `none` and Shift+Tab restored it. A follow-up `test-findbar-paint-sway.py` uses a disposable 1280×800 headless Sway compositor, captures the checkbox region while focused, blurred, and refocused, and compares it with a repeated focused-frame control. In four Nova/vanilla-textfox samples, 68–80 pixels changed on blur; the repeated focused and refocused crops matched the initial crop exactly. This is bounded painted-pixel evidence for that region, not proof of contrast or focus accessibility on a real desktop. It does not substitute for a real RTL locale or OS forced colors.

## Completed-download context icons

Tagged [downloads panel markup](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/downloads/content/downloadsPanel.inc.xhtml) defines `#downloadsContextMenu` and its class- and command-based items. `probe-download-icons.py` creates and verifies a local data-URL download inside each disposable profile, opens the actual panel and right-clicks its completed row. Across both Nova states and both icon-preference values on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, five of the textfox-targeted menu items had visible geometry and all computed `background-image: none`; disabled items are included. The legacy media query was false in each sample, while the modern query followed the preference. A temporary modern-query user sheet applied Firefox's folder icon to the visible Show item only when enabled. Pointer activation of Remove From History removed the fixture entry while leaving its file intact in all four profiles. This does not cover active, paused, failed, blocked, private, or library download states, nor painted icons. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-download-icons.py`; no network download is used.

## Bookmark context icon candidates

`test-bookmarks-toolbar-live.py` now right-clicks an actual disposable toolbar bookmark before navigating it. It checks both alignment values, both Nova states, and both context-icon preference values. `check-places-icon-live.py` extracts 27 simple ID candidates from the nested `#placesContext` rules, decoding CSS escapes, and joins them with the live menu. Tagged [Places context markup](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/places/content/placesContextMenu.inc.xhtml) defines 26 candidates; `toggle_PersonalToolbar` is absent from that file but present in the live menu. All 27 candidates were present in each of eight headless Firefox 159.0 samples; 14 had visible geometry, including disabled items, and their computed background images were `none`. The legacy preference query remained false while the modern control followed the preference. A second fixture creates a toolbar folder with one local child. Its menu exposes 12 candidate IDs with visible geometry, all with computed `background-image: none`; the bookmark and folder menus expose 18 distinct candidate IDs together. Pointer activation of the folder's Open All in Tabs command opened its child in all eight profiles. This ID join does not establish full-selector matches or painted icons; history/download menus and other Places states remain open. Run the live probe, then `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/textfox-t2-css-parser python3 tools/firefox-chrome/check-places-icon-live.py <jsonl> --source <tagged-placesContextMenu.inc.xhtml>` with tinycss2 1.4.0 available.

## Browser root background

Tagged [browser markup](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-box.inc.xhtml) defines `#browser`. In four headless Firefox 159.0 samples with temporary light/dark themes and both Nova states, `probe-tab-colors.py` found `:root[lwtheme] #browser:not(.browser-toolbox-background)` matched. The computed background color followed `--lwt-accent-color`; adding the class temporarily switched it to `--tf-bg`, then the probe restored the class. The mismatch variant deliberately sets those colors apart and confirms the difference. In these samples the accent was an RGB color, which `CSS.supports('background-image', …)` rejected; computed `background-image` was `none`. This is not proof about image themes, painting, or whether the declaration needs changing. Run the probe with `--mismatch` to repeat the distinguishing control.

## Selected tab radius token

Tagged Firefox 159.0 [`tabs.css`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/themes/shared/tabbrowser/tabs.css) uses `--tab-border-radius` on tab backgrounds. `probe-tab-colors.py` now configures `--tf-rounding: 5px` and checks both the computed `--tab-border-radius` and top-left radius on the selected horizontal tab background. All four Nova/light-dark theme samples on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` computed 5px for both; the accessibility-theme preference and deliberately mismatched background variants also passed. A follow-up in `test-sidebar-tools-live.py` configures the same radius for collapsed vertical tabs. The selected tab and four pinned tab backgrounds computed 5px for both the token and top-left radius in both Nova states. These samples do not cover other unselected or expanded tab states, painted corners, or installed delivery paths. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-tab-colors.py`.

## Multiselected tab background

`probe-tab-colors.py` now adds an unselected tab to Firefox's multiselection using `gBrowser.addToMultiSelectedTabs`. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, both temporary light and dark themes in both browser Nova states produced an unselected, multiselected tab and a `.tab-background[multiselected]` with nonzero geometry. The background computed transparent. Setting `--tab-selected-bgcolor` only on that background to a diagnostic color changed its computed background in all four samples, confirming the variable's effect on this state. Geometry was sampled during tab initialization and is not a layout measurement. This programmatic multiselection does not test keyboard or pointer selection, painted visibility, contrast, other themes, or delivery paths. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-tab-colors.py`.

## Shared root color-variable chain

`probe-root-variables.py` compares 19 root custom properties in disposable vanilla and textfox profiles for both browser Nova states. In headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, textfox profiles with non-default 7px border, 3px radius, background, and accent computed the requested border, tab radius, and toolbar-field background. In both themed samples `--toolbar-field-color` and `--toolbar-bgcolor` were empty, as were textfox's derived `--tab-selected-bgcolor`, `--tab-hover-background-color`, `--toolbarbutton-hover-background`, and `--button-background-color-active`. Vanilla also had empty values for the two input properties, but its `--button-background-color-active` was nonempty.

A temporary root-level intervention supplying both inputs made all four derived themed properties nonempty; all 19 sampled root values returned to their previous values after the intervention. This demonstrates the dependency chain at the root, not that every consumer uses it or that the diagnostic colors are safe. Existing tab and menu probes separately sample consumers. Other themes, painted active/hover states, and contrast remain unverified. V4–V6 cover configuration and UI behavior; no production fix or new invariant was made. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-root-variables.py`.

## Active app-menu fullscreen token

Tagged Firefox 159.0 [`panelUI-shared.css`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/themes/shared/customizableui/panelUI-shared.css) uses `--button-background-color-active` for the hovered and active app-menu fullscreen icon. `probe-root-variables.py` opens the actual app menu and locks `:hover` and `:active` on its enabled fullscreen button without activating fullscreen. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the icon computed a translucent dark background and nonempty active token in both vanilla Nova states; both themed states computed a transparent background and an empty token. Temporarily setting the token on the icon changed its background in all four profiles. The probe removes its pseudo-class locks and restores inline styles afterward.

A follow-up presses the actual icon with a Marionette pointer action, waits for real `:hover:active`, then releases outside the button and verifies fullscreen was not activated. The same vanilla/textfox computed-background difference appeared in all four profiles. This establishes a computed active-state difference and a working variable control for this icon; it does not establish painted visibility, contrast, a complete activation cycle, or behavior of other button consumers. V5 and V6 cover UI feedback and interaction; a §B entry remains subject to spec-protocol approval. No production fix was applied. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-root-variables.py`.

## Moving-release shared-variable search

A moving Firefox-release Searchfox query returned no textual occurrences for `--urlbarView-highlight-background`, `--toolbox-non-lwt-bgcolor`, `--tab-selected-bgcolor`, `--tab-hover-background-color`, `--toolbar-field-color`, or `--toolbar-bgcolor`. A query for `--toolbarbutton-hover-background` found definitions and uses in DevTools CSS. Textfox defines several of these identifiers itself, so no upstream occurrence does not make its own definitions invalid or prove all runtime consumers are absent. Searchfox's moving tree is not pinned to the tested Firefox 159.0 build; runtime evidence for selected tab, menu, and toolbar consumers is recorded separately. No source or product change was made.

## Back button hover token

Tagged Firefox 159.0 [`toolbarbuttons.css`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/themes/shared/toolbarbuttons.css) uses `--toolbarbutton-background-color-hover` for the hovered toolbar-button icon. `probe-root-variables.py` now navigates twice in each disposable profile, waits for an enabled Back button, and locks its `:hover` pseudo-class. In vanilla and textfox profiles with both Nova states, its icon computed the same translucent hover background. Temporarily setting textfox's older `--toolbarbutton-hover-background` on the icon did not change that computed background; setting Firefox's current hover token did. The probe disables the icon transition during these diagnostic overrides and restores inline styles and the pseudo-class lock afterward. This covers a single Back-button hover fixture, not real pointer input, active-state consumers, painted contrast, other themes, or all consumers of the old token. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-root-variables.py`.

## Container tab colors

Tagged Firefox 159.0 [`usercontext.css`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/contextualidentity/content/usercontext.css) defines `--identity-tab-color` and `--identity-icon-color`, plus Nova's `--identity-stroke-color`. Tagged [`tabs.css`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/themes/shared/tabbrowser/tabs.css) uses the icon color for classic container strokes and the stroke color for Nova strokes. `chrome/overwrites.css` contains existing fixed color values for eight identity colors; these predate this probe and conflict with the project's no-hard-coded-colors constraint.

`probe-container-colors.py` opens tabs for the four default container identities in disposable vanilla and textfox profiles. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, each tab had the expected identity color class and nonzero geometry in both Nova states. In classic samples, computed line background followed the sampled identity icon color, which differed between vanilla and textfox. In Nova samples, line background color computed transparent in both themes, but each unselected tab had a gradient using Firefox's `--identity-stroke-color`; the computed stroke tokens matched between vanilla and textfox. Transparent background color alone therefore does not establish an invisible indicator. A follow-up creates six additional container identities only in the disposable profile, covering Firefox's blue, orange, green, pink, cyan, yellow, red, purple, violet, and gray color names. In all eight Nova, theme, and horizontal/vertical combinations, the sampled themed cyan, violet, and gray legacy tab-color tokens matched vanilla while the seven sampled textfox-overridden names differed. Textfox has a `turquoise` identity selector, while tagged Firefox identity CSS defines `cyan`; this evidence does not establish whether another supported state uses `turquoise` or whether the fallback is visually wrong. The follow-up also covers horizontal and vertical tab layouts. In each of the eight Nova, theme, and orientation profiles it selects, then pins, the first default container tab and verifies the line has nonzero geometry. Its computed selected and selected-pinned line colors match each other; Nova uses the same sampled Firefox stroke color in vanilla and textfox. These are computed-style and geometry samples, not painted contrast or proof for unselected pinned tabs, other identities in selected/pinned states, custom colors, or other platforms. Probe development first encountered a JavaScript parenthesis error and then a wrong Firefox module URL; both were probe errors, not product defects. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-container-colors.py`.

## Multiple collapsed pinned tabs

Firefox 159.0 tagged source defines `box#vertical-tabs`, `#sidebar-box`, `#sidebar-header`, and `#sidebar` in [`browser-box.inc.xhtml`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-box.inc.xhtml), and `#pinned-tabs-container` in the tagged toolbar markup linked above. The tagged sidebar source defines the tools splitter and wrapper in `sidebar-main.mjs` and `sidebar-main.css`. This confirms individual source elements, not every nested selector or state.

`test-sidebar-tools-live.py` now pins four tabs in the collapsed vertical launcher after its one-tab check. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the pinned container and tab strip measured equal widths in both browser Nova states: 51 CSS pixels with Nova off and 47 with Nova on. The container's reported `scrollWidth` equaled its `clientWidth` in each sample. After programmatically expanding the launcher, all four pinned tabs had nonzero rectangles inside the container and computed `visibility: visible` in both Nova states. These measurements do not establish painted visibility or clipping, keyboard access, other window sizes, or other platforms. With four pinned tabs and the strip collapsed, the full legacy `#tabbrowser-tabs[orient="vertical"]:not([expanded]) #vertical-pinned-tabs-container` selector matched zero light-DOM elements in both states. The corresponding selector using `#pinned-tabs-container` matched one, and the sibling `.tab-stack` selector matched five. The stale selector remains unchanged; matching container widths alone do not prove the rule is unnecessary or establish other states. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-sidebar-tools-live.py`.

## URL bar height variables

Textfox's `chrome/navbar.css` assigns `--urlbar-toolbar-height` and `--urlbar-container-height` to `32px` on every element. Tagged [`SmartbarInput.mjs`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/urlbar/content/SmartbarInput.mjs) sets `--urlbar-container-height` on its popover anchor from its measured height, but that source describes `SmartbarInput`, not necessarily the regular browser URL bar. The sampled regular browser's `gURLBar` constructor is `UrlbarInput`; tagged [`UrlbarInputBase.mjs`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/components/urlbar/content/UrlbarInputBase.mjs) instead opens its panel popover and marks the host with a `popover-open` attribute.

`test-navbar-options-live.py` now reports both properties and the URL bar rectangle after a valid page loads. In all 16 headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` combinations of browser Nova, navigation-button option, bidi pseudo-locale, and accessibility-theme preference, both properties computed `32px` on `#urlbar` and its measured height was 32 CSS pixels. `test-urlbar-live.py` also samples the anchor with URL bar results open. In both Nova states, `#urlbar-container` measured 40 CSS pixels high, computed `--urlbar-container-height: 32px`, and had no inline value for that property. The `moz-urlbar` host did not match `:popover-open`; that was not evidence that results were closed. A follow-up `probe-urlbar-popover.py` verified the host's `popover-open` attribute and the panel's `:popover-open` state in vanilla and textfox profiles across both Nova states. On the 40px anchor, both variables computed empty in vanilla and `32px` with textfox; no inline `--urlbar-container-height` appeared in these regular URL-bar samples. The `SmartbarInput` measurement path, its actual consumers, and painted positioning remain unverified. Run the URL-bar probes with headless Firefox.

## Navigation button configuration

Firefox release `browser/base/content/navigator-toolbox.inc.xhtml` defines `#back-button`, matching `chrome/navbar.css`. `tools/firefox-chrome/test-navbar-options-live.py` checks both navigation buttons in disposable profiles. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, `--tf-display-nav-buttons: none` gave both buttons zero geometry and `flex` made them visible in both browser Nova states. With `flex`, clicking Back and Forward navigated between two local data pages in both states. The probe also checks `--tf-display-urlbar-icons` on the bookmark star. After navigating to a local data page and waiting for the URL bar's valid page state, the star followed `none` and `flex` in both browser Nova states. An earlier version checked too early and failed on `flex`: Firefox release `browser/themes/shared/urlbar.css` hides page actions when `pageproxystate` is invalid. A diagnostic attempt also used an unavailable `InspectorUtils.getCSSStyleRules` API and was removed. Neither failure established a theme defect. A separate keyboard experiment called `focus()` on the enabled Back button after navigation; focus remained on browser content in a textfox profile. A control with only minimal CSS to show the navigation buttons and bookmark star gave the same result in both Nova states. Firefox release `browser/base/content/browser-toolbarKeyNav.js` explains that toolbar buttons are not normally focusable until its keyboard navigator assigns `tabindex`. The probe now uses F6 and Tab to reach the sidebar toolbar button, Arrow Right to focus Back, and Enter to navigate. This passed with browser Nova on and off. The same check passed with Firefox's bidi pseudo-locale, using Arrow Left and verifying RTL direction, and with `ui.useAccessibilityTheme` enabled and verified `(prefers-contrast)`. Firefox release `browser/themes/shared/toolbarbuttons.css` applies the focus outline to the icon rather than the button. The Back icon computed a 2px solid outline in those samples. The temporary minimal-CSS control is `/tmp/tf-vanilla-focus-probe.py` and may disappear. The test covers one Linux keyboard path, not painted focus or contrast, actual forced colors, real RTL locales, other toolbar buttons, or installed delivery paths.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-navbar-options-live.py`.

## Private-window app-menu icon

Firefox release `browser/base/content/appmenu-viewcache.inc.xhtml` defines `#appMenu-new-private-window-button2`, and `browser/themes/shared/jar.inc.mn` registers `chrome://browser/skin/privateBrowsing.svg`. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, `probe-panel-colors.py` found the app-menu item with nonzero geometry and the expected computed icon URL in both browser Nova states and both sampled document color preferences. A separate image load in the same browser successfully decoded the resource at 16×16 pixels. A follow-up activated that menu item by pointer and by Arrow Down plus Enter in disposable non-private profiles. Firefox reported a new private window for each activation, Nova state, and sampled document color preference. The probe verified that no private window existed before activation. ## Horizontal tabs and window controls

Firefox release `browser/base/content/titlebar-items.inc.xhtml` defines `.titlebar-buttonbox-container`. `chrome/tabs.css` applies `--tf-display-window-controls` to that selector and `--tf-display-horizontal-tabs` to `#TabsToolbar`. The README documents `block` for horizontal tabs, and the Nix `tabs.horizontal.enable` option generates `block`.

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-window-controls.py` compares vanilla Firefox with textfox profiles using `block` or `flex` for horizontal tabs. On Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` under a headed 1280×800 Sway headless compositor, with vertical tabs disabled and browser Nova on or off, the selected tab and close button had no vertical intersection with `block`. The close button appeared on a separate row below the tab. Vanilla and the textfox `flex` intervention placed both on the same row. The `block` and `flex` profiles both requested visible window controls. The same distinction appeared with Firefox's bidi pseudo-locale and verified RTL direction. This is a verified geometry difference in those configurations; it does not establish painted appearance, hit testing, multiple tabs, real RTL locales, other desktop environments, or other platforms. No CSS fix was applied. V4 and V5 already cover configuration and layout; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol.

The probe requires a headed Firefox session with a custom titlebar and nonzero close-button geometry. Run `"$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-window-controls.py` on such a display. Pass `--rtl` to use the bidi pseudo-locale. Its output includes measured rectangles and vertical intersection. A headless Firefox run lacked `customtitlebar` and could not validate this case. The temporary Sway launcher is `/tmp/tf-sway-window-test.py` and may disappear.

## Private-window indicator visibility

Firefox release `browser/base/content/private-browsing-indicator.inc.xhtml` defines labeled and button indicators. Release `browser/themes/shared/browser-shared.css` chooses their placement using `#navigator-toolbox[tabs-hidden]`. Textfox's default `--tf-display-horizontal-tabs: none` hides `#TabsToolbar` through CSS without setting Firefox's `tabs-hidden` attribute.

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-private-indicator.py` compared disposable private windows on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` in headless mode and under a headed Sway headless compositor. With `sidebar.verticalTabs: false`, vanilla displayed an indicator in `#TabsToolbar`. With textfox's default hidden horizontal tabs, the tab-toolbar indicator had zero geometry and the navbar indicator computed `display: none`; no sampled indicator had visible geometry. This occurred with browser Nova on and off. With `sidebar.verticalTabs: true`, Firefox set `tabs-hidden` and the textfox navbar indicator had nonzero geometry in both states. In headless runs, a temporary `tabs-hidden` attribute intervention made the navbar indicator acquire nonzero geometry in both Nova states. This supports the placement mismatch as the cause, but changing Firefox's state attribute is not a verified fix and may affect other UI. The probe verifies the window is private; this finding concerns the visible indicator, not proof that private browsing itself is disabled. It checks geometry and computed visibility, not screenshots, accessibility-tree exposure, every configuration, or other platforms. The probe reports the missing indicator rather than treating that observation as a passing support check. No CSS fix was applied. V5 and V6 cover the visibility and real-behavior requirement; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-private-indicator.py`. It includes vanilla and vertical-tab positive controls.

The opened menu was a `panel-list` with no `.context-menu` match or ancestor. In textfox profiles configured with a 7px border and 3px rounding, the menu computed a 1px border, 8px or 24px rounding, and a shadow. The non-default properties were verified on the document root. These are computed-style observations, not proof of painted output, menu item activation, contrast, or all shortcut configurations. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-topsite-menu.py`. Probe-only feed and widget overrides may make network requests.

## Split-panel overflow and clipping

Firefox release `browser/themes/shared/tabbrowser/content-area.css` sets `overflow: clip` on inactive split panels to contain their dialogs, and clips `.browserContainer`. Textfox's `chrome/browser.css` overrides both to `overflow: visible !important`. `test-split-view-live.py` now records the computed overflow and tests a disposable synthetic element positioned outside the inactive panel. An inside-panel hit-test control must work before the outside result is accepted.

On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, vanilla inactive panels and their browser containers computed `clip` in both browser Nova states. Textfox computed `visible` for both. Across the sampled bidi and contrast-preference combinations, the synthetic element was not hit outside vanilla panels. In textfox profiles it was hit outside the inactive panel with browser Nova on, but not with Nova off. The inside control was hit in all samples. This establishes a computed overflow difference and a synthetic hit-testing difference, not an actual dialog escape or a security exploit. Stacking and other theme rules also differ, so the fixture alone does not isolate overflow as the sole cause. Real dialogs, painted clipping, user interaction, actual forced colors, real RTL locales, and other platforms remain unverified. V5 and V6 cover layout and real interaction; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. No CSS fix was applied.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-split-view-live.py`. The test validates fixture placement and the inside control; it reports outside hits without asserting a support verdict.

## Inactive split-panel alert over selected content

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-split-dialog.py` opens a real `alert()` from a local data page in the inactive split panel. It verifies the prompt text in Firefox's `commonDialog.xhtml`, confirms the other panel remains selected, and checks hit testing both inside the alert and at a point within the selected browser's content rectangle. The probe resizes a disposable window to 720×600 to make the alert wider than its panel.

On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the alert's bounding box extended into the selected content area in vanilla and textfox profiles. At the sampled point, vanilla hit testing did not reach the alert, while textfox hit testing reached its dialog browser, with browser Nova both on and off. The inside-alert control succeeded in all profiles. A temporary profile-only intervention replacing both textfox `overflow: visible !important` declarations in `chrome/browser.css` with `overflow: clip !important` made the sampled selected-content point stop hitting the alert in both states. This supports the overflow override as the cause of the observed hit-test difference. The intervention is not a verified fix: it may affect other layout and interaction requirements.

A follow-up dispatched a pointer click at the sampled point. The selected local data page recorded the pointer event in vanilla and clip-control profiles. In textfox profiles, the inactive alert's document recorded it instead. This occurred with browser Nova on and off in both headless Firefox and headed Firefox on a 720×600 Sway headless compositor. The headed content viewport was 716×572 CSS pixels. Each case verified the alert message, inactive-tab state, selected-content geometry, and an inside-alert hit control. The fixture uses only disposable profiles and local data pages.

This establishes pointer interception by a real inactive-panel alert in those samples, not proof of a painted overlap, an exploit, or behavior on all desktops. It covers one viewport range, alert, platform, and panel order. Painted clipping, other dialog types, RTL, real desktop environments, and delivery paths remain unverified. The probe reports the difference rather than passing it as a support verdict. V5 and V6 cover layout and real behavior; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. No production CSS fix was applied.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-split-dialog.py`. The clip control modifies only a disposable copied profile.

## URL-bar result background and selection token

Firefox release `browser/themes/shared/urlbar/view-proton.css` and `view-nova.css` use `--urlbarview-background-color-selected` on selected rows. A release-source search found no use of `--urlbarView-highlight-background`, which textfox sets in `chrome/overwrites.css`. The release tree moves independently of the tested build.

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-urlbar-colors.py` inserts two local bookmark fixtures, disables search suggestions, selects a result with Arrow Down, and compares vanilla and textfox profiles. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, in both browser Nova states, textfox's `.urlbarView` computed transparent and its `.urlbarView-background` child computed white despite either a dark or light configured `--tf-bg`. The selected row computed Firefox's blue selection background. Changing the old highlight property on the selected row did not change that background; changing `--urlbarview-background-color-selected` did. These are computed-style and controlled-intervention observations, not proof of painted output or contrast across themes.

A headed run on a 1280×800 Sway headless compositor repeated the same comparison. In that environment the background child computed dark gray for vanilla and both configured textfox colors, while the old and current selection-token interventions behaved as in headless mode. A separate temporary override changed only the background child to the configured color. In headless Firefox, the dark configuration left unselected row text black. In the headed Sway run, the light configuration left unselected row text white. These computed values demonstrate why applying the background alone is not a verified fix. V4 and V5 cover configuration and contrast; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. No production CSS fix was applied.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-urlbar-colors.py`. The probe reports the mismatch rather than treating it as a passing support check.

## Context-menu icon source candidates

`check-context-icon-source.py` compares simple ID candidates from icon rules nested under a context containing `#contentAreaContextMenu` against a supplied Firefox markup file. Against tagged Firefox 159.0 [`browser-context.inc.xhtml`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-context.inc.xhtml), it extracted 89 distinct candidate IDs from 95 nested rule records. The file contained 83 IDs; six were absent from that file: two extension-generated menu IDs, `context-keywordfield`, `context-pdfjs-selectall`, `context-pocket`, and `context-savelinktopocket`. No escaped selectors were skipped in this subset. This checks text presence in one included markup file, not full selector matching, conditional visibility, dynamic or extension-provided nodes, or removal from Firefox. The containing `main-popupset.inc.xhtml` includes this file; searching that parent file alone missed every candidate. The utility uses `tinycss2` 1.4.0; `test-context-icon-source.py` checks a minimal positive and negative markup fixture. Fetch the tagged file locally, then run `PYTHONPATH=<tinycss2-target> python3 tools/firefox-chrome/check-context-icon-source.py <browser-context.inc.xhtml>`.

## Context icon candidates in a real link menu

`probe-context-icon.py` now records IDs and computed visibility/geometry for menu and menuitem descendants when a link context menu opens. `check-context-icon-source.py --live-jsonl <probe-output>` joins those results to the 89 simple icon-ID candidates from the tagged source comparison. In all four headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` browser Nova and icon-preference combinations, 83 candidate IDs existed in that live menu tree and 11 had non-hidden, visible, nonzero-geometry menu elements. The same six candidates absent from the tagged markup comparison were absent in these profiles. The probe also passed its `--pdf` variant after instrumentation.

All 11 visible candidate elements computed `background-image: none` with the icon preference both off and on in every sample. The comparison reports each visible candidate's computed background image; this does not rule out icons supplied by other properties or descendants. These counts check IDs, not complete nested CSS selectors, icon painting, or every context. Hidden elements can become relevant in other page, PDF, extension, or menu states; the two extension IDs are not expected without their extensions. The comparator has a synthetic positive/hidden/absent fixture. Capture the probe's JSONL, then run `PYTHONPATH=<tinycss2-target> python3 tools/firefox-chrome/check-context-icon-source.py <tagged-browser-context.inc.xhtml> --live-jsonl <probe-output>`.

## Context icon candidates across page and input menus

`probe-context-icon.py` now right-clicks a non-link paragraph and an editable input on its disposable local page, in addition to the existing link fixture. It requires the expected Save Page or Select All item to be visible, records the menu tree, and closes each menu with Escape. In all four headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` Nova and icon-preference combinations, the source comparator found 83 of the 89 simple candidate IDs present in each menu. Nine candidates had visible geometry in the page menu and eight in the input menu; all computed `background-image: none` with the preference either off or on. Combined with the link and PDF editor fixtures, 31 distinct candidate IDs had visible geometry in the sampled states. The count includes disabled items, checks IDs rather than complete selectors, and does not rule out icons supplied by other properties or descendants.

A follow-up adds a loaded local data-URL image and a real DOM text selection, then opens their context menus. Across the same four Nova/preference profiles, seven candidate IDs had visible geometry in each added state, all with `background-image: none`. The union across link, page, input, image, selected text, and PDF editor fixtures is 38 distinct visible candidate IDs. Counts include disabled items and do not prove complete selector matches or painted icons. The input fixture contains only disposable test text, and the page, input, image, and selection probes do not activate menu commands. Media, other image or selection types, extension, and other context states remain incomplete; the PDF Select All spelling mismatch is tracked separately. The comparator accepts `--context page`, `input`, `image`, and `selection`; run the probe with `--pdf` to include all six contexts. Run the commands in the context-icon comparison section above.

## Context icon candidates on video

With `--media`, `probe-context-icon.py` generates a local one-second WebM fixture using `ffmpeg` with `libvpx` and `libvorbis`, embeds it as a data URL, waits for decoded video dimensions, and right-clicks the paused video. It clears and verifies the prior text selection so the video menu is not mixed with selection commands. Across both browser Nova states and both icon-preference values on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, 12 simple icon-ID candidates had visible geometry in the video menu, including disabled items; each computed `background-image: none`. Together with the six earlier contexts, 48 distinct candidates had visible geometry in these sampled states.

An initial follow-up started a muted, looping video programmatically. The current probe generates a silent audio track, then uses pointer actions on the real context menu to activate Play, Mute, Unmute, and Pause in sequence. With `--keyboard-media`, it instead reaches each item with Arrow Down and activates it with Enter. Both modes passed in all four profiles, waiting for and verifying the corresponding playback and muted states after each command; the menu closes after each activation. Twelve candidates had visible geometry in each sampled media state and computed `background-image: none`; the union across the earlier eight sampled contexts remains 50 distinct candidates. The first exploratory video sample retained selected text from the preceding fixture, so its mixed menu was discarded. A further audio-only WebM fixture uses a silent track and exercises Play, Mute, Unmute, and Pause through the same pointer and keyboard paths. Both modes reached the expected audio states in all four profiles. The paused and playing/muted audio menus each exposed eight candidate IDs with visible geometry; all computed `background-image: none`. Including audio, the sampled context union is 53 distinct candidates. This does not test audible output, full-screen states, other keyboard navigation paths, or painted icons. `--media` requires `ffmpeg` with the named encoders; it does not fetch the media from the network. Run `probe-context-icon.py --pdf --media`, then use `check-context-icon-source.py <tagged-browser-context.inc.xhtml> --live-jsonl <output> --context video` with `tinycss2` 1.4.0 available.
