`probe-context-icon.py --pdf` now records menu and menuitem descendants while the real PDF editor context menu is open. The source comparator accepts `--context pdf` to join that fixture to the same 89 simple icon-ID candidates. Across both browser Nova states and both icon-preference values on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, 83 candidates existed in the menu tree and 14 had non-hidden, visible, nonzero geometry; all 14 computed `background-image: none`. This count includes disabled items. The current `#context-pdfjs-select-all` is not among those candidates because textfox spells it differently; the dedicated check below covers it. These are ID and computed-background observations, not complete selector matches or proof that no icon is painted through other properties. Other PDF editor states, multiple annotations, and platforms remain open. Use `probe-context-icon.py --pdf` and pass its JSONL to `check-context-icon-source.py <tagged-browser-context.inc.xhtml> --live-jsonl <output> --context pdf` with `tinycss2` 1.4.0 available.

## PDF select-all context-menu selector

Tagged Firefox 159.0 [`browser-context.inc.xhtml`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/browser-context.inc.xhtml) defines `#context-pdfjs-select-all`; `chrome/icons.css` targets `#context-pdfjs-selectall` without the second hyphen. In all four headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` Nova and icon-preference combinations, `probe-context-icon.py` found zero legacy IDs and one current ID in the real content menu. The current item was hidden in this link-context fixture. A temporary user-origin `-moz-pref(...)` rule targeting the current ID changed its computed background only with the preference enabled; the diagnostic image is not a production fix. A follow-up `--pdf` fixture generates a local PDF with `ps2pdf`, adds a free-text annotation through Firefox's PDF editor, and opens its real context menu. In all four Nova and icon-preference combinations, the current Select All item was enabled with nonzero geometry and `background-image: none`; the legacy ID matched zero elements. A temporary modern-query rule targeting the current ID applied a diagnostic image only when the preference was enabled. Pointer activation dispatched the item's command, closed the menu, and left the single editor selected. This is not a multi-editor Select All test or proof of painted icon visibility. An initial WebDriver element click dispatched the command but left the menu open; the probe now uses a pointer action, and existing V6 covers real interaction evidence. The separate legacy-media-query gap also prevents the existing conditional rule from applying. V5 and V6 cover UI behavior; a §B entry remains subject to spec-protocol approval. No production CSS changed. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-context-icon.py --pdf`; this option requires `ps2pdf`.

## Conditional extension toolbar icon

`probe-extension-icon.py` installs a locally generated temporary WebExtension into each verified disposable profile. The fixture uses the ID targeted by textfox's uBlock toolbar selector but is not uBlock and does not load its code. The probe places its browser-action widget in the navbar through Firefox's customization API and checks the real `.webextension-browser-action[data-extensionid] .toolbarbutton-icon` target. In both browser Nova states on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the icon retained the fixture extension's image whether `shyfox.enable.ext.mono.toolbar.icons` was false or true; the legacy media query remained false. A temporary user-origin rule using `-moz-pref(...)` and the same target applied textfox's `ublock.svg` only when the preference was true, both with normal priority and with `!important` as a control.

The temporary extension now also registers a link context-menu item whose generated ID matches textfox's uBlock context selector. In the same four profiles, a real link context menu exposed that item with nonzero geometry, but its computed background image remained `none` with the context-icon preference both off and on. A temporary user-origin modern-query rule using the same item selector applied `ublock.svg` only with the preference enabled; Escape closed the menu. This verifies one extension toolbar selector and one extension context selector in a synthetic extension fixture, not real uBlock behavior, icon painting, menu activation, other extension selectors, or installed delivery paths. The first fixture run confused Firefox's toolbaritem widget ID with its inner browser-action button ID; the probe now verifies their actual relationship. Existing V4–V6 cover configuration and UI behavior; the §B media-query proposal remains pending approval. No production CSS changed. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-extension-icon.py`.

## Conditional link context-menu icon

`probe-context-icon.py` right-clicks a local `data:` page link in a disposable profile and inspects Firefox's actual `#context-openlinkprivate` item. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the item had nonzero geometry in both Nova states. With `shyfox.enable.context.menu.icons` false or true, its computed background image remained `none`. The legacy media query `(-moz-bool-pref: "shyfox.enable.context.menu.icons")` did not match either preference value; `-moz-pref("shyfox.enable.context.menu.icons")` matched only true. A temporary user-origin stylesheet using the latter query and the same icon declaration applied Firefox's private-browsing SVG only when the preference was true. Escape closed the menu in all four samples.

The tagged Firefox 159.0 [CSS parsing reftest](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/layout/reftests/css-parsing/moz-bool-pref.css) also uses `@media -moz-pref(...)`. These results establish a conditional-selector gap for this one built-in menu item, not painted rendering or all icon rules. The same probe independently reads the two extension-icon preferences and compares their media queries with each preference false and true. In both Nova states, both legacy queries remained false and both modern queries followed their effective preference values. This covers the media conditions, not matching or rendering extension icons. Three media rules in `chrome/icons.css` and one in `chrome/navbar.css` use the legacy syntax; their individual UI effects remain partly unverified. The candidate intervention is not a production fix, and disabled icons still require a contrast and rendering review. V4, V5, and V6 already cover configuration and real UI behavior; a §B entry is pending spec-protocol approval. No production CSS changed. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-context-icon.py`.

## App-menu shadow selector

`probe-panel-colors.py` now counts light-DOM matches for `#appMenu-popup #shadow-root > *` and checks for an element with id `shadow-root` inside the open panel's shadow root. ## Overflow menu scrollbox

Tagged Firefox 159.0 [`menupopup.js`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/toolkit/content/widgets/menupopup.js) creates `.menupopup-arrowscrollbox` inside the menupopup shadow root. `probe-menu-active.py --overflow` creates an 80-item disposable XUL menu, verifies the scrollbox has more content than its viewport, and sends End after keyboard focus reaches the first item. In both browser Nova states, headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` scrolled the menu in vanilla and textfox profiles. The shadow scrollbox computed a 5px solid top border in textfox profiles configured with `--tf-border-width: 5px`, versus 1px in vanilla. This demonstrates the themed declaration's effective computed value in the fixture despite the shadow boundary; it does not prove the border is painted or that all built-in overflow menus behave identically. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-menu-active.py --overflow`.

## Disabled menu-item hover

`probe-menu-active.py` now includes a disabled item in its synthetic XUL menu and moves the pointer over it. In both browser Nova states on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the item had nonzero geometry, matched `:hover`, and acquired `_moz-menuactive="true"`. Its computed background was light gray in vanilla and transparent in textfox; the themed `--tab-hover-background-color` computed empty. Temporarily setting that variable on the disabled item to system `Highlight` changed its computed background. The probe does not activate the disabled item. This is a synthetic menu and computed-style observation, not proof of painted visibility, disabled-item keyboard behavior, real built-in menu states, contrast, or other platforms. V5 and V6 cover UI feedback and behavior; no production fix was applied. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-menu-active.py`.

## Keyboard-active synthetic menu item

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-menu-active.py` creates a two-item XUL menupopup in disposable Firefox profiles, opens it, sends Arrow Down, checks an enabled `menuitem[_moz-menuactive="true"]` with nonzero geometry, and sends Escape. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, both browser Nova states produced a blue computed background for the active item in vanilla and a transparent background in textfox. In those themed samples `--tab-selected-bgcolor`, `--toolbar-field-color`, and `--toolbar-bgcolor` computed empty. The menu rule consumes the first property; the textfox root definition derives it from the latter two. Setting only `--tab-selected-bgcolor` on the active item to system `Highlight` in the disposable fixture changed its computed background to blue. This intervention supports a variable dependency in that fixture, not a safe production fix or a contrast claim.

With `--builtin`, the probe instead opens Firefox's `#toolbar-context-menu`, defined in tagged [`main-popupset.inc.xhtml`](https://github.com/mozilla-firefox/firefox/blob/9a2dc53c369c7ae5172018b35988e1d2ffeff402/browser/base/content/main-popupset.inc.xhtml). Arrow Down reached its `Menu Bar` item in all four vanilla/textfox and Nova combinations. The computed backgrounds and themed intervention matched the synthetic results. With `--tf-rounding: 7px`, active menu items in both fixtures computed a 7px top-left radius in both Nova states; vanilla computed 2px with Nova off and 4px with Nova on. The probe opens the built-in menu programmatically, not through a normal context click, and does not establish painted focus visibility. Disabled items, pointer activation, actual OS forced colors, other themes, and other platforms remain untested. V5 already requires visible focus and contrast; whether the real menu has a painted defect remains unverified, so no new spec invariant or product change was made. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-menu-active.py`, adding `--builtin` for the built-in menu.

## Findbar no-match feedback

Firefox release `toolkit/content/widgets/findbar.js` defines `.findbar-textbox` and `.findbar-find-status`. Release `toolkit/themes/shared/findbar.css` changes the field background for `[status="notfound"]`; textfox's `chrome/findbar.css` sets the field background from `--tf-bg` with `!important`. `test-findbar-live.py` now searches for absent text, waits for the status label, then searches for matching text again. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the full Nova, bidi pseudo-locale, and contrast-preference matrix passed: the not-found status text had nonzero visible geometry, the next matching search selected its text, and Escape closed the bar.

In those samples, vanilla changed the field's computed background on a miss while textfox retained its matched-state background. A temporary copied-profile intervention removing only textfox's field background declaration restored Firefox's computed no-match background in both browser Nova states. That intervention also stopped applying the configured field background, so it is not a verified fix. The text status remained present in the sampled profiles. Computed styles and geometry do not prove painted readability, contrast, or behavior on other platforms. V4 and V5 cover configuration and readable feedback; a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. No production CSS fix was applied.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-findbar-live.py`. The comparison reports the field-background difference; it does not assert that difference as a passing support check. The temporary intervention script `/tmp/tf-findbar-background-control.py` may disappear.

`test-findbar-live.py` marks the open findbar before Escape and inspects the same element afterward. ## New-tab shortcut hover selectors

Firefox release `browser/extensions/newtab/content-src/components/TopSites/_TopSites.scss` styles the tile when `.top-site-inner:hover` or a keyboard-focused descendant matches; Nova adds scoped expansion and control-reveal rules in `styles/nova/_TopSites.scss`. Textfox sets the tile background to `--newtab-element-hover-color` only for `.tile:hover`, while also overriding Firefox's `--newtab-background-color-secondary` and hover token on the body.

`probe-newtab-shortcut-hover.py` moves a pointer first over a shortcut title, then its tile, checking the actual `:hover` states. In sampled Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` profiles, all eight combinations of browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and theme passed in headless and headed Sway runs. Themed tile-hover computed backgrounds matched textfox's hover token. With the pointer over the title instead, the same tile computed a different background; vanilla samples computed the same tile color for both positions. This is a selector and state difference, not proof of a visual defect: icons or other layers may cover the background, and no painted comparison or contrast assessment was performed. No production CSS was changed.

An initial probe failure exposed a moving fixture: the tile center moved from y=393.75 to y=253 after coordinates were captured, so the pointer missed. The probe now re-targets only when target geometry moves and reports that count; it still fails if the expected hover state is not reached. This is an inventory-fixture issue rather than evidence of a product invariant gap; §B recording requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. The sample does not cover drag-and-drop, keyboard focus styling, actual forced colors, wallpaper variants, or other platforms. Disposable feeds may access the network.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-newtab-shortcut-hover.py`. The headed Sway launcher used for this sample was `/tmp/tf-sway-shortcut-hover.py` and may disappear.

## New-tab search handoff shadow boundary

Firefox release `browser/components/search/content/contentSearchHandoffUI.mjs` renders `.search-handoff-button` inside `content-search-handoff-ui`'s shadow root. Its `contentSearchHandoffUI.css` uses `--content-search-handoff-ui-border-width`, `--content-search-handoff-ui-border-radius`, and separate focus and background variables; `browser/extensions/newtab/content-src/components/Search/_Search.scss` assigns several of those variables on the host. Textfox's `.search-wrapper .search-handoff-button` and `.search-wrapper input` selectors in `chrome/content/newtab.css` do not cross the shadow boundary.

`probe-newtab-search-handoff.py` explicitly enables handoff in disposable profiles and tests all browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and vanilla/textfox combinations on Linux Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, both headless and headed Sway. The themed profiles set `--tf-border-width: 7px` and `--tf-rounding: 3px`. The shortcut tile provided a positive control at 7px/3px, but the shadow search button remained 1px wide with 8px, 12px, or 9999px radius according to the sampled preference combination. It retained Firefox's computed shadow and transparent unfocused border. The button existed in the shadow root, not as a light-DOM descendant. Setting only Firefox's two border variables on the host changed the sampled shadow button's computed width and radius to 7px/3px. After removing the intervention and verifying the URL bar was not already focused, clicking the host focused it in every sample.

This is a verified configuration and selector gap, not a tested fix. The temporary variable intervention left the unfocused border transparent and did not assess focus contrast, hover, or painted appearance. Actual forced colors, keyboard activation, and delivery paths remain untested. The non-handoff input mode and a gated `moz-urlbar` sample are described below. §V4 and §V5 cover customization and UI; recording a §B entry requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. No production CSS was changed.

The first probe incorrectly treated the reflected attribute `nonhandoff="false"` as enabled merely because it existed. Later, a click encountered a stale host, another found the host absent just before a diagnostic read found it again, and a separate cleanup lookup found no host. The probe checks the attribute value, samples and removes its temporary intervention in one script execution, and retries only missing or stale click targets with a deadline, reporting retry counts. These fixture observations do not establish a new product invariant. Disposable feeds may access the network.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-newtab-search-handoff.py`. The headed Sway launcher `/tmp/tf-sway-search-handoff.py` may disappear.

## New-tab non-handoff search input

Firefox release `browser/components/search/SearchUIUtils.sys.mjs` selects non-handoff mode from `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.improvesearch.handoffToAwesomebar`; `contentSearchHandoffUI.mjs` renders `#newtab-search-text` in the same shadow root when the reflected `nonhandoff` value is `true`. `contentSearchHandoffUI.css` styles that input with the same host border-width and radius variables as the handoff button. Textfox's `.search-wrapper input` selector cannot reach it through the shadow boundary.

`probe-newtab-search-input.py` sets handoff off and checks effective preferences in disposable profiles. On Linux Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, all eight combinations of browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and vanilla/textfox passed in headless and headed Sway runs. In themed profiles, configured `--tf-border-width: 7px` and `--tf-rounding: 3px` reached the shortcut tile but not the shadow input: its computed border width remained 1px and its radius was 8px, 12px, or 9999px according to the sampled preference combination. Temporarily setting the two Firefox host variables changed the input to 7px/3px; its unfocused border stayed transparent. After removing the intervention, the probe clicked the host, confirmed input focus, typed `textfoxprobe` into the shadow input, and confirmed that the URL bar did not gain focus. It did not submit a search. Search suggestions were disabled in disposable profiles; feeds may still make network requests.

This extends the recorded shadow-boundary configuration gap to non-handoff mode. It does not verify painted appearance, focus visibility or contrast, search submission, suggestions, keyboard traversal, actual forced colors, or delivery paths. No production CSS was changed. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-newtab-search-input.py`. The headed Sway launcher `/tmp/tf-sway-search-input.py` may disappear.

## Gated new-tab `moz-urlbar`

Firefox release `UrlbarNewTabComponentRegistrant.sys.mjs` registers the in-page `moz-urlbar` in place of handoff search when `UrlbarPrefs.get('newtabFeatureGate')` is true. `UrlbarPrefs.sys.mjs` also requires `browser.nova.enabled`; its feature gate and A/B variants have `browser.urlbar.newtab.*` fallback preferences. `UrlbarInputBase.mjs` appends the `.urlbar-background` and `.urlbar-input` in light DOM, unlike the shadow-root handoff component. Firefox's `browser/themes/shared/urlbar.css` styles the in-page background independently from the input.

`probe-newtab-moz-urlbar.py` sets the gate preference true and, by default, variants A/B false only in disposable profiles, then checks both the preference and effective `UrlbarPrefs` values. On Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, the eight browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and vanilla/textfox combinations completed in both headless and headed Sway samples. With browser Nova off, the effective gate was false and the handoff component appeared despite the true gate preference. With browser Nova on, the effective gate was true and the in-page `moz-urlbar` appeared for both new-tab Nova states.

In the enabled samples, the light-DOM input matched textfox's `.search-wrapper input` selector. With configured `--tf-border-width: 7px` and `--tf-rounding: 3px`, its computed border and radius were 7px/3px; the separate `.urlbar-background` remained 1px/26px. Vanilla input computed 0px/0px while its background also computed 1px/26px. The shortcut tile was a 7px/3px positive control for the custom configuration. The probe clicked the input and typed without submitting, but the separate computed borders do not establish painted appearance, overlap, or contrast. Treat this layer mismatch as an inventory finding requiring visual assessment, not a verified fix or a claim of broken styling.

During probe development, the input lost focus and a marked element was replaced while new-tab initialized, including in vanilla. Another failed run typed the test string twice because the probe itself had duplicate keyboard actions; that duplicate was removed. The probe now records replacement retries and retries interaction only when its marked input disappears or is replaced, with a deadline; it fails when the same input does not receive focus or the expected text. This bounded probe cannot establish whether user input is preserved during initialization. §V5 and §V6 cover functional and visual behavior; §B recording requires approval under the spec backprop protocol. Search suggestions were disabled in the disposable profile, but feeds may make network requests.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-newtab-moz-urlbar.py`. The headed Sway launcher `/tmp/tf-sway-newtab-urlbar.py` may disappear. A/B layout samples are described below. Search submission, keyboard traversal, painted contrast, forced colors, RTL, and delivery paths remain open. No production CSS or user preferences were changed.

## Gated new-tab `moz-urlbar` A/B variants

Firefox release `UrlbarNewTabComponentRegistrant.sys.mjs` gives variant B precedence when both variant preferences are true. `browser/themes/shared/urlbar.css` positions variant B's `.searchmode-switcher` on a row above the input; variant A keeps it in the input row and can display a search-engine wordmark. The `--variant a`, `--variant b`, and `--variant both` options in `probe-newtab-moz-urlbar.py` set only disposable fallback preferences and check both preference and effective `UrlbarPrefs` values.

On Linux Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, each variant option completed eight browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and vanilla/textfox combinations in both headless and headed Sway samples. Browser Nova off kept the gate and effective variants false and showed the handoff component. Browser Nova on produced the requested variant attribute; when both were requested, only `variant-b` appeared. The sampled B switcher geometry was above the input. In themed profiles the input still computed a 7px border while the separate background computed 1px, as in the default-variant sample. The wordmark was not always populated at initial measurement time; a later contrast-cycle sample waits for Google branding as described below.

For active A, B, and both-preference samples, the probe clicked the search-engine switcher, observed its menu open, sent Escape, observed the menu close while the switcher remained in place, then clicked and typed into the search input without submitting. During probe development an A switcher was replaced after its click in a vanilla profile, leaving no menu open. The probe marks the switcher, reports replacement retries, and retries only when that marked element disappears or is replaced; it fails if the same element's menu does not open or close. This does not establish user-input preservation while the page initializes. §V5 and §V6 cover behavior; §B recording requires approval under the spec backprop protocol.

Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-newtab-moz-urlbar.py --variant a`, replacing `a` with `b` or `both`. The headed launcher `/tmp/tf-sway-newtab-urlbar-variant.py` may disappear. These samples do not verify painted layout, occlusion, menu-item actions, keyboard traversal, search submission, actual forced colors, real RTL, responsive widths, other engines, or delivery paths. Search suggestions were disabled; disposable feeds may make network requests. No production CSS or user preferences changed.

## New-tab search-engine wordmark and contrast

Firefox release `browser/components/urlbar/content/SearchModeSwitcher.mjs` uses `(prefers-contrast)` to replace recognized search-engine wordmarks with an icon and name for variants A and B. It listens for changes to that media query. Firefox's `browser_engineWordmark.js` tests the same transition using accessibility-theme and document-color preferences.

`probe-newtab-moz-urlbar.py --variant a --contrast-cycle` and `--variant b --contrast-cycle` perform a dynamic cycle in disposable profiles. On Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, each option covered all browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and vanilla/textfox combinations in headless and headed Sway samples. Browser Nova off kept the in-page component disabled. For every enabled sample, the probe waited for the default Google wordmark with nonzero geometry and an engine-wordmark background image, enabled `ui.useAccessibilityTheme=1` and `browser.display.document_color_use=2`, then restored both to their initial disposable values. On the same `moz-urlbar` host, content media queries reported both `(prefers-contrast)` and `(forced-colors: active)` during the enabled state; the wordmark attribute and background image disappeared, an icon source was present, and the `Google` title computed `display: block` with nonzero geometry. The wordmark and normal media-query state returned after restoration.

These are DOM, media-query, and computed-geometry observations, not proof of painted readability, color contrast, icon visibility, accessible name, or real OS high-contrast integration. The probe does not exercise the switcher menu while forced colors are active; earlier menu tests ran before this cycle. Only the profile's default Google engine was sampled. Search suggestions were disabled; disposable feeds may make network requests. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/probe-newtab-moz-urlbar.py --variant a --contrast-cycle`, replacing `a` with `b` for the other sample. The headed launcher `/tmp/tf-sway-newtab-contrast.py` may disappear. No production CSS or user preferences changed.

Keep Nova disabled and retain the README warning.

Firefox release `browser/components/urlbar/tests/browser-newtab/browser_searchModeSwitcher.js` tests Shift+Tab from the input to `.searchmode-switcher`, followed by Enter, with `browser.urlbar.searchModeSwitcher.skipTabStop=true`. The disposable-profile probe now checks the effective preference and repeats this interaction. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, variants `a`, `b`, and `both` each covered all eight browser Nova, new-tab Nova, and vanilla/textfox combinations. When browser Nova was enabled, Shift+Tab focused the switcher and Enter opened its menu in all 12 samples. Browser Nova off used the handoff component instead. At the focus sample, `:focus-visible` was false, outline style was `none`, and box shadow was `none` in both vanilla and textfox. These computed values do not establish painted focus visibility or a textfox-specific defect. The probe does not verify Escape closure for this keyboard interaction, menu item activation, real OS accessibility settings, or other platforms. It clicks the input first, so it does not establish keyboard-only traversal from outside the component. Disposable feeds may make network requests.

An immediate menu-state read failed once during probe development. Waiting up to 15 seconds for the popover to open produced the results above; the probe still fails if it never opens. This was a probe timing failure, not an established Firefox defect. `chrome/content/newtab.css` now contains the former probe-only `--experiment-wrap` candidate as production rules inside the `about:home`/`about:newtab` document block: `grid-template-rows: minmax(var(--row-height), auto) var(--row-height)` on `.container.nova-enabled`, `grid-row: 1` on `.sidebar-inline-start`, `height: auto` and `max-inline-size: calc(100cqi - var(--side-col-width) - 3 * var(--space-xlarge))` on `.wordmark`, and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on its `::before`. The logo still paints inside its own start sidebar (1386 changed pixels). With the rules stashed, the same baseline run paints 18 logo pixels in the search crop; fixed paints 0.

Scroll fixture at requested 900px window: search remained sticky at top 22.5px with identical `wrapperPosition: sticky`, scroll offset and click focus in fixed and stashed baseline; at requested 800px the wrapper is static in that fixture in both.

## T4 B2 legacy preference query port

All four legacy `@media (-moz-bool-pref: ...)` conditions in the checkout (three in `chrome/icons.css`, one gating the `sidebar.revamp` navbar rules in `chrome/navbar.css`) were converted to `@media -moz-pref(...)`, the form Firefox's own tagged CSS-parsing reftest uses. This is the §B B2 fix: on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` the legacy form never matched, so all 164 conditional icon rules and the navbar revamp gate were dead regardless of `shyfox.*` and `sidebar.revamp` values. No selectors, declarations, assets, or `--tf-*` variables changed, so V4 option sync is untouched. `tools/firefox-chrome/check-rule-ledger.py` and its fixture route the new media text; the record counts and icon groups are unchanged (382 records: 241 icon, 141 component).

`tools/firefox-chrome/test-pref-icons-live.py` is the new V4/V6 regression check. On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, in eight disposable textfox profiles covering both browser Nova states with the icon preference and `sidebar.revamp` both ways (each effective preference read back), production rules followed the effective values: with the icon preference true, `#context-openlink` computed the configured relative asset `file:///.../chrome/icons/window.svg` and `#context-openlinkprivate` computed `chrome://browser/skin/privateBrowsing.svg`; with it false, both computed `none`. The `sidebar.revamp` gate flipped `.titlebar-spacer` computed width between `0px` and `40px` as configured. The link context menu is opened before measurement; items in a closed popup return empty computed values in these samples, which was the first version's harness failure, not a cascade result.

Adjacent regressions on the same Firefox baseline: `test-rule-ledger.py`, `test-inventory-css.py`, and `check-rule-ledger.py` passed; `probe-sidebar-header.py`, `test-navbar-options-live.py`, and `probe-context-icon.py` passed; `test-sidebar-live.py` failed once with the previously recorded intermittent empty splitter focus outline, then passed on immediate rerun with `focusVisible: true` in all cases; that flake predates this slice. `git diff --check` clean.

This activates rules that were silently inactive on 159: shyfox context/extension icons now actually render when their preferences are on, and the navbar spacer/`#nav-bar::before` rules now apply whenever `sidebar.revamp` is true (the Firefox 159 default). Painted appearance of these revived rules, extension-icon states, real pointer interaction, forced colors, and delivery paths remain open under T4/T8. §B B2 status correction belongs to the spec skill under the write policy; the code fix landed with its regression test. T4 remains in progress. Run `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-pref-icons-live.py`.

## T4 stale context and bookmarks icon selectors

Four icon selectors recorded as verified mismatches in T2 were ported in `chrome/icons.css`, no other declarations touched:
- `#context-pdfjs-selectall` → `#context-pdfjs-select-all` (tagged Firefox markup defines only the current ID).
- `#PersonalToolbar[collapsed="true"]` → `#PersonalToolbar[collapsed]` (Firefox exposes an empty `collapsed` attribute; the literal branch never matched).
- The five `#contentAreaContextMenu > #context-back|forward|reload|stop|bookmarkpage` direct-child rules became descendant rules (`& #context-*`). Tagged markup nests them under `#context-navigation` outside macOS; descendant form matches both nested Linux samples and any flat macOS structure.
- Bare type selector `context-openlinkincontainertab` → `#context-openlinkincontainertab` (T2's container-tab probe showed the real target is a `menuitem` with that ID and the original selector failed while an ID control applied).

Verification on headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`: `probe-context-icon.py` passed all four Nova/icon-preference combinations; with the preference on, nested `#context-back` computed the production `chrome://browser/skin/back.svg` while `matches('#contentAreaContextMenu > #context-back')` stayed false for every nav item, proving the rules now apply through the nesting; with it off, `none`. The probe's navigation control marker asset was switched to `privateBrowsing.svg` because the production back icon would otherwise collide with the marker. `probe-bookmarks-menu-icons.py` passed after updating its drift guard and controls to `[collapsed]` with the natural empty attribute: the new selector matches the naturally collapsed toolbar, and in the collapsed preference-on sample the item computed the production `eye-show.svg` — the temporary two-ID control can no longer outrank the three-ID production rule, and the probe expectation now asserts that production outcome. The first bookmarks run failed opening the submenu (transient; passed on immediate rerun). Static: `test-rule-ledger.py`, `test-inventory-css.py`, `check-rule-ledger.py` pass; record count unchanged at 382; `git diff --check` clean.

Bounds: computed styles in sampled Linux profiles, not painted output; macOS flat-menu behavior is by selector shape, not tested; painted icon rendering and pointer interaction over these items remain open. T4 remains in progress.

## T4 collapsed vertical pinned-tabs container (3-agent consensus)

The pinned-tabs container question ran the operator-mandated three-agent consensus (`ccs work` account with Opus, `grok -p`, `pi -p` on `openrouter/deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`; question and answers kept at `/tmp/tf-consensus-q.md`, `/tmp/tf-consensus-{ccs,grok,pi}.txt`, which may disappear). All three independently corrected the pending fixture: an empty `expanded=""` attribute is still the EXPANDED state, so `:not([expanded])` never matched the earlier single-pin sample; the old evidence said nothing about the rule. Consensus direction: do not add `#pinned-tabs-container` to the width rule unless a true collapsed measurement shows the container narrower than the strip; otherwise delete the dead container branch.

`tools/firefox-chrome/probe-pinned-tabs-container.py` implements the agreed fixture: launcher collapsed via `updateUIState({launcherExpanded:false})`, hard requirement that `#tabbrowser-tabs` lacks the `expanded` attribute before measurement, then 0/1/3 pinned tabs (`gBrowser.pinTab`) in both browser Nova states, themed and vanilla (`TF_VANILLA=1`). On headless Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` all six themed and six vanilla cases passed and matched each other: the stale `#vertical-pinned-tabs-container` is absent with and without pins; `#pinned-tabs-container` equals the strip width (51px Nova off, 47px Nova on) with 1 and 3 pins in both Nova states and in vanilla without any textfox CSS; empty (0-pin) container width is 0 in both. Pinned `.tab-stack` elements span the strip and none overflows its right edge. The textfox rule shape `#tabbrowser-tabs[orient="vertical"]:not([expanded]) #pinned-tabs-container` matches the live element, but Firefox's own layout already produces the same width, so per consensus the dead container branch was deleted and only `.tab-stack` remains in `chrome/sidebar.css`.

Post-change verification on the same baseline: probe matrix green; `test-sidebar-tools-live.py` green; `test-rule-ledger.py`, `test-inventory-css.py`, `check-rule-ledger.py` pass (382 records unchanged; the edit merged selector-list items, not a record count). Bounds: computed geometry in sampled Linux profiles, no painted output, no drag or keyboard interaction on pinned tabs, other Firefox versions unproven. A harness bug found en route: `gBrowser.content` is falsy on 159, so vertical-tabs readiness waits must not use it. T4 remains in progress.

## T4 widget menu keyboard flake root-caused

`test-newtab-widget-menu.py` failed at `expected first widget menu item is not focused` identically in T3's baseline samples. A disposable DEBUG-TMP run showed the Nova new-tab widgets popover (`panel-list` with `popover=auto`) does open on Enter and the first Arrow Down does focus the `Hide widgets` menuitem — but the focus move is asynchronous; the probe read `document.activeElement` immediately and still saw the trigger button (classic timer menu is synchronous, which is why only Nova-state cases failed). The test now waits up to five seconds for the expected menuitem with read-back before asserting. Full matrix passed: all 64 browser-Nova, new-tab-Nova, themed, content forced-colors, bidi pseudo-locale, and reduced-motion combinations activated `Hide widgets` (new-tab Nova) or notification toggle (classic) with a computed focus outline on the same Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. This is a harness race fix, not a production CSS change; painted focus, real RTL locales, and real OS forced colors remain T8 gates. T4 remains in progress.
