## T5 delivery protocol closed

The remaining gap was Home Manager activation, previously blocked by "no Home Manager source offline" and "`packages.default` cannot be built here". Both blocks were environmental and both are gone: network access is available, and `pkgs.callPackage ./nix/pkgs/default.nix { }` against nixpkgs-unstable now builds `/nix/store/4y65hc8cdx5i02xd7l5lsk25053bp2fh-textfox-git`, so the earlier `cp -r` permission failure does not reproduce in a `stdenv` build. §C was amended accordingly.

Sources for this run: nixpkgs `https://channels.nixos.org/nixos-unstable/nixexprs.tar.xz` as `/nix/store/impz97ds0jz39b93p35g3gy8rad2lsrs-source` (26.11pre1114946.177d0540942f) and home-manager `master` as `/nix/store/8sp96qrpjlzhmfvhr94z8k0yq03mszk8-source`. Home Manager `release-25.05` with that nixpkgs is not usable: the fixtures ask for `home.stateVersion = "26.05"` and the release branch's enum stops at `25.05`, so evaluation fails before any activation. `test-home-manager-vm.sh` now states the pairing rule. `check-home-manager.nix` also built its real home-manager-generation against those sources.

`sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-home-manager-vm.sh /nix/store/impz97ds0jz39b93p35g3gy8rad2lsrs-source /nix/store/8sp96qrpjlzhmfvhr94z8k0yq03mszk8-source` built a disposable NixOS VM, required `home-manager-probe.service` to succeed before starting Firefox, and passed: guest-local Marionette reported Firefox 159.0.1 build `20261102144711`, profile `/home/probe/.config/mozilla/firefox/test`, computed navbar border `7px` from `textfox.config.border.width`, `browser.nova.enabled` effective false with default true and a user value, `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.nova.enabled` effective true, default true, no user value. That is real activation writing both the recursive `chrome/` tree and the generated `chrome/config.css` inside a home-manager-managed profile, and the configured value reaching a live Firefox. The border check is discriminating, not a hardcoded pass: an earlier 3px fixture reported FAIL.

Re-run on the same tree: `node tools/firefox-chrome/test-nixos-update.js` (19 scenarios), `check-cascade-order.py` and `test-cascade-order.py`, `check-home-manager-inputs.py`, `test-manual-install-files.py` (3 cases), `test-manual-install-live.py` on host Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` (both optional-`user.js` cases, 3px border and monospace `about:preferences`), and `NP_RUNTIME=bwrap nix-portable nix run nixpkgs#nixfmt-tree -- --ci` (0 changed). The NixOS protocol VM from the previous session is unchanged by this slice and still green.

Bounds: a standalone (non-NixOS) Home Manager host, non-unstable Home Manager source pairings, Darwin, upgrades of an existing Home Manager-managed profile, and painted appearance remain unverified; the VM proves activation and computed style, not visual layout. Nova remains disabled and support remains unclaimed. T5 is closed; T6 is next.

## T6 configuration wiring closed

Three gaps sat under "existing configuration options still work". Home Manager never applied `configPrefs`, so `textfox.config.icons.*` set `configCss` and nothing else. `textfox.config.tabs.vertical.enable` had no read site at all: `--tf-display-vertical-tabs` was hard-coded to `flex`, so a user asking for no vertical strip kept it. And the manual CSS built `--tf-display-horizontal-tabs` with `block`/`none` while `configCss` used the same pair, but nothing compared option → computed style for non-default values.

Fixes: `nix/modules/home-manager.nix` now writes `textfox.configPrefs` through `programs.firefox.profiles.<name>.extraConfig` (NixOS already passed them to `wrapTextfox`), and `chrome/tabs.css` consumes `--tf-display-vertical-tabs` instead of a literal `flex`. No `--tf-*` variable changed name or default, so the V4 sync list is untouched.

New checks, each proven to fail before it passed:

- `check-option-wiring.py` (with `check-option-wiring.nix`) evaluates the options module once per option, flips booleans and writes sentinel strings, then requires each to change `configCss` or `configPrefs`. 24 options: 21 through the CSS, the 3 icon options through the preference set. With the `--tf-display-vertical-tabs` line deleted it reports `tabs.vertical.enable: flipping the option changes nothing`.
- `check-option-parity.py` writes a hand-written `config.css` for six non-default options, generates the same configuration through the module, and compares every computed `--tf-*` value plus the rendered new-tab logo in two disposable Firefox profiles. Mapping `horizontal.enable` to `flex` makes it report `{'--tf-display-horizontal-tabs': {'manual': 'block', 'nix': 'flex'}}`.
- `test-vertical-tabs-option-live.py` covers the strip itself: 6 cases over `display: flex`/`none` × vertical tabs off/on × browser Nova off/on on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. `box#vertical-tabs` computed the requested display, with one tab present.
- `check-home-manager-options.py` builds textfox through Home Manager and requires each option to move its file; reported `configuredVerticalTabs: none`.
- `test-home-manager-vm.sh` now also sets font size, hidden titles, hidden vertical tabs and a menu-icon preference. The VM probe reads every `--tf-*` variable from the shipped `defaults.css`, fails on any empty one, and compares expected values and preferences. The run passed with all 20 variables resolved, `--tf-font-size: 17px`, `--tf-display-vertical-tabs: none`, and `shyfox.enable.context.menu.icons: true` / `shyfox.enable.ext.mono.toolbar.icons: false` effective from the generated `user.js` — that is Home Manager activation writing prefs, not just `configCss`.

A stale-harness find: `test-nixos-generated-live.py` had not run since the T5 autoconfig rewrite and could not. It looked for the retired `textfoxChrome` variable, expected `/nix/store` icon URLs instead of the profile-local asset directory, and read a store path it never realised. It now realises `storeDir`, reads through `nix store cat`, requires the generated CSS to name no store paths, and checks each referenced icon. It also sets `textfox.config.tabs.vertical.enable = false` and reports `--tf-display-vertical-tabs: none` with `box#vertical-tabs` at `display: none`, zero box, in a live Firefox; the existing 11px border, sentinel, icon and new-tab assertions still pass. No new §V invariant was added: V9 already requires checks for supported delivery paths, and these checks now execute; recording the T5/T6 harness lapse as a §B entry needs operator approval under the backprop protocol.

Re-run at this checkpoint: `check-nixos-eval.nix`, `check-nixos-build.nix` via the generated-live test, `check-cascade-order.py`, `test-cascade-order.py`, `test-nixos-update.js` (19), `test-manual-install-files.py` (3), `test-manual-install-live.py`, `test-rule-ledger.py`, `test-inventory-css.py`, `check-rule-ledger.py`, `check-home-manager-inputs.py`, `test-home-manager-live.py`, `test-nix-logo-live.py`, `test-pref-icons-live.py` (8), `test-sidebar-tools-live.py`, `test-navbar-options-live.py`, `test-bookmarks-toolbar-live.py`, `test-about-font-live.py`, `test-firefox-live.sh`, `test-newtab-live.sh`, `np nixfmt-tree --ci` (0 changed), `git diff --check`. `test-nova-layout-live.py` exited 2 (inconclusive, as recorded: observations, no failure). `test-sidebar-live.py`, split view, private window, findbar and the context-menu probes were not re-run in this checkpoint.

Bounds: computed styles and preferences in sampled Linux profiles and one disposable Home Manager VM, no painted output; standalone Home Manager hosts, Darwin, other Firefox versions and the NixOS protocol VM for this slice remain unverified. The VM proofs need the paired nixpkgs/home-manager sources above. Nova remains disabled and support remains unclaimed. T6 is closed; T7 is next.

## T7 preference migration

Textfox keeps Nova off through one assignment per delivery path: `browser.nova.enabled` false, set as a user value. Every path sets it again on every start, so a value the profile already persisted is overridden rather than trusted. Removing the assignment would not migrate anything (V3). `check-pref-parity.py` requires `user.js` and `wrapTextfox.nix` to name the same four preferences with the same values. It refuses missing, extra, differing or duplicate entries and a flipped Nova gate; `test-pref-parity.py` covers six fixtures.

How each path reaches an existing profile:

- Manual: `tf-install.sh` merges textfox's preferences into the profile's `user.js` inside a marker block instead of replacing the file (§B5). The user's own lines stay, a stale block is replaced, an unclosed marker is refused, and the old file is backed up. `tf-uninstall.sh` removes only the block, or the file when it holds nothing else. `test-manual-install-files.py` (9 cases) and `test-manual-install-live.py` cover a profile whose `prefs.js` enables Nova and holds a sentinel preference, plus the user's own `user.js`. After install, both launches report Nova false as a user value and the user's preferences still apply. The first shutdown persisted Nova false in `prefs.js`.
- Home Manager: owns `user.js` wholesale through `extraConfig`. A user's own preferences therefore live in `prefs.js`. `test-home-manager-vm.sh` seeds the profile before activation (`prefs.js` with Nova true and a sentinel, plus a user file under `chrome/`). Activation met no collision. Firefox 159.0.1 build `20261102144711` reported Nova effective false, default true, user value set. The sentinel and the user file were kept. After a graceful stop, `prefs.js` held both the sentinel and Nova false.
- NixOS: the autoconfig `pref()` sets a user value on every start and writes no `user.js`. `test-nixos-prefs-live.py` runs the wrapped binary under nix-portable. `test-nixos-prefs-vm.sh` boots the module in a disposable VM. Both seed `prefs.js` with Nova true and a sentinel, and both observe Nova false as a user value, the sentinel kept, the configured border applied, no `user.js`, and Nova false plus the sentinel persisted after shutdown. The VM also keeps an unrelated profile file unchanged.

A suspected NixOS Marionette regression was a test artifact. On a first install the autoconfig copies the CSS and restarts Firefox (`eForceQuit | eRestart`), and Marionette is not carried into the restarted process. Under nix-portable's bwrap runtime the restarted child also dies once the launched process exits. Separately, `marionette_driver`'s `quit()` refuses a Firefox it did not launch, and the old session swallowed that error, so Firefox kept the profile lock. The live test now runs one install launch, keeps the sandbox alive and stops the lock owner with SIGTERM; the VM relies on systemd `Restart=always`. With the CSS already current, Firefox does not restart and Marionette listens. Real restart behavior stays covered by `test-nixos-update-vm.sh`.

The same sweep found `test-nixos-autoconfig-vm.sh` permanently red: it waited for the pre-T5 `<hash>-textfox-chrome` marker and never supplied `defaults.css` to the T6 variable probe. It now waits for `textfox-installed.json`. The vanilla call passes `--no-textfox-css`, which requires that no `--tf-*` variable is defined.

Retiring the assignment later (once Nova is supported) is not a deletion. Each path has already persisted `browser.nova.enabled` false as a user value in `prefs.js`, and Firefox keeps that after the assignment disappears. Retirement therefore needs a one-shot clear of that user value: `Services.prefs.clearUserPref` behind a textfox-owned "cleared" marker preference in the autoconfig, and the equivalent for manual and Home Manager users, with an existing-profile regression showing Nova returns to the Firefox default. That design is unimplemented and unverified.

Open gaps: `tf-uninstall.sh` still removes the whole `chrome/` directory (`tf-uninstall.sh:34`), including the user's `config.css` (V2). Textfox does not set `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.nova.enabled`, so new-tab Nova stays at Firefox's default (true) on every path; whether that is intended belongs to T8's support decision. A standalone Home Manager host, Darwin, and other Firefox builds remain unverified.

Re-run at this checkpoint: `test-pref-parity.py`, `check-pref-parity.py`, `test-manual-install-files.py` (9), `test-manual-install-live.py`, `test-nixos-prefs-live.py`, `test-home-manager-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-prefs-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-autoconfig-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-update.js` (19), `test-rule-ledger.py`, `check-rule-ledger.py` (tinycss2 venv), `check-home-manager-inputs.py`, `check-home-manager-options.py`, `nixfmt-tree --ci`, and `git diff --check`. Sources: nixpkgs `/nix/store/wa9kz0gcy4wgakdv28p7i4jc7xkcbbky-source` (rev `1d060086`), home-manager `/nix/store/8sp96qrpjlzhmfvhr94z8k0yq03mszk8-source`. `test-nixos-update-vm.sh` was not re-run, because nothing it covers changed.

## T8 support gate

Every runnable check passed on the tree at `290cce8`, on host Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` (headless, plus a headless Sway compositor for the painted findbar check) and in disposable NixOS VMs on Firefox 159.0.1 build `20261102144711` (nixpkgs `/nix/store/wa9kz0gcy4wgakdv28p7i4jc7xkcbbky-source`, rev `1d060086`; home-manager `/nix/store/8sp96qrpjlzhmfvhr94z8k0yq03mszk8-source`):

- Static and fixture: rule ledger, CSS and variable inventories, expanded selectors, selector evidence and atoms, unobserved dispositions, about-page index, Firefox variable source, cascade order (check and fixtures), context-icon source fixture, pref parity (check and 6 fixtures), `test-manual-install-files.py` (14), `test-nixos-update.js` (19), `check-nixos-update-exhaustive.js` depth 4 (no new violations), `check-home-manager-inputs.py`, `nixfmt-tree --ci` (0 changed), `git diff --check`. The tinycss2 checks need `~/.cache/textfox-css-venv`; the live venv lacks it.
- Host live: every `test-*-live.py` and `test-*-live.sh` check, `check-config-defaults.py`, the places-icon pair, `test-newtab-widget-menu.py` (64-combination matrix with content forced colors, bidi pseudo-locale and reduced motion), `test-vertical-tabs-option-live.py` (10), `test-pref-icons-live.py` (8). `test-nova-layout-live.py` exits 2 (inconclusive), as recorded since T3.
- Accessibility and paint: `probe-nova-logo-paint.py` default, widths 700/1000/1600, bidi long logo at 18px, empty and centered logo, scroll, reduced motion and content forced colors; `test-findbar-paint-sway.py`; `probe-context-icon.py`, `probe-sidebar-header.py`, `probe-bookmarks-menu-icons.py`, `probe-pinned-tabs-container.py`.
- Nix: `check-option-wiring.py`, `check-option-parity.py`, `check-home-manager-options.py`, `test-home-manager-live.py`, `test-nix-logo-live.py`, `test-nixos-generated-live.py` in all five variants (default, forced colors, wallpaper, classic new tab, browser Nova), `test-nixos-prefs-live.py`, and the four VM suites `test-home-manager-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-autoconfig-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-prefs-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-update-vm.sh`.

Fixed during the gate:

- §B6: `tf-uninstall.sh` deleted the whole `chrome/` directory, including the user's `config.css`. It now removes only shipped files and restores a backup beside the user's files.
- §B7 (independent review): T6's `display: … !important` on `box#vertical-tabs` showed an empty bordered strip with horizontal tabs under `sidebar.revamp`. The override now applies only with `[visible]`; four horizontal-tab cases were red before the fix.
- §B8 (independent review): uninstall used process substitution, which macOS `sh` rejects; a `config.css` kept in the checkout's `chrome/` removed the profile's; reversed `user.js` markers made the strip drop user lines.
- Review: four autoconfig helpers were defined twice in `wrapTextfox.nix` (the second copy deleted; protocol checks unchanged). README now says the Nix modules own the three `shyfox.*` preferences and documents the NixOS refusal and `textfox.force-adopt` recovery.
- Harness: `test-nova-no-search-live.py` failed about one run in three in vanilla and themed profiles alike. Firefox centers the Nova logo only without a stories feed or content-area widget, and both depend on runtime region and Nimbus state. The fixture now pins them off; 10 consecutive runs passed.

Support decision: not claimed. The README warning and `browser.nova.enabled` false stay on every path (V1). Gates that remain open:

- Platforms: no macOS or Windows run. The macOS flat context menu is supported by selector shape only, and the manual scripts were not run under macOS `sh`.
- Accessibility: painted contrast for arbitrary `--tf-*` colors, the app-menu foreground pairing (both addressed by T11 for computed colors; see "T11 readable text" below), and Windows high contrast (the only platform whose default `browser.display.document_color_use` of 0 lets the OS force content colors). The GTK HighContrast detection step itself is unexercised; see below.
- Delivery (V2): the autoconfig protocol's known findings F1 and F2 leave some interrupted NixOS installs refused until `textfox.force-adopt` is set; F3 can reuse a partly removed asset directory; F4 and F5 are cosmetic or conservative. Review finding: `tf-uninstall.sh` offers only the newest `chrome-*.bak`, which after a reinstall holds the previous textfox files, not the user's pre-textfox files (nothing is lost; the older backup stays). Review finding: a damaged icon inside the current `textfox-assets-<hash>` directory is not repaired until the icon set changes.
- Unverified environments: a standalone Home Manager host, Darwin, other Firefox builds, and whether `Cu.reportError` refusals reach the Browser Console.
- New-tab Nova (`browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.nova.enabled`) is left at Firefox's default (true) on every path and is covered by the new-tab matrix above. Browser-chrome Nova stays off.

Retiring the Nova assignment later still needs the one-shot clear designed in T7.

### T8 follow-up: real RTL locale and Linux high contrast

Browser chrome never matches `(forced-colors: active)` on Firefox 159, on any platform: `layout/style/PreferenceSheet.cpp` says chrome documents "always use system colors, not stand-ins, not forced". The earlier "real OS forced colors in chrome" gate therefore cannot exist. OS high contrast reaches chrome as `(prefers-contrast: more)`. Outside Windows, `browser.display.document_color_use` defaults to `1` (`StaticPrefList.yaml`), so on Linux the only way content colors are forced is the user's "Override colors: Always" (`2`). That is the path the existing content forced-colors checks already set, so they test the real Linux behavior, not an emulation of it.

`test-high-contrast-live.py` runs headed on headless Sway, because the headless look-and-feel paints black on white and its ratios mean nothing. It covers high contrast off, and on together with "Override colors: Always", for browser Nova off/on, vanilla and textfox. It requires `prefers-contrast` to follow the setting, chrome never forced, new-tab forced exactly when overridden, and 4.5:1 for URL bar, selected tab, navigation bar, bookmarks bar and new-tab text. 8/8 passed on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. Textfox chrome text measured 13.52:1 where vanilla's was 7.54:1. A low-contrast `config.css` on `#nav-bar` failed at 1.31:1. Bound: `GTK_THEME=HighContrast` and a `settings.ini` theme name both applied the theme's colors under headless Sway, but Firefox did not report `UseAccessibilityTheme`, and no settings portal was present to supply `contrast=1`. The check therefore sets `ui.useAccessibilityTheme`, the override for that same look-and-feel value. Everything downstream of detection is real; the detection itself is not.

`test-rtl-locale-live.py` installs Mozilla's signed Hebrew language pack for 159.0 (`he.xpi`, verified against `SHA256SUMS`) and requests `he`. It covers browser Nova off/on, horizontal and vertical tabs, vanilla and textfox. It checks that chrome and new tab are right-to-left, toolbar items are mirrored around the URL bar and stay inside the navigation bar, textfox keeps vanilla's mirrored order, vertical tabs sit on the right, chrome stays inside the window, and the new-tab shortcut title stays inside its tile. 8/8 passed. Forcing `#nav-bar` to `ltr` in `config.css` failed it. The first version compared the back button, which textfox hides by default; that was a harness error, not a theme defect. Bound: geometry in headless profiles only; painted RTL output, Arabic (with its shaping) and RTL text inside the URL bar are not checked.

## T10 backup choice and asset repair

Closes the two T8 review findings left open under "Delivery (V2)".

- Manual uninstall offered the newest `chrome-*.bak`, which after a reinstall holds textfox's previous files. `tf-install.sh` now judges each file it moves aside on its own. A file is textfox's when it is byte-identical to the checkout's copy. `userChrome.css` and `userContent.css` are textfox's when they carry the textfox logo, which has been in both sheets since 2024-10, before any install script. Any other name is textfox's when the profile shows a textfox install: a managed-prefs marker in `user.js`, or the logo in `chrome/userChrome.css`. textfox's files go to `chrome-textfox-<stamp>.bak` and the user's files go to `chrome-pre-textfox-<stamp>.bak`. A `_N` suffix keeps two installs in one second apart. `tf-uninstall.sh` offers the newest pre-textfox backup, using C collation. Failing that, it offers the oldest legacy `chrome-<stamp>.bak` and warns that the backup may hold textfox's files. It never offers a `chrome-textfox-*` backup. Residual: a textfox sheet whose logo the user removed counts as the user's. Regression: `test-manual-install-files.py` (19 cases; the 5 new cases fail against the previous scripts). Independent review found 3 risks: per-profile classification, the logo as the only signal, and locale-dependent sort. All three are fixed above.
- NixOS: T9's F3 fix already checks the current `textfox-assets-<hash>` directory against the manifest hashes, so the next start rebuilds a damaged icon. This needed no code change. Regression: `test-nixos-update.js` adds "a damaged icon in the current asset directory is repaired on the next start" (22 scenarios). The test fails when `assetsIntact` checks presence instead of hashes, so it can fail. Icons arrive read-only from the store, so the test has to chmod the file before damaging it.

## T11 readable text for arbitrary `--tf-*` colors

Closes the app-menu foreground pairing and the arbitrary-`--tf-bg` part of the T8 accessibility gate, for computed colors on Linux.

- New variable `--tf-fg`, default `contrast-color(var(--tf-bg))`. Firefox 159 supports `contrast-color()` in chrome; it returns black or white, whichever contrasts more with the background. Every text color that sits on `--tf-bg` now takes `--tf-fg`. Users who want the theme's tinted text back set `--tf-fg: var(--lwt-text-color)` (built-in themes set no `--lwt-*` on Firefox 159, so this only helps static themes). textfox never sets `--lwt-text-color` itself, because `--tf-fg` may refer to it. Nix: `textfox.config.font.color`, wired into `configCss`. `--tf-accent` is not guarded: it stays the user's choice.
- Firefox 159 renamed the toolbar tokens. textfox's `--toolbar-color` and `--toolbar-field-color` were dead (T2 already listed them as unused). The overrides now use `--toolbar-text-color`, `--toolbar-field-text-color[-focus]`, `--toolbox-text-color[-inactive]` and `--sidebar-text-color`. The selected tab redeclares `--tab-text-color-selected` on `.tabbrowser-tab`, so it is overridden there; without it, selected-tab text failed on built-in themes with classic UI.
- `body { background-color: var(--tf-bg) }` in `userChrome.css` is a user-origin normal rule and lost to Firefox's `body { background-color: var(--toolbox-background-color) }`. Text around the navigation bar therefore sat on the theme's toolbox color, not `--tf-bg`, while the section titles sat on `--tf-bg`. `--toolbox-background-color[-inactive]` now take `--tf-bg`, so everything behind chrome text is the configured background. Visible change: with the default configuration on a built-in theme, the toolbox now shows `-moz-dialog` (the `--tf-bg` default) instead of the header-bar color, matching the title patches that already used it. An inactive window keeps the active color.
- Panels: Firefox 159 paints popups with `--panel-background-color` and `--panel-text-color`; textfox set only the old `--panel-background`. Both are now set from `--tf-bg` and `--tf-fg`, so the app menu and context menus follow `--tf-bg` with readable text.
- Selected and hovered tab backgrounds mix 8% and 4% of `--tf-fg` into `--tf-bg`. The old mix referenced the dead `--toolbar-field-color`, so it was invalid and the selected tab showed no highlight on Firefox 159. Menu-item hover uses the same tokens.

Regression: `test-readable-colors-live.py`, headed on headless Sway. Themes: Firefox's built-in light and dark themes, and static light and dark themes with tinted text (`#4c4f69` on `#eff1f5`, `#b4befe` on `#1e1e2e`). Each runs with the default colors and with a `--tf-bg` of the opposite lightness, in both browser Nova states (16 cases). It measures the navigation bar, URL bar input and results, selected and unselected tab labels, the main, navbar, tabs and find titles, findbar input and checkbox, app-menu items and a context-menu item. Each must reach 4.5:1 against its nearest opaque background (walking slots and shadow hosts; tab labels against `.tab-background`). A 17th case sets `--tf-fg: var(--lwt-text-color)` and requires the theme text to reach toolbar, app menu and context menu. Against the previous CSS the same test reported 69 failures. With default colors, the static dark theme drew black URL bar and findbar text on the dark toolbox (1.28:1), and the selected tab had no background at all: the old `--tab-selected-bgcolor` mixed two dead tokens, so it was invalid and computed transparent. After the change, 17/17 pass.

Bounds: computed colors on a real compositor, not painted pixels. `contrast-color()` guarantees about 4.5:1 only for an opaque `--tf-bg`; a translucent one is measured against whatever shows through. Accent-colored titles on hover are not checked. Sidebar panel documents (bookmarks, history) draw their own colors and are not covered. Linux only.


Verification on host Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, nixpkgs `/nix/store/wa9kz0gcy4wgakdv28p7i4jc7xkcbbky-source`, home-manager `/nix/store/8sp96qrpjlzhmfvhr94z8k0yq03mszk8-source`:

- Passed: `test-readable-colors-live.py` (17), `test-high-contrast-live.py` (8), `probe-panel-colors.py`, `check-option-wiring.py` (25 options), `check-config-defaults.py`, `check-option-parity.py`, `check-home-manager-options.py`, `test-home-manager-live.py`, `test-nixos-generated-live.py` (default and `--browser-nova`), every other host `test-*-live.py` and `test-*-live.sh` except the two below, the rule ledger, inventories, cascade order, expanded selectors, unobserved dispositions, `nixfmt --check`, `git diff --check`.
- `test-nova-layout-live.py` exits 2 (inconclusive), as since T3.
- `test-rtl-locale-live.py` could not connect to Marionette, three times. The tree at HEAD (before T11) fails the same way, so this is an environment problem on this host, not a T11 regression. It is unverified for T11.
- Subtraction check: without the `.tabbrowser-tab` override, 2 selected-tab cases fail (built-in themes, classic UI).

## T12 Windows and macOS matrix

Closes the "Platforms" line and the Windows high-contrast part of the T8 accessibility gate. Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` on both hosts, the same build as the Linux baseline, verified against Mozilla's `SHA256SUMS`.

macOS 27.2.1 arm64 (M4 Max). Firefox ran from a disposable copy in `~/tf-t12`; the installed `/Applications/Firefox.app` (109.0.1) was not touched.

- Manual scripts under macOS `/bin/sh` (bash 3.2.57, POSIX mode on) with BSD userland: `TF_TEST_SHELL=sh test-manual-install-files.py` passes 19/19. The harness can fail: macOS `sh` rejects the pre-B8 `tf-uninstall.sh` at parse time (`syntax error near unexpected token '<'`), while `bash` accepts it. `test-manual-install-live.py` (`FIREFOX`, `TF_TEST_SHELL=sh`, headless) passes for a fresh and an existing profile.
- Flat context menu: `probe-context-icon.py` (headless, both Nova states, icon preference off and on). All five navigation items are direct children of `#contentAreaContextMenu`, as the tagged macOS markup says; there is no `#context-navigation`. textfox's icons compute when the preference is on and are `none` when it is off.
- Headed, that styling never shows. `probe-macos-native-menu.py` found `widget.macos.native-context-menus` true by default: macOS draws the page context menu itself, and the XUL `#context-back` has 0x0 geometry while its icon still computes. With the preference false, the XUL menu shows (219x25) with textfox's icon. Headless runs always keep the XUL popup, so no earlier check could see this. Recorded as §B B9 with invariant V10; T13 sets the preference.

Windows 11 Enterprise Evaluation 25H2 (build 26100) in a KVM guest on the Linux host: unattended install, local account with autologon, OpenSSH. Firefox needs the interactive desktop, so tests run as a scheduled task in that session.

- `test-high-contrast-windows.py` switches real OS high contrast with `SystemParametersInfo(SPI_SETHIGHCONTRAST)` ("High Contrast Black") and sets no stand-in preference: `ui.useAccessibilityTheme` and `browser.display.document_color_use` stay unset, and the test requires the effective `document_color_use` to be Windows' default `0`. 8/8 pass (high contrast off/on, browser Nova off/on, vanilla and textfox). With high contrast on, chrome matches `(prefers-contrast: more)` and never `(forced-colors: active)`; new-tab content is forced by the OS alone. textfox chrome text measured 16.29:1 (vanilla's selected tab 7.07:1); with it off, 21:1. Can fail: a `config.css` setting `#nav-bar` text to `Canvas` failed all 4 textfox cases at 1:1.
- Harness fix found on the way: on Windows, `terminate()` stops only the parent `firefox.exe`; its children keep the profile's cache open and the temporary profile cannot be removed. The test ends the process tree with `taskkill /T /F`.

Bounds: computed colors, not painted pixels; one contrast theme (High Contrast Black); Nova browser chrome is still unsupported; macOS was not run headed for anything but the native-menu probe; no Windows run of the manual scripts (they need a POSIX `sh`).

## T13 macOS native context menus off on every delivery path

Fixes §B B9. textfox now sets `widget.macos.native-context-menus` to false on every delivery path. Manual installs get it from the managed block in `user.js`. Home Manager reads the same packaged `user.js` through `extraConfig`. NixOS sets it as an autoconfig `pref()` in `wrapTextfox.nix`. On other platforms Firefox ignores the preference.

- `check-pref-parity.py` now requires the preference to be false in both artifacts. `test-pref-parity.py` adds two failure cases: set to true in both files, and missing from both. Both are refused.
- `test-nixos-update.js` expects the preference in the autoconfig pref list with value false. Without the `wrapTextfox.nix` line, that scenario fails.
- `probe-macos-native-menu.py` has a third case. It writes the shipped `user.js` and sets nothing else about native menus. The probe exits 1 unless the context menu is a XUL popup with a visible Back item that computes textfox's icon.

Headed on macOS 27.2.1 arm64 with Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` (`~/tf-t12` copy):

- Default profile: the preference is true, the menu is native, and `#context-back` is 0x0.
- Shipped `user.js`: the preference is false, `#context-back` is 219x25 with `back.svg`, and the probe prints `ok`.
- Subtraction: with the preference line removed from the Mac copy of `user.js`, the menu is native again (0x0) and the probe prints `not ok`.
- `TF_TEST_SHELL=sh test-manual-install-files.py`: 19/19.

Linux: pref parity (check and 8 fixtures), `test-nixos-update.js` (22), `check-nixos-update-exhaustive.js` (no new violations), `test-manual-install-files.py` (19), `nixfmt-tree --ci` (0 changed), `git diff --check`.

Bounds: only the page context menu's Back item was measured. Other context menus, painted pixels, and a headed run through a home-manager or NixOS install on macOS remain unverified. The NixOS module does not target Darwin.

## T14 Nova logo and search box proven from headed painted pixels

`test-nova-layout-live.py` no longer exits 2. It exits 0 on pass and 1 on fail, including any run whose instrument cannot be trusted. The estimated pseudo-element line boxes are gone (V8). The verdict now comes from painted pixels in a headed Firefox:

- The test starts a disposable 1280x800 headless Sway compositor (`WLR_BACKENDS=headless`, pixman renderer, no borders). It runs Firefox headed inside it and captures the compositor output with `grim`. It drops `MOZ_HEADLESS` from its environment and fails unless Firefox reports `isHeadless` false.
- Under Wayland, `mozInnerScreenX/Y` did not match the compositor (the search box painted about 9px left and 20px above the mapped box). The content origin is now measured: a fixed 20x20 marker at content (10, 10) is painted, and its changed-pixel box must be 24x24 (square plus outline).
- Logo and search box visibility: each is hidden in turn with `visibility: hidden`, so layout is kept. The changed pixels are the pixels that element painted. The logo must paint at least 100 pixels, and the search box must paint pixels whose box is centered inside its DOM box.
- Overlap: the count of logo-changed pixels inside the union of the search box's painted box and its DOM box must be zero.
- Controls: a repeated capture and a capture after restoring the styles must match the first capture exactly. Animations, transitions and the caret are frozen during capture. An injected fixture moves the logo onto the search box above it, and the test fails unless that overlap is detected.
- Wikipedia shortcut checks are unchanged (clipping, title collision, both self-tests). An unmeasured neighbor title is now a failure, not an inconclusive note. Wrapping is still only an observation.

Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, Linux, 1244x700 content viewport:

- HEAD: exit 0. The logo paints 3052 pixels and the search box 48032. 0 logo pixels fall inside the search region; the injected fixture puts 1447 there. Both control diffs are 0. A second run with `MOZ_HEADLESS=1` set in the caller also passes.
- Subtraction: with the T3 "contain the generated logo" rules removed from `chrome/content/newtab.css`, the test exits 1 with `295 logo pixels paint inside the search box`. The rules were restored afterward.

Run `"$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-nova-layout-live.py`. It needs `sway`, `grim` and ImageMagick. New-tab feeds are on, so it may make network requests.

Bounds: one viewport (1280x800 output), LTR, default font and logo, browser and new-tab Nova both on, Linux Wayland with software rendering. Responsive widths, the bidi pseudo-locale, other platforms, and the other surfaces belong to T15. Painted pixels prove where the logo paints, not that its text is legible.

## T15 painted-pixel regression for key surfaces

`test-surface-paint-live.py` checks textfox's key surfaces from painted pixels in a headed Firefox. It reuses the T14 harness: a disposable 1280x800 headless Sway compositor, Firefox headed inside it, and `grim` captures. It exits 0 or 1, never inconclusive.

- Surfaces: navbar, URL bar text, URL bar results, horizontal tabs, app menu, toolbar context menu, findbar, new-tab search box and first shortcut tile. Each runs in both browser Nova states.
- Each surface is hidden in turn with `visibility: hidden`, so layout is kept. The changed pixels are what it painted, and their bounding box is the surface on screen. A box much larger than the element fails the run as a wrong measurement.
- Chrome surfaces must show three things. The dominant color must be `--tf-bg`. A border of the surface's variable must show on the left, right and bottom edges: `--tf-border` for navbar, tabs and findbar, `--tf-accent` for URL results, app menu and context menu. The top edge is skipped because the section title sits there, and a border color indistinguishable from the background fails. A textfox-colored text target must paint at least 30 pixels at 4.5:1 against the painted background. The text target is hidden in its own capture: the navbar title, URL bar text, an unselected tab label, the findbar Highlight All checkbox, an unselected result title, the app menu's New Tab item, and the first context-menu item. A whole-surface count was too loose: the app menu's Sync banner uses Firefox's own colors and stayed readable under a broken config.
- New-tab surfaces must show a border of the resolved `--newtab-border-color` on the same three edges.
- Colors are resolved inside each surface. The default `--tf-border` is `color-mix(in srgb, currentColor 24%, transparent)`, so a value resolved at `:root` (black text) did not match what painted on the navbar (white text). Translucent colors are composited over `--tf-bg`.
- Cases: the shipped defaults, a sentinel `config.css` with distinct colors (`--config FILE` replaces it), and a built-in known-bad config (`--tf-fg: var(--tf-bg); --tf-border-width: 0px`). Every surface must fail the known-bad case, or the run fails.
- Controls: a repeated capture and a capture after restoring styles must match the base exactly. Animations, transitions and the caret are frozen, and `ui.prefersReducedMotion` is 1. Probe-only overrides keep the page static: startup page blank; new-tab widgets, weather, stories and sponsored shortcuts off. The Marionette `remotecontrol` attribute is removed, because its URL bar stripes are not textfox's paint.

First run at `9dd741b` (exit 1, both Nova states, defaults and sentinel):

- URL bar results painted Firefox's background (56,56,60) with no accent border. Firefox 159 draws the open results on a positioned joint `.urlbarView-background` (`browser/themes/shared/urlbar.css`): its own color, rounded corners, a shadow, and an outset past the view that covers `.urlbarView`'s border. textfox's `.urlbarView` background was a normal user rule, so Firefox's author rule beat it. T11 passed because it measured computed colors only.
- The new-tab search box had no textfox border. It is the handoff button inside `content-search-handoff-ui`'s shadow root, which this file already recorded as a selector gap that was never fixed.

Recorded as §B B10 with invariant V11. Fixes:

- `chrome/urlbar.css`: `.urlbarView-background` is `display: none !important`, and the `.urlbarView` background is `!important`. Results now paint `--tf-bg` in a flat box with the `--tf-accent` border.
- `chrome/content/newtab.css`: the host sets `--content-search-handoff-ui-border-width: var(--tf-border-width)`, `--content-search-handoff-ui-border-radius: var(--tf-rounding)`, `--content-search-handoff-ui-unfocused-border-color: var(--newtab-border-color)` and `--box-shadow-level-3: none`. The search box is now square with the same border as the shortcut tiles. Firefox's fake-focus border still uses its own variable.

After the fix, Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, Linux Wayland, software rendering:

- `test-surface-paint-live.py`: exit 0. 9 surfaces pass in both browser Nova states with the defaults and the sentinel config, and the built-in known-bad config fails every surface. Before the fix, the same test failed URL bar results and new-tab search in all four good cases.
- `--config` with a user file setting `--tf-accent: var(--tf-bg)`: exit 1. The URL results, app menu and context menu borders are reported as indistinguishable from the background, in both Nova states.
- Passed: `test-nova-layout-live.py`, `test-readable-colors-live.py` (17), `test-high-contrast-live.py`, `test-newtab-live.sh`, `git diff --check`.
- `test-urlbar-live.py` could not connect to Marionette, twice. A worktree at `9dd741b` (before T15) fails the same way, so this is a host problem, not a T15 regression. It is unverified for T15.

Run `"$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-surface-paint-live.py`. It needs `sway`, `grim` and ImageMagick, and takes about 3 minutes (6 Firefox launches). Set `TF_SURFACE_ARTIFACTS=<dir>` to keep captures and masks. New-tab feeds are on, so it may make network requests.

Bounds: one 1280x800 output, LTR, one platform (Linux Wayland, software rendering), new-tab Nova on (Firefox's default), handoff search mode only (not the gated in-page `moz-urlbar` or non-handoff input), horizontal tabs only (vertical tabs and the sidebar are not covered). The toolbar context menu stands in for the page context menu. Hover, focus rings and selected-row colors are not checked. Painted pixels show that colors and borders reach the screen and that text pixels reach 4.5:1, not that every label is legible.

`test-nova-layout-live.py` enables new-tab widgets. The world clock ticks each minute. Weather, the crossword, and the wallpaper arrive from the network. Two captures of one page can therefore differ, and the test treats that difference as an unstable capture. The first sweep failed the overlap fixture at 545 restored pixels and the real page at 785. A run whose captures straddle the minute failed the same way, 785 pixels on the control capture and on the restored capture. The freeze CSS now also sets `visibility: hidden` on `.nova-enabled :is(.sidebar-inline-end, .content-full-width)` and on its descendants. Layout stays. The ticking pixels do not. The same minute-straddle with that CSS passed, both diffs 0. A later run of the file in this tree exited 0 on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. The logo painted 3052 pixels, the search box 44304, and 0 logo pixels fell inside the search region. The injected overlap fixture put 1414 there. The test had been failing safe. No §V invariant failed, so no §B entry was added.

## T17 Linux standalone home-manager activation

The Linux half of T17 passed on this Omarchy host (Linux 7.2.5-3-omarchy, x86_64, non-NixOS). The Darwin half is the next section. Nova support stays unclaimed. T18 is still open.

The proof is Home Manager's real `activate` script, built impurely from `tools/firefox-chrome/check-home-manager-standalone.nix` and run by `tools/firefox-chrome/test-home-manager-standalone.sh`. It is a standalone home-manager activation into a disposable `HOME` under `/tmp`. It is separate from the NixOS-module VM in `test-home-manager-vm.sh`.

```
sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-home-manager-standalone.sh \
  /nix/store/wa9kz0gcy4wgakdv28p7i4jc7xkcbbky-source \
  /nix/store/8sp96qrpjlzhmfvhr94z8k0yq03mszk8-source
```

Exit 0. Paired sources are the T16 pair (nixpkgs rev `1d060086`, home-manager master). `home.stateVersion` is `"26.05"`. `home.username` is the invoking user and `home.homeDirectory` is the disposable home, because `activate` checks both. The profile name is `test`. Options: border `7px`, font `17px`, titles off, vertical tabs off, Firefox context icons on.

Passing generations:

- Seeded home: `/nix/store/jzzaph3m9psyyjl9yj5raz0wcqf3ny2x-home-manager-generation`
- Fresh home: `/nix/store/dcwihq4szcaxjrg2fax5j82cg48rli31-home-manager-generation`

The seeded home started with `chrome/user-notes.txt` (`kept by the user`) and a `prefs.js` sentinel `tf.test.sentinel=true`, plus `browser.nova.enabled` true and `browser.shell.checkDefaultBrowser` false. The first `activate` was killed while `nix-env --profile … --set` was held. The notes and the sentinel were still there. Two later activations of the same generation completed, and both still had the notes, the sentinel, and `--tf-border-width: 7px` in `config.css`. The fresh home had one activation, no notes file, and no sentinel.

Host Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` ran headless inside the same bwrap against each activated profile. The navbar border computed `7px`. `--tf-font-size` was `17px`, `--tf-display-vertical-tabs` was `none`, and `--tf-display-titles` was `none`. On the seeded profile, `browser.nova.enabled` was effective false, default true, user value present; `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.nova.enabled` was effective true with no user value; `browser.shell.checkDefaultBrowser` was effective false with a user value; `tf.test.sentinel` was effective true. After a graceful shutdown, seeded `prefs.js` still had the sentinel and `browser.nova.enabled` false. The fresh profile had the same Nova and new-tab result, persisted `browser.nova.enabled` false, and did not contain the sentinel.

Linked files, read through the nix-portable store because this host has no `/nix/store` at root: `chrome/icons/blank.svg` is present; `config.css` has the four configured variables; `user.js` has `user_pref("browser.nova.enabled", false);` and `user_pref("widget.macos.native-context-menus", false);`; the last two `@import`s in `userChrome.css` are `defaults.css` then `config.css`. The disposable home has no `Library` directory. Each completed activation logged `User systemd daemon not running. Skipping reload.` and did not log `Attempting to reload services`. The real `~/.nix-profile`, `~/.local/state/nix/profiles`, and `~/.config/mozilla` were unchanged.

Bounds. This host has no system Nix daemon and no `/nix/store` at `/`. Nix came from nix-portable with `NP_RUNTIME=bwrap`, and `nix-env` was the store's Nix 2.20.6 (`/nix/store/j823gnvpxh1fjibycmldrspkk3pgivwp-nix-2.20.6/bin/nix-env`). The interrupt is a test-only wrapper around that binary: a warm store finishes `activate` before a poll can catch it, so the wrapper holds `--set` and the test kills the process while it is held. Completed activations call the store binary with no wrapper. Firefox runs inside bwrap so the activation symlinks resolve; `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` and `NIX_LDFLAGS` from `nix shell` are unset first. File checks rewrite `/nix/store` links onto `~/.nix-portable`. The module copies `chrome/` except `userChrome.css`, and this worktree has an untracked `chrome/.omc`; the test does not assert that directory is absent. On Darwin, `nix/modules/home-manager.nix` appends `Profiles/` and Home Manager's Firefox module also sets `profilesPath` to `${configPath}/Profiles`. With `home.stateVersion = "26.05"` that module's `configPath` is `Library/Application Support/Firefox`, so the profile directory is `Library/Application Support/Firefox/Profiles/test`. The `26.11` override to `org.nixos.firefox` does not apply. The Darwin run of that path is the next section.

The physical Mac `<mac-host>` (macOS 27.2.1 arm64, Firefox 159.0 at `~/tf-t12`) was reachable over a private network and has no Nix install. `sudo` there requires a password. macOS has no single-user Nix installer, so a host install would be the multi-user daemon plus an `/nix` volume. That daemon was not installed on the physical Mac. The Darwin run used Tart VM `mac_jr` on that Mac.

```
  /nix/store/0nzi19bqisn1y1h2kha03gbdn1b7cbvl-home-manager
```

`home.stateVersion` is `"26.05"`. The disposable home was `/tmp/textfox-hm-standalone.yHQx3v`. Firefox was `/tmp/Firefox.app` (Mozilla Firefox 159.0). The log printed `Standalone home-manager activation passed`.

Passing generations:

- Seeded home: `/nix/store/nikz6jryq6lg77azw7kbl58m5g3m6bia-home-manager-generation`
- Fresh home: `/nix/store/50ilh8ahh3na33wgxybhc7dd9166f0ks-home-manager-generation`

The seeded home kept `chrome/user-notes.txt` (`kept by the user`) and `tf.test.sentinel` through an interrupted activate and two completed activates. `config.css` sets `--tf-border-width: 7px`, `--tf-font-size: 17px`, `--tf-display-vertical-tabs: none`, and `--tf-display-titles: none`. `user.js` sets `browser.nova.enabled` false and `widget.macos.native-context-menus` false. On the seeded profile, `browser.nova.enabled` was effective false, default true, with a user value; `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.nova.enabled` was effective true with no user value; `browser.shell.checkDefaultBrowser` was effective false with a user value; `tf.test.sentinel` was effective true. Bounds. Nix inside the VM is the multi-user daemon, Nix 2.35.6. A non-login shell does not source it, so the script sources `/nix/var/nix/profiles/default/etc/profile.d/nix-daemon.sh` before `nix build` and before `activate`. The build is not `--offline`. It fetched 164 paths (470.5 MiB) and built 34 derivations, including `firefox-159.0.1`, because `programs.firefox.enable` pulls `pkgs.firefox`. The probe used the copied Firefox 159.0 app. The first launch exited with `nix: command not found` before that sourcing; that was a harness bug, and the rerun above is the pass. ProfD was compared with `pwd -P`, so the probe path is `/private/tmp/...`.

## T18 support decision

Decision: Nova support is not claimed. `browser.nova.enabled` stays false on every path and the README warning stays (V1). T19 stays blocked because it runs only after a claim.

Evidence base: T16's full matrix at `e158586`, plus T17. `git diff e158586 HEAD -- chrome nix user.js tf-install.sh tf-uninstall.sh flake.nix` is empty, so no theme or delivery file changed after that run. An independent review (a separate agent given SPEC.md, this file and the test scripts, not this verdict) reached the same gate results.

| Gate | Result | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| UI (V6, V11) | Fail | Headed painted proof covers T15's 9 surfaces in both browser Nova states (`test-surface-paint-live.py`) and the Nova logo and search box (`test-nova-layout-live.py`). Sidebar, vertical tabs, split view, private windows, other panels, the page context menu and `about:` pages have computed-style checks only. V11 does not count those surfaces as styled. |
| Delivery (V2, V7, V9) | Pass for the current artifacts | NixOS and home-manager VMs, the standalone home-manager activation on Linux and on Darwin, `test-nixos-update.js` (22), the exhaustive autoconfig check and `test-manual-install-files.py` (19) all pass. Every run proves the Nova-false state only. |
| Migration (V3) | Fail; cannot pass before T19 | The one-shot clear of the persisted `browser.nova.enabled` user value is designed (T7) but unimplemented and unverified. |
| Accessibility (V5) | Fail | Painted contrast covers Linux only (T11, T15, `test-high-contrast-live.py`). Focus visibility is a computed outline check only; T15 does not check focus rings. RTL is headless geometry only. Reduced motion is set as a harness override, never asserted. |
| Platforms (V10) | Linux pass; macOS and Windows fail | macOS has one headed check, the native-menu probe on the Back item. Windows high contrast measures computed colors, not painted pixels. |

Blockers, each needed before a claim:

1. V11, V6: headed painted proof, in both browser Nova states, for the sidebar (revamp and classic), vertical tabs, split view, private windows, remaining panels, the page context menu and supported `about:` pages. The alternative is to narrow the claim to the surfaces already proven.
2. V5: painted focus-ring visibility on keyboard navigation, painted RTL output, and an assertion that chrome honors reduced motion.
3. V10: a headed painted run of the surface regression on macOS and Windows, or a claim scoped to Linux Wayland.
4. V3 with V1: V1 needs a verified migration before a claim, and T19 builds that migration only after a claim. The spec should let T18 record a claim that depends on T19, with T19 building and verifying the clear before any README or preference change.
5. V3 design gap: `user.js` can set a preference but cannot clear one. Setting `true` pins Nova on, which is not Firefox's default, and editing `prefs.js` while Firefox runs is unsafe. The manual and home-manager equivalent of the autoconfig clear is still undefined.
6. V7: T19 changes the autoconfig, so it must re-run `check-nixos-update-exhaustive.js` and the VM suites, including persistence of the new marker preference.

Proofs weaker than their records state:

- T16's `test-nova-layout-live.py` fix hides `.sidebar-inline-end` and `.content-full-width` during capture. New-tab widgets are removed from the painted proof, not tested by it.
- T15 runs with new-tab widgets, stories and weather off. Firefox's default Nova new tab is not proven in pixels.
- Linux high contrast sets `ui.useAccessibilityTheme`, so OS detection is never exercised. T8 discloses this.
- T17 Darwin: home-manager built `firefox-159.0.1`, but the probe ran a copied 159.0. The home-manager-delivered binary was never launched there.
- The painted checks (T14, T15) ran on host Firefox 159.0 only. The VMs ship 159.0.1, which has no painted run.
- Every painted proof uses one setup: 1280x800, LTR, Wayland, pixman. X11 and GPU rendering are not covered.

The assertions themselves can fail: T13, T14, T15 and the Windows high-contrast check each failed on a subtraction run or a known-bad configuration. The blockers above need new §T tasks through the spec skill. T18 adds none itself.

## T19 retirement clear for manual and home-manager paths

Mechanism: `tools/clear-nova-pref.py PROFILE`. Nothing ships: `nix/pkgs/default.nix` installs only `chrome/` and `user.js`, the shipped block still assigns `browser.nova.enabled` false, and `check-pref-parity.py` still passes with the same five preferences.

Why a separate tool: `user.js` can set a preference but cannot clear one, and assigning `true` pins Nova on. `browser.nova.enabled` is a plain `pref()` in Firefox 159's `firefox.js`, not sticky, so once the user value is gone the default applies. The tool edits `prefs.js` while it holds Firefox's own profile lock. That lock is an fcntl lock on `.parentlock` on both Linux and macOS. On Linux the `lock` symlink is not the lock: Firefox 159 took over a `lock` symlink that named a live PID, because the `+` in `addr:+pid` defers to the fcntl lock. Measured both ways: a running Firefox makes the lock busy, and a Firefox started while the lock is held refuses the profile. Linux prints `Firefox is already running`. macOS prints `A copy of Firefox is already open`.

Behavior:

- Exit 75 when Firefox holds the profile, with nothing touched. Only EACCES or EAGAIN count as busy. Any other lock error exits 1.
- Exit 1 when the textfox block in `user.js` (read through a link) still assigns Nova. Run before the retired block is in place, the next start would write the value back after the marker, and Nova would stay false for good.
- Exit 1 for a symlinked `prefs.js` (opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`), for a directory with none of `prefs.js`, `times.json`, `compatibility.ini` or `user.js`, and for a profile or `prefs.js` owned by another uid.
- Otherwise it removes only `user_pref("browser.nova.enabled", false)` lines, since textfox only ever wrote `false`. It splits on `\n` bytes only, so other line separators inside string values stay intact. In the same atomic rewrite (temp file, fsync, rename, directory fsync) it adds the one-shot marker `textfox.nova.cleared` true. File mode is kept, and the old bytes go to `prefs.js-<stamp>.textfox.bak`. Temp files left by a killed run are swept while the lock is held.
- A profile that already has the marker is left alone, so a `false` the user sets later stays. The marker lives in `prefs.js`. Resetting it in `about:config`, Refresh Firefox, or deleting `prefs.js` removes it, and a later run would then clear a user's `false`.

Proof: `tools/firefox-chrome/test-clear-nova-live.py`.

- 8 file cases: a non-profile directory is refused, a fresh profile, an existing profile, a no-op second run, kept values (a `true`, and a `false` in the user's own `user.js` lines), the unretired block refused, the helper's lock against itself, a symlinked `prefs.js`.
- Live cases on disposable existing profiles:
  - Manual: installed with `tf-install.sh`, then started once, so Firefox itself persisted Nova false. The clear is refused before the block is retired. Control: with the block retired and no clear, Nova stays false as a user value. The clear exits 75 while Firefox runs. Firefox refuses the profile while the helper-style lock is held. The clear then runs, is one-shot, and two later starts report Nova effective true, default true, no user value. The sentinel in `prefs.js` and the user's own `user.js` preference survive, and the marker persists.
  - User value: after the clear, a `false` in the user's own `user.js` lines applies, and a rerun keeps it.
  - Home-manager shape: `user.js` is a read-only symlink swapped from the old generation to the retired one, with a persisted false and a control start. The clear does not touch the link.
  - Stale lock: after Firefox is killed with SIGKILL, the clear proceeds and Firefox starts on the profile.
- The harness quits Firefox through `Services.startup.quit`. With SIGTERM, macOS Firefox exited before a pending `prefs.js` save, and the user-value case failed on mac_jr. That was a harness fault, not a helper fault.

Results, `MOZ_HEADLESS=1`, all cases PASS:

- Linux host: Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`.
- Tart VM `mac_jr`: macOS 27.2 (26B123) arm64, Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` at `/tmp/Firefox.app`, Command Line Tools `python3` 3.9.6, `marionette_driver` in `/tmp/tf-t19/venv`. On macOS the stale case leaves only `.parentlock`; there is no `lock` symlink.

Subtraction runs, each red on Linux: lock removed (`held lock: expected exit 75, got 0`), marker check removed (`second run: not a no-op`), order check removed (`unretired block: expected refusal, got 0`). An independent review found the order hazard, the removal of user values other than `false`, the non-EACCES lock errors, the owner change, the line splitting, the missing proof that Firefox refuses a held lock, and a weak home-manager case. All are fixed above.

Wiring is T25, unbuilt and unproven:

- Manual: `tf-install.sh` runs the tool after `merge_user_js` writes the retired block, only on a profile textfox was installed in, and refuses the install on exit 75.
- Home-manager: a `home.activation` entry after `linkGeneration` runs the tool for each `textfox.profiles` entry, using `${pkgs.python3}`. It treats exit 75 as a warning and retries on the next activation, because the marker is written only on success. Until the retry, Nova stays at the persisted false. Proof needs `test-home-manager-vm.sh` and the Darwin `Profiles/` layout.

Bounds:

- The tool needs Python 3. Home-manager can supply it. On the manual path, macOS has `python3` only once Command Line Tools are installed (mac_jr has them), so T25 must decide what a manual user without Python gets, for example a message to reset `browser.nova.enabled` in `about:config`.
- Filesystems without fcntl locks (NFS) are refused, not handled.
- Windows is out of scope, because `fcntl` cannot be imported there.
- Headed runs were not needed: the result is preference state, not painting.

Run: `MOZ_HEADLESS=1 "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-clear-nova-live.py`. On macOS, set `FIREFOX=/path/Firefox.app/Contents/MacOS/firefox`.

## T20 painted regression for remaining surfaces

`test-surface-paint-more-live.py` extends T15's painted check to the surfaces T18 listed as computed-style only. It reuses T15's harness: a disposable 1280x800 headless Sway compositor, Firefox headed inside it, `grim` captures, each surface hidden in turn, and the same judge. It exits 0 or 1, never inconclusive.

- Classic layout (`sidebar.revamp` false): the bookmarks sidebar box, the content box (`#tabbrowser-tabbox`, title "main"), the horizontal tab strip, both split-view panels, the unified extensions panel, the all-tabs panel, the page context menu (opened with a real right-click on a `data:` page), four `about:` pages, and a private window's navbar, tab strip, content box and `about:privatebrowsing`.
- Revamp layout (`sidebar.revamp` and `sidebar.verticalTabs` true): the vertical tab strip, the bookmarks sidebar box, the content box, and a private window's navbar, vertical tab strip, content box and `about:privatebrowsing`.
- Each layout runs in both browser Nova states, with the defaults, a sentinel config and a known-bad config: 12 Firefox launches, about 13 minutes.
- Boxes that frame web content (content box, split panels) skip the browser's rectangle when finding the dominant color. The painted ring around it must be `--tf-bg`. That color comes from `#browser`, which textfox now paints. Element rectangles are used as compositor pixels. Wayland's `mozInnerScreenX` reports 20, the CSD shadow margin, so the test instead checks that the skipped rectangle lies inside the surface's painted box.
- `about:` pages carry only textfox's font (`chrome/content/about.css`), so V11 now counts them as styled when painted text uses the configured font at 4.5:1. Each page gets two injected runs, ten `i` and ten `W`, with an inline serif font. textfox's user-level `!important` rule must override that font. The painted widths must match within 0.8, and the text must reach 4.5:1 against the painted page. Pages: `about:preferences`, `about:addons`, `about:config`, `about:support`, `about:privatebrowsing`.
- Known-bad config: `--tf-fg: var(--tf-bg); --tf-border-width: 0px; --tf-font-family: serif`. Every surface must fail it on a border, text or font check. Other failures, such as "paints no pixels", do not count.
- Controls kept from T15: repeated and restored captures must match the base, and animations, transitions and the caret are frozen. Also: the pointer is parked at (2, 2), outside every bordered box, again after the private window maps and after the context menu opens. The sidebar's own document gets no freeze sheet, so focus moves out of its search field. Content-tab lookup matches on `browserId`.

First runs, Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, found two unpainted surfaces (§B B11):

- No Firefox theme, both Nova states: the content box and split panels sat on Firefox's `--toolbar-background-color` (84,84,88), not `--tf-bg` (54,54,58). With the defaults, the border was composited over the wrong color. The sentinel `--tf-bg` never reached that ring. `#browser` paints that variable in `tabbrowser/content-area.css`, and textfox's override applied only under `:root[lwtheme]`.
- Browser Nova on, both layouts: `skin/sidebar.css` has `@-moz-pref("browser.nova.enabled") { #sidebar-box { border: … } }` as author styles, so `#sidebar-box` computed a 0px border. With `sidebar.revamp`, Nova also paints `#sidebar` with `--sidebar-background-color` (46,46,50). `test-sidebar-live.py` and `test-sidebar-theme-live.py` never saw either.

Fixes:

- `chrome/browser.css`: `#browser` background is `--tf-bg !important` with no theme condition, and `background-image: none !important`. Under a theme this paints what it did before, because the old rule's `background-image: var(--lwt-accent-color)` was an invalid image and computed to `none`.
- `chrome/sidebar.css`: the `#sidebar-box` border is `!important`, and `#sidebar-box` sets `--sidebar-background-color: var(--tf-bg) !important`.

After the fixes, `test-surface-paint-more-live.py` exits 0. In each browser Nova state, 23 surfaces pass (16 classic, 7 revamp) with the defaults and the sentinel config, and the known-bad config fails every surface on a border, text or font check. Before the fixes, the same test failed the content box, the split panels and the private content box in both Nova states, and the sidebar with Nova on in both layouts.

Regression at the same tree: `test-surface-paint-live.py` (T15, exit 0, 9 surfaces), `test-readable-colors-live.py` (17), `test-nova-layout-live.py`, `test-high-contrast-live.py` (8), `git diff --check`. Under `MOZ_HEADLESS=1`, these also exit 0: `test-sidebar-live.py`, `test-sidebar-theme-live.py`, `test-sidebar-tools-live.py`, `test-split-view-live.py`, `test-split-fullscreen-live.py`, `test-vertical-tabs-option-live.py` (10) and `test-private-window-live.py`. Without a display or `MOZ_HEADLESS`, those seven could not connect to Marionette. That comes from the shell environment, not from the tests.

An independent review (a separate agent given the diff and the spec, not this verdict) found no blocker. Changes from it: the known-bad check now requires a border, text or font failure; the pointer is parked after the private window maps and after the context menu opens; content-tab lookup uses `browserId`; and screen placement is asserted. Its other findings are listed under the bounds below.

Run `"$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-surface-paint-more-live.py`. `--layout classic|revamp` and `--nova false|true` narrow the run, and `--config FILE` replaces the sentinel. It needs `sway`, `grim` and ImageMagick. Set `TF_SURFACE_ARTIFACTS=<dir>` to keep captures. A full run writes about 3 GB of PPM files, so leave it unset unless you are debugging.

Bounds:

- Panels: only the unified extensions and all-tabs panels. With the default config the URL bar icons are hidden (`--tf-display-urlbar-icons: none`), so the identity, protections and permission popups have no anchor and do not open. The downloads panel needs a downloads button, which appears only after a download. The bookmark-edit and library panels are not measured. The panels share the `menupopup, panel` variable rule that the measured ones prove.
- Split view, popups, the context menu and `about:` pages run in the classic layout only.
- The revamp launcher (`sidebar-main`) and the "tools" box live in a shadow root that a test sheet cannot hide, so they are not separate surfaces. Only the bookmarks sidebar panel is opened.
- The font probe is in the light DOM. Whether shadow-DOM text on `about:` pages inherits the font is T2's computed record (`T2-ABOUT-INDEX.md`), not a painted one.
- No image theme or fullscreen case. The setup is the same as T15: one 1280x800 output, LTR, Linux Wayland, pixman.
- Layout observation, not a paint failure: in the revamp launcher, the "tools" title paints over the customize (gear) button. Focus and RTL painting belong to T21.

## T21 painted accessibility: keyboard focus, RTL, reduced motion

`test-a11y-paint-live.py` reuses T15's harness: a disposable 1280x800 headless Sway compositor, Firefox headed inside it, and `grim` captures. It exits 0 or 1, never inconclusive. It runs both browser Nova states in `en` and in `he` (the signed Hebrew language pack from `test-rtl-locale-live.py`), with `ui.prefersReducedMotion` 1.

- Focus (`en` and `he`): trusted key presses from `nsITextInputProcessor` move keyboard focus. F6 reaches the URL bar. Shift+Tab then reaches a navbar button and the selected tab. ArrowDown reaches the app menu's New Tab item and activates the first toolbar context menu item. The findbar field takes `FLAG_BYKEY` focus. Each focused capture is diffed against an unfocused one. The change must lie near the target. It must surround the target's center and span at least 60% of its width and height. Across the middle half of every side, at least 80% of rows or columns must hold a pixel within 8px of the edge that changed at 3:1 or more (WCAG 2.4.13). A text selection inside the URL bar therefore does not count. The check accepts a ring or a filled indicator, as long as its pixels change at 3:1 across every side. It measures a focus indicator, not ring shape. Firefox rings a toolbar button's 32x32 icon, which is inset in the button. The Nova URL bar is pill-shaped, so its ring curves through the band. The context menu row must instead change a distinct active background over at least half the row.
- RTL (`he`): the T15 chrome surfaces (navbar, URL bar text and results, tab strip, findbar, app menu, toolbar context menu) must pass T15's judge: `--tf-bg`, a border and 4.5:1 text. Mirroring is checked from painted boxes: the app menu button must paint left of the URL bar, and the second tab left of the first.
- Motion (`en` and `he`): nothing in the chrome document may be animating at rest. `:hover` is locked through `InspectorUtils` on the navbar and the tab strip, without T15's freeze sheet. No new transition or animation may start. The first frame captured 0.25s later must already paint the hovered `--tf-accent` border on the left, right and bottom. The findbar is not a motion surface: Firefox's own findbar rule overrides textfox's transition there, so it never transitions, even with the known-bad config.
- Configs: the shipped defaults, a sentinel (`--tf-bg` rgb(24,36,64), `--tf-border` rgb(200,0,90), `--tf-accent` rgb(250,200,0)), and one known-bad config per check. Focus: `--tf-accent: var(--tf-bg)` with menu backgrounds transparent. Motion: `--tf-border-transition: 4s linear !important`. RTL has two known-bad configs. A paint config (text the color of the background, no borders) must fail the paint surfaces. A direction-only config (`#nav-bar, #tabbrowser-tabs { direction: ltr !important }`) must fail the mirror surfaces. The sentinel accent is yellow, not T15's green: green on the magenta border changes luminance by only about 1.2:1, so a green-sentinel focus failure would measure the palette, not textfox. Every surface of a check must fail that check's known-bad config, with a failure of the kind the check exists for. Focus counts a missing indicator, contrast below 3:1 or a missing active background. Motion counts a started transition or an unsettled first frame. RTL paint counts background, border or text. RTL mirroring counts only a failed mirror. That makes 20 Firefox launches, about 22 minutes.

Findings, Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` (§B B12–B14):

- B12: keyboard focus rings painted Firefox's `AccentColor` on `--tf-bg`. With browser Nova off, the URL bar ring (`--toolbar-field-border-color-focus`, `AccentColor` at 50%) painted (54,93,143) on (54,54,58), about 1.8:1. The tab ring (53,132,228) painted on the selected tab's (70,70,74), about 2.4:1. The findbar field ring was also below 3:1.
- B13: with reduced motion on, the navbar and tab strip started 200ms `border-*-color` `CSSTransition`s. T15's freeze sheet turns transitions off, so no painted test saw this.
- B14: with browser Nova on, the focused URL bar painted only the text selection, no ring. Nova draws focus as a `.urlbar-background` border (`--urlbar-background-outline-focused: none`), and textfox's `border: unset !important` removes it.

Fixes:

- `chrome/overwrites.css`: `--focus-outline-color` and `--toolbar-field-border-color-focus` take `--tf-accent`.
- `chrome/defaults.css`: `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { :root { --tf-border-transition: 0s !important; } }`. A configured transition, including the Nix `border.transition` option (written without `!important`), yields to reduced motion. A user `!important` transition still wins. That is the motion known-bad config.
- `chrome/urlbar.css`: `#urlbar[focused] > .urlbar-background` gets a `--focus-outline-width` (2px fallback) outline in `--toolbar-field-border-color-focus`, in both Nova states.

Red before, green after. A copy of the tree with the three CSS files restored to `HEAD` failed `--only motion` in every good case: navbar and tabs, both Nova states, both locales. It failed `--only focus` with the defaults and browser Nova off, on the URL bar, tab and findbar field (no side at 3:1). The pre-fix run on the tree with only B12 fixed failed the Nova-on URL bar (B14). After all three fixes, the full test exits 0. In each browser Nova state there are 6 focus surfaces and 2 motion surfaces per locale and 9 RTL surfaces in `he`, and every known-bad config fails every surface of its check.

Instrument notes: an early version accepted the URL bar's text selection as a ring, which the span rule now rejects. The RTL paint surfaces use T15's capture with one change: its stability controls ignore gray differences of 2/255 or less. With the known-bad paint config and browser Nova on in `he`, text the color of the background still rendered antialiasing that differed by one unit between identical captures. In two reruns this was 3 pixels at the URL bar results' start edge, and T15's exact comparison aborted the run as unstable. The judge's own color tolerance is 4, so this noise cannot decide a verdict. Artifacts (`TF_SURFACE_ARTIFACTS`) for a full run exceed 4 GB of PPM files. One run filled `/tmp`.

Run `"$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-a11y-paint-live.py`. `--only focus|motion|rtl`, `--nova false|true` and `--locale en|he` narrow the run, and `--config FILE` replaces the sentinel. It needs `sway`, `grim`, ImageMagick and the Hebrew language pack for the running Firefox version.

Bounds:

- Focus covers six surfaces: URL bar, one navbar button, the selected tab, the findbar field, one app menu item and one context menu row. The sidebar, vertical tabs, split view, private windows and the new tab page were not measured.
- WCAG 2.4.13 change contrast is measured pixel against pixel. Ring thickness and area are not measured. Contrast against neighbouring colors (1.4.11) is not measured separately.
- Motion covers the two textfox transitions that apply (navbar, tab strip). Other textfox transitions (sidebar, split view, bookmarks toolbar, vertical tabs) use the same variable and were not measured. Firefox's own animations are checked only at rest, where none may run.
- RTL is one locale (`he`), horizontal tabs, classic layout. `test-rtl-locale-live.py` keeps the vertical-tab and new-tab RTL geometry.
- Linux Wayland, pixman, 1280x800, dpr 1. macOS and Windows belong to T22.
- No Firefox theme (lwtheme) and no light theme were run. `--focus-outline-color` now follows `--tf-accent` under a theme too.
- Focus was measured only with reduced motion on. With motion allowed, the focus change passes through textfox's 0.2s border and outline transitions.
- The URL bar outline (B14) shows on pointer focus as well as keyboard focus, as Firefox's own ring does.
- Observation: with browser Nova on, the URL bar is pill-shaped regardless of `--tf-rounding`.
- `inventory-variables.py --non-textfox --check-ledger` already failed before T21: the T2 ledger misses about 20 Firefox variables added in T4–T20. T21 adds `--focus-outline-color` and `--toolbar-field-border-color-focus`. The ledger was not updated. `check-rule-ledger.py`, `test-rule-ledger.py` and `test-inventory-css.py` exit 0.

An independent review (a separate agent given the diff and the spec, not this verdict) found no CSS defect. Changes from it:
- RTL now checks tab-strip mirroring. The direction fault has its own known-bad config. A first run with the stricter accounting showed why: under the combined config the navbar mirror check failed only because the menu icon painted nothing (`--tf-fg` = background), not on direction.
- A known-bad config counts only through its check's own kind of failure.
- Animations at rest now fail the run.
- The docs say "focus indicator".
- The bounds now list themes, motion-on focus and pointer focus.

Its remaining findings are listed under the bounds above: motion covers only two surfaces, and the T2 ledger is not updated.

Regression at this tree:
- `test-surface-paint-live.py` (T15, 9 surfaces) exits 0.
- `test-readable-colors-live.py` (17) exits 0.
- `test-high-contrast-live.py` (8) exits 0.
- `test-nova-layout-live.py` exits 0.
- Under `MOZ_HEADLESS=1`, these exit 0: `test-rtl-locale-live.py` (8), `test-vertical-tabs-option-live.py` (10) and `test-navbar-options-live.py`.
- `test-surface-paint-more-live.py` (T20) first failed with `private navbar: captures unstable {'control': 0, 'restored': 3528}`. The failure came from browser Nova off, classic layout, defaults. A new private window focuses its URL bar at an unpredictable moment, and that ring now paints at `--tf-accent`. The T20 harness now moves focus to the content browser before measuring the private window, as it already does in the main window.
- After that harness change, `test-surface-paint-more-live.py` exits 0: 23 surfaces in both browser Nova states, and the known-bad config fails every one.

Final run at this tree: `test-a11y-paint-live.py` exits 0 over 20 Firefox launches. In each browser Nova state there are 6 focus surfaces and 2 motion surfaces in each locale, plus 9 RTL surfaces in `he`. The defaults and the sentinel pass, and every known-bad config fails each surface it applies to, with its own kind of failure.

## T22 painted surface regression on macOS and Windows

`test-surface-paint-live.py` (T15) now runs headed on Linux, macOS and Windows. Capture moved into `tools/firefox-chrome/paint_display.py`:

- Linux: the headless Sway compositor and `grim`, as before. The capture is the whole 1280x800 output.
- macOS: the logged-in desktop. Firefox is maximized and brought to the front with `nsIMacDockSupport.activateApplication`, and the test waits until the chrome document has focus. The capture is `screencapture -R` over the window, decoded from BMP. The capturing process needs Screen Recording permission.
- Windows: the interactive desktop, run as a scheduled task in that session. The capture is a GDI `BitBlt` with `CAPTUREBLT` over the maximized window, clamped to the screen. Firefox is ended with `taskkill /T /F`.
- Captures are compared in pure Python, so macOS and Windows need no ImageMagick. Element boxes, slack and edge search scale with `devicePixelRatio`, and the text-pixel floor scales with its square, so the Retina run meets the same bar.
- The probe profile starts from the shipped `user.js`, then adds probe-only prefs. New probe-only prefs: `ui.useOverlayScrollbars` 0, because a tall app menu scrolls on a 768-pixel screen and overlay scrollbars fade between captures. All four ASRouter providers are off and `cfr.features` is false.
- Stability controls ignore channel differences up to 2/255. macOS antialiases a native popup's rounded corners one unit differently between frames: 20 pixels at the corners differed by 1. The judge's colour tolerance is 4. T21's `StableCapture` makes the same allowance.
- On Windows the test sets OS high contrast on or off itself and restores the previous state afterwards. `prefers-contrast: more` must match. Each launch failure now names its cause.
- `--high-contrast` (Windows only) runs the regression under real OS High Contrast Black. `browser.display.document_color_use` must be the default 0 and unset, and `ui.useAccessibilityTheme` unset. Firefox's system colours then replace textfox's background and chrome borders in places, as high contrast intends. So chrome surfaces must paint text at 4.5:1 against what actually painted, and new-tab surfaces must show their border. The known-bad config must still fail at least one surface, and the run lists the surfaces that high contrast kept readable.

Hosts, Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` on both:

- macOS: Tart VM `mac_jr` on the physical Mac. macOS 27.2 (26B123) arm64, Firefox at `/tmp/Firefox.app`, Command Line Tools `python3` 3.9.6, `marionette_driver` in `/tmp/tf-t19/venv`. In the guest, `sshd-keygen-wrapper` already had Screen Recording, so a run over ssh can capture. On the physical Mac, `screencapture` over ssh fails with `could not create image from display`. The window captured at 2048x1476 device pixels with `devicePixelRatio` 2.
- Windows: the T12 KVM guest, Windows 11 Enterprise Evaluation (NT 10.0.26200), 1024x768, maximized window captured at 1024x728 with `devicePixelRatio` 1. The guest's Firefox had updated itself to 160.0 (build `20261104181552`). It was reinstalled from the SHA-verified 159.0 installer, and `distribution/policies.json` now sets `DisableAppUpdate`. The guest was found with OS high contrast left on.

Results:
