- Linux Wayland: exit 0. 9 surfaces pass in both browser Nova states (defaults, sentinel), and the known-bad config fails every one.
- macOS: exit 0, with the same result.
- Windows: exit 0, with the same result.
- Windows with OS high contrast: exit 0. With the defaults and the sentinel config, all 9 surfaces paint readable text in both browser Nova states. The known-bad config fails navbar, URL bar text, findbar, URL bar results, context menu and new-tab search. High contrast kept tabs, the app menu and the new-tab tile readable or bordered anyway. With the sentinel config, the app menu painted the system background (32,32,32) instead of `--tf-bg`.

Found and fixed on the way:

- §B B16 (theme): with browser Nova on, macOS and Windows painted the navbar, URL bar and tab strip with a lavender tint. Defaults painted (244,238,255) for white; the sentinel painted (129,129,160) for (24,36,64), and its text fell to 0 pixels at 4.5:1. Nova sets `--toolbox-background-image` to `--toolbox-background-gradient`, a 50%-transparent violet-to-orange gradient from toolkit `tokens-shared.css`, and `browser-shared.css` paints it as `body`'s `background-image` above `--tf-bg`. Linux's native GTK tokens set the gradient to `image(transparent)`, so no Linux painted check could see it. Fix: `chrome/browser.css` sets `--toolbox-background-gradient: none` on `body` and, outside `lwtheme`, `--toolbox-background-image: none`. Subtraction: with that block removed, macOS exits 1 with exactly these failures. With it, macOS exits 0.
- §B B15 (harness): the test opened `#toolbar-context-menu` anchored to the navbar. On macOS `widget.macos.native-anchored-menus` defaults to true, so that menu was OS-drawn (XUL box 0x0, no textfox CSS). A right-click opens a context menu at a screen point, which is a XUL popup under T13's `native-context-menus` false; the probe measured 176x64. A real anchored toolbar-button dropdown, the `#bookmarks-menu-button` popup, stayed a XUL popup (213x269) with the pref true or false, so no product gap was found. The test now opens the menu with `openPopupAtScreen(..., true)`.
- Harness faults that each failed safe, as unstable or failing captures: Firefox opening behind another app on macOS; `inflate` clamping to Linux's 1280x800 output; a Windows "pin Firefox to your taskbar?" prompt covering the findbar and app menu. Turning off every ASRouter provider did not stop that prompt; its trigger was not found. The prompt was answered Yes once in the disposable guest, so Firefox is pinned there and the prompt no longer appears. A guard that treated a painted box smaller than its element as covered was wrong and was removed: with the sentinel config, hiding the navbar reveals the same `--tf-bg` underneath.

Run on macOS or Windows with `FIREFOX` set to the Firefox binary, for example `FIREFOX=/tmp/Firefox.app/Contents/MacOS/firefox python test-surface-paint-live.py` from `tools/firefox-chrome`. On Windows, run it from a scheduled task in the desktop session (`/IT`). New-tab feeds are on, so the profile may make network requests.

Bounds: one VM display per platform (macOS 1024x768 points at DPR 2; Windows 1024x768 at 100%) and LTR only. Only T15's 9 surfaces run on macOS and Windows; the T20 surfaces and the T21 focus, RTL and motion checks remain Linux-only. One contrast theme (High Contrast Black). The macOS run is in a VM, not on physical display hardware or a physical GPU. Nova support stays unclaimed.

## T23 closing T18's weaker-than-recorded proofs

T18 listed painted and delivery proofs that claimed more than they showed. Four of them are now covered. Nova support stays unclaimed. T24 makes the support decision.

### Default Nova new tab in pixels, widgets not hidden

Marionette's `RecommendedPreferences.sys.mjs` writes `widgets.enabled`, `showSponsoredTopSites`, `testing.shouldInitializeFeeds` and `asrouter.userprefs.cfr.features` as `false` to the default branch. A runtime read of the default branch therefore reports the automation values, not Firefox's. Firefox 159's own defaults (`ActivityStream.sys.mjs` `PREFS_CONFIG`) are `true` for the first three. The widgets market gate restricts no region for `widgets.enabled`. The region decides which widgets ship. In the US, the clocks, lists, focus timer and picture-of-the-day widgets are region-blocked, and weather showed its error state. In GB, the page renders weather (opt-in state), clocks, crossword, focus timer, lists and stories. The probes therefore set those three preferences back to `true` and `browser.search.region` to `GB`. Then they assert that `Region.home` is GB and that `showWeather`, `system.showWeather`, `feeds.weatherfeed`, `feeds.section.topstories`, `feeds.system.topstories`, `widgets.system.enabled` and `widgets.weather.enabled` are effective true with no user value. `cfr.features` stays off: it is a chrome recommendation, not new-tab content (§C, probe-only).

- `test-surface-paint-live.py --newtab-defaults` runs T15's 9 surfaces with that new tab. Before measuring, the weather, clocks and widget-row articles and at least one story card must be rendered, and no image in the viewport may be pending. The widgets must paint at least 5000 pixels, measured by hiding them in one capture. They painted 161,172 to 161,190 pixels per launch. Each launch had 77 story cards and the widgets `weather-widget`, `crossword`, `clocks-widget` and `focus-timer`. Exit 0: 9 surfaces pass in both browser Nova states (defaults, sentinel), and the known-bad config fails every one.
- `test-nova-layout-live.py` no longer hides `.sidebar-inline-end` and `.content-full-width`. It uses the same default new tab and the same 5000-pixel widgets guard. Exit 0 on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`. The logo painted 3052 pixels, the search box 44,304, and 0 logo pixels fell inside the search region. The injected overlap fixture put 1414 there. The widgets painted 161,179 pixels.
- Live widgets: the clocks tick each minute. A capture set whose controls changed is retried up to 4 times and then fails the run. It is never accepted. The tile measurement retried twice (358 and 310 changed pixels) and settled.
- The first layout run failed every attempt on 7 pixels. They were the antialiasing of a shortcut title glyph, differing by 1/255 between identical frames. The layout test's stability controls now ignore per-channel differences up to 2/255, the same allowance T21 and T22 use. Exact comparison still applies to every measured mask.
- Subtraction: a copy of the layout test with T16's widget-hiding freeze restored exits 1 (`default new-tab widgets paint 0 pixels, fewer than 5000`).

Observation for T24, not a failure under any §V: textfox styles the logo, search box and shortcut tiles. The widgets (rounded cards, purple buttons) and the story cards (`.ds-card`) keep Firefox's own styling. textfox's `.card-outer .card` rule matches nothing on the Nova new tab.

### Painted run on Firefox 159.0.1

`FIREFOX` may now be a command line (`paint_display.py`, split with `shlex` outside Windows). `--expect-version` fails a run that reached another Firefox. The run used the nixpkgs build the VMs deliver, launched through nix-portable:

```
FIREFOX="sh $PWD/nix-portable-run.sh /nix/store/1sq19d9y3ccsb2pm1pb4y5i2zf6z7i2v-firefox-159.0.1 firefox" \
  python test-surface-paint-live.py --expect-version 159.0.1
```

Exit 0 on Firefox 159.0.1 build `20261102144711`, Linux Wayland: 9 surfaces pass in both browser Nova states, and the known-bad config fails every one. The first attempt failed in cleanup with `Directory not empty: …/profile/cache2/entries`. Terminating the launcher ended bwrap but not the Firefox under it, which kept writing the profile. Firefox now starts in its own session, and `stop_firefox` ends the whole process group, SIGTERM then SIGKILL, waiting until the group is empty. No probe Firefox was left running after the passing run.

### Home-manager-delivered Firefox on Darwin

`test-home-manager-standalone.sh` takes `FIREFOX=home-manager`, which launches the Firefox that the activated generation delivers: `home-path/Applications/Firefox.app/Contents/MacOS/firefox` on Darwin, `home-path/bin/firefox` on Linux. Tart VM `mac_jr`, macOS 27.2 (26B123) arm64, the same nixpkgs and home-manager sources as T17:

```
FIREFOX=home-manager sh tools/firefox-chrome/test-home-manager-standalone.sh \
  /nix/store/lw9n2gsykqbq10cxmfwj2lnij4c7v6a2-nixpkgs \
  /nix/store/0nzi19bqisn1y1h2kha03gbdn1b7cbvl-home-manager
```

The log printed `Standalone home-manager activation passed`. Generations: seeded `/nix/store/9sii2pzbyhfszwvv8pi9ibkkfjf7m4n9-home-manager-generation`, fresh `/nix/store/i766yhsbcwxkr86zv16w5s8sw381bfyx-home-manager-generation`. Both generations' Firefox reported 159.0.1 build `20261102144711`, with a navbar border of `7px` at `Library/Application Support/Firefox/Profiles/test`. On the seeded profile, `browser.nova.enabled` was effective false, default true, with a user value. `browser.newtabpage.activity-stream.nova.enabled` was effective true with no user value, and `tf.test.sentinel` survived. The generation's `Firefox.app` is a link farm into `firefox-unwrapped-159.0.1`. The launch is headless, as in T17: it proves delivery and preference state, not painting.

Host notes: the guest stopped during a file copy for this run, for an unknown cause, and was restarted with `tart run`. macOS cleared the guest's `/tmp` at boot, so T19's and T22's `/tmp/Firefox.app` (159.0) and `/tmp/tf-t19/venv` are gone. The tree and the `marionette_driver` venv now live in `~/tf-t23` and `~/tf-t23-venv`.

### X11 painted run

`TF_PAINT_X11=1` starts the disposable Sway with `xwayland force`, reads the `DISPLAY` Sway gives its clients, and launches Firefox with `GDK_BACKEND=x11`, `MOZ_ENABLE_WAYLAND=0` and no `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`. The run fails unless Firefox's `nsIGfxInfo.windowProtocol` is `x11`. `grim` still captures the compositor output. Exit 0 on Firefox 159.0: 9 surfaces pass in both browser Nova states, and the known-bad config fails every one. The window protocol was `x11` and the layer manager `WebRender (Software)`.

Bounds:

- No GPU rendering run. Every painted proof, including this X11 one, uses software WebRender over a pixman compositor.
- The default new tab is one region (GB) and one locale (en-US), with the weather widget in its opt-in state, not showing a forecast. Stories render below the 800-pixel fold. They are asserted rendered, not painted.
- The 159.0.1 painted run is on Linux only. On Darwin, 159.0.1 is the home-manager launch, headless.
- The T20 and T21 painted tests were not run on 159.0.1, on X11 or with the default new tab.

## T24 support decision re-review

Decision: conditional. The evidence supports Nova support on Firefox 159.0 and 159.0.1 within the scope below, if T26 and T25 pass. Nothing public changes in T24. `browser.nova.enabled` stays false on every path, the README warning stays, and V1 is unchanged. T26 re-runs the painted suites on the current Firefox release (160.0 or newer); any failure there withdraws this decision. T25 then retires the preference with a one-shot clear, and only its passing regression may change the README.

Why 159.x only: every painted proof ran on 159.0 or 159.0.1. Firefox 160.0 (build `20261104181552`) had already shipped; the T22 Windows guest updated itself to it. textfox supports only the latest release, and B16 showed that a release can bring new Nova tokens that only paint on some platforms.

### Re-runs at HEAD (`99e235f`)

The review found proofs older than the theme files they cover. B16 (`f30d25b`) changed `chrome/browser.css` after the last T20 and T21 runs. T21 (`1f860d5`) changed `chrome/defaults.css`, which affects the Nix `border.transition` option, and no option check had run since. Re-run on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, Linux Wayland:

- `test-surface-paint-more-live.py` (T20): exit 0. 23 surfaces in both browser Nova states, classic and revamp layouts, defaults and sentinel; the known-bad config fails every one.
- `test-a11y-paint-live.py` (T21): exit 0, run alone. In each browser Nova state: 6 focus and 2 motion surfaces in `en` and in `he`, 9 RTL surfaces in `he`. Defaults and sentinel pass; each known-bad config fails every surface of its check.
- `check-option-wiring.py` (25 options), `check-option-parity.py` (6), `check-home-manager-options.py`, `check-config-defaults.py`, `test-nixos-generated-live.py`: exit 0. Sources: nixpkgs `/nix/store/wa9kz0gcy4wgakdv28p7i4jc7xkcbbky-source`, home-manager `/nix/store/8sp96qrpjlzhmfvhr94z8k0yq03mszk8-source`. `check-option-parity.py` needs `MOZ_HEADLESS=1` without a display.
- `check-pref-parity.py` (5 preferences), `test-pref-parity.py`, `test-nixos-update.js` (22), `test-manual-install-files.py` (19), `nixfmt-tree --ci` (0 changed), `git diff --check`: exit 0.

T15's `test-surface-paint-live.py` ran at this tree in T23 (Linux Wayland, X11, 159.0.1). The macOS and Windows runs are at `f30d25b`; no theme file changed after them. The NixOS and home-manager VM suites last ran at `e158586` (T16) and in T17 and T23. Since then, only CSS changed, and the generated-CSS and option checks above cover that.

The first T21 re-run ran next to T20 and stopped with `Failed to write ppm; only 0 of 3072016 bytes written`: the two runs' captures filled the 16 GB `/tmp` tmpfs. Every row it finished matched the expected result. Run the painted suites one at a time.

### Gates

| Gate | Result | Evidence | Scope and gaps |
|---|---|---|---|
| UI (V6, V11) | Pass with scope | T15 and T22: 9 key surfaces on Linux, macOS and Windows. T20: 23 surfaces on Linux, re-run above. T23: default Nova new tab with widgets, stories and weather on, nothing hidden, and the layout test. | Only 2 panels measured (unified extensions, all tabs). Identity, protections, permissions, downloads, bookmark-edit and library panels are not. Split view, the page context menu and `about:` pages are classic layout only. The revamp launcher is not a separate surface. New-tab widgets and story cards keep Firefox styling (below). |
| Delivery (V2, V7, V9) | Pass for current artifacts | T16 VMs (NixOS, home-manager), T17 standalone home-manager on Linux and Darwin, T23 home-manager-delivered 159.0.1 launched on Darwin, plus the re-runs above. | Every run proves the Nova-false state. The retired state is T25. |
| Migration (V3) | Pass on design, conditional on T25 | T19: `tools/clear-nova-pref.py` and `test-clear-nova-live.py`, Linux and macOS, 159.0. T7: autoconfig clear design. | Wiring is unbuilt (T25). Windows manual path has no mechanism (`fcntl`). Manual macOS needs Python 3. The autoconfig design is one sentence; the open points are T25 acceptance items below. |
| Accessibility (V5, V12) | Pass with scope | T21 re-run above: 6 focus surfaces, 2 motion surfaces, 9 painted RTL surfaces (`he`), both browser Nova states. T22: Windows OS High Contrast Black from painted pixels. `test-high-contrast-live.py` on Linux. | Focus, motion and RTL are Linux only. Focus measured under reduced motion only. No lwtheme or light theme. RTL is `he`, horizontal tabs, classic layout. Linux high contrast is detected through `ui.useAccessibilityTheme`, not the OS. |
| Platforms (V10) | Pass with scope | Linux Wayland and X11 (T23). macOS (VM, DPR 2) and Windows (1024x768, plus High Contrast Black): T15's 9 surfaces headed (T22). | T20 surfaces and T21 checks never ran on macOS or Windows; B16 showed defects that only those platforms paint. No GPU rendering: every painted run is software WebRender over pixman (Linux) or a VM display. |

Claim scope if T26 and T25 pass: Linux (Wayland and X11, software WebRender) for every measured surface and the accessibility checks; macOS and Windows for T15's 9 key surfaces, the navbar, URL bar and results, tabs, app menu, context menu, findbar and new tab, plus Windows High Contrast Black. Listed as unverified: T20 surfaces and T21 checks on macOS and Windows, the unmeasured panels, GPU rendering, lwtheme and light themes, the Windows manual migration.

### T18 blockers and weak proofs

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| 1. Painted remaining surfaces (V11) | Closed on Linux (T20, re-run). Open on macOS and Windows. |
| 2. Painted focus, RTL, reduced motion (V5) | Closed on Linux (T21, re-run). |
| 3. macOS and Windows painted run (V10) | Partly: T15 surfaces only, one VM display each. |
| 4. V1/V3 ordering | Closed: T24 records a conditional decision, T25 builds and proves the clear. |
| 5. Manual and home-manager clear | Closed as tool and proof (T19). Wiring is T25; Windows and Python-less manual macOS open. |
| 6. Autoconfig re-check after the clear | Open by design: T25. |
| T16 widget hiding | Closed (T23, subtraction red). |
| Default new tab not painted | Closed for the 9 surfaces, one region and locale (GB, en-US). Stories asserted rendered, not painted. |
| Linux high contrast via `ui.useAccessibilityTheme` | Open on Linux. Real OS high contrast is painted on Windows. |
| Darwin ran a copied binary | Closed: T23 launched the delivered 159.0.1, headless. |
| No painted run on 159.0.1 | Partly: T15 surfaces on Linux only. |
| One render setup | Partly: X11 added. No GPU run. |

### T25 acceptance items (autoconfig clear)

T7's design is `Services.prefs.clearUserPref` behind a textfox-owned marker preference. T25 must settle:

- The marker name. Reuse T19's `textfox.nova.cleared`, so a profile that moves between delivery paths is cleared once.
- Where the clear runs in the autoconfig script relative to the removed `pref()`, and whether it runs on a refused (`refuse()`) or force-adopted install.
- A crash before `prefs.js` is saved loses the marker. The next start clears again and drops a `false` the user set in that session. Decide whether that is accepted, and test it.
- Switching between NixOS and the manual or home-manager path, in both directions.

### New-tab widgets and story cards

On the default Nova new tab, the widgets (weather, clocks, crossword, focus timer) and the story cards (`.ds-card`) keep Firefox styling. textfox's `.card-outer .card` rule in `chrome/content/newtab.css` matches nothing there. V11 does not require every surface, so no gate fails. It does fall short of §G's "without losing TUI appearance". New-tab Nova is Firefox's default and textfox never sets it, so users see this today, whatever happens to browser Nova. It needs its own §T task; T24 records it and adds none.

### Harness findings, not fixed in T24

- `test-surface-paint-live.py` counts the known-bad config as detected by any failure on a surface. T20's `KNOWN_BAD_SIGNS` and T21's per-check signs require a border, text or font failure. A surface that fails for an unrelated reason therefore counts as detecting the bad config.
- In `--high-contrast` mode the known-bad config has to fail only one surface. Disclosed in T22.
- An empty first measurement would carry through as zero surfaces. Missing targets raise, so the risk is low; a non-empty assertion would remove it.

### Review

An independent review (a separate agent given SPEC.md, this file and the test scripts, not this verdict) returned accept-with-reservations: a scoped, conditional claim is supported, an unscoped one is not. Its findings are the HEAD re-runs, the Firefox 160 drift, V1's wording against a recorded claim, the thin autoconfig design, the new-tab cards and the harness findings above. The user chose to scope to 159.x, add T26 as a 160 re-run gate before T25, and keep V1 by recording a decision that changes nothing public.

## T26 painted suites on Firefox 160.0

Firefox 160.0 build `20261104181552` is the current release (`product-details.mozilla.org`, released 2026-11-10). Each host got Mozilla's release build, verified against `releases/160.0/SHA256SUMS`, with `distribution/policies.json` setting `DisableAppUpdate`. Every run asserted 160.0 (`--expect-version 160.0` for T15; the other suites record the version in each launch's JSON).

- Linux: `~/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/firefox-160.0` (tarball). `test-surface-paint-more-live.py` and `test-a11y-paint-live.py` launch a bare `firefox`, so the run puts a `bin/firefox` link first in `PATH`. Hebrew language pack `langpacks/160.0/he.xpi`, verified the same way.
- macOS: Tart VM `mac_jr`, `~/ff160/Firefox.app` from the 160.0 dmg. The guest is reached by ssh from the Mac host (`ssh <mac-host>`, then `ssh admin@<vm-ip>`); this machine has no host key for the guest.
- Windows: the T12 KVM guest, `C:\tf\Firefox160` extracted from `Firefox Setup 160.0.exe` (`/ExtractDir`), scheduled tasks `tf-t26` and `tf-t26hc` running `C:\tf\t26\run-t26.cmd`. SSH on `127.0.0.1:2222` uses `~/VMs/tf-t12/known_hosts`. The global `known_hosts` holds a different host for that address.

Results, all exit 0:

| Suite | Host | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `test-surface-paint-live.py` (T15) | Linux Wayland | 9 surfaces, both browser Nova states, defaults and sentinel; known-bad fails every one |
| `test-surface-paint-live.py` | macOS (DPR 2) | same |
| `test-surface-paint-live.py` | Windows | same |
| `test-surface-paint-live.py --high-contrast` | Windows | OS High Contrast Black: all 9 surfaces paint readable text and new-tab borders in both Nova states; known-bad fails the rest. High contrast kept tabs, app menu and new-tab tile readable, as on 159 |
| `test-surface-paint-more-live.py` (T20) | Linux Wayland | 23 surfaces, classic and revamp, both Nova states; known-bad fails every one |
| `test-a11y-paint-live.py` (T21) | Linux Wayland | 6 focus and 2 motion surfaces per locale (`en`, `he`), 9 RTL surfaces, both Nova states; each known-bad config fails every surface of its check |
| `test-nova-layout-live.py` | Linux Wayland | logo and search box paint, no overlap |

Found on the way (§B B17):

- macOS, browser Nova off, defaults: the T15 tabs surface failed with `tabs text paints 55 pixels at 4.5:1, fewer than 120`. It reproduced on a second run. Firefox 160 de-emphasizes unselected tab labels with `.tab-label-container:not([selected], [multiselected]) { opacity: var(--opacity-deemphasized-strong); }` in `chrome://browser/skin/browser.css`, which computes to 0.6. Firefox 159 had a literal 0.7 (`browser/themes/osx/browser.css:160`). The label is black, so it paints (102,102,102) on white: 5.7:1, readable. That gray is within 16 of the default `--tf-accent` (91,91,102) on macOS, and the judge dropped `--tf-border` and `--tf-accent` pixels from every surface's text count. Recounting the saved captures: 1072 text pixels changed and 583 reach 4.5:1. 528 of those are (102,102,102), and every one was excluded. The judge now excludes only the surface's own border color, which `inflate()` takes into the text region. A borderless surface (URL bar text) keeps both exclusions. The saved capture then counts 583. Linux's 160 `browser.css` has no such rule. Windows was not inspected; it passed with the old judge too.
- Windows: the first 160 run stopped with `module 'os' has no attribute 'getpgid'`. T23's process-group stop in `paint_display.py` ran after `WindowsDisplay`'s `taskkill`, and T23 never re-ran Windows. On Windows, `stop_firefox` now uses only the `taskkill /T /F` tree kill and then waits. Under the old judge, Windows on 160 passed. All results in the table use the new judge.

Both are harness faults, not theme defects. No CSS changed in T26. V13 now requires a shared painted-harness change to be re-run on every platform a claim cites.

Infrastructure notes:

- `/tmp` here is a RAM-backed tmpfs (16 GB, about 13 GB used by leftovers from earlier sessions). With the Windows VM holding 12 GB of RAM, `grim` failed to write captures twice: once with T20 and T21 running side by side, once with T20 alone. The Linux runs above used `TMPDIR=~/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/tmp` on disk; the tests take their temporary directories from `tempfile`.
- Firefox 160 Marionette still needs system access for chrome context. The suites pass `-remote-allow-system-access`; an ad hoc probe also needed `MOZ_REMOTE_ALLOW_SYSTEM_ACCESS=1`. `InspectorUtils.getCSSStyleRules` is now `getMatchingCSSRules`.

Effect on T24: T26 passed, so T24's conditional decision stands and now covers 160.0 as well as 159.x, within the same scope and bounds. T25 may start. X11, the default new tab (`--newtab-defaults`) and 159.0.1 were not re-run on 160.

Run on Linux:

```
D=~/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/firefox-160.0
TMPDIR=~/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/tmp PATH=$D/bin:$PATH FIREFOX=$D/bin/firefox \
  "$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python" tools/firefox-chrome/test-surface-paint-live.py --expect-version 160.0
```

## T25 Nova assignment retired with a one-shot clear

textfox no longer assigns `browser.nova.enabled` on any delivery path. The `false` that earlier versions persisted in `prefs.js` is cleared once per profile, behind the marker `textfox.nova.cleared` that every path shares. A profile that moves between delivery paths is therefore cleared only once. Nova support is claimed in the README within T24's scope, extended to Firefox 160.0 by T26.

What ships:

- `user.js`: the Nova line is gone. The pre-T25 block is kept as the fixture `tools/firefox-chrome/fixtures/user.js-nova-false`.
- NixOS (`nix/pkgs/wrapTextfox.nix`): no `pref()` for Nova. In the prefs section, before any profile write, the clear runs if the marker is absent and the profile's `user.js` does not assign Nova on any line. It removes the user value only when that value is a boolean `false`, then sets the marker and calls `Services.prefs.savePrefFile(null)`. A non-boolean value is left alone and still marked. The clear also runs on a refused or force-adopted install, because preferences apply on every start. Firefox loads `prefs.js` and `user.js` in `Preferences::InitializeUserPrefs` before it notifies `prefservice:before-read-userprefs`, the topic that runs the autoconfig, so the clear sees the persisted value. The live and VM runs confirm it.
- `tools/clear-nova-pref.py`:
  - New `--check-lock`: it takes and releases Firefox's profile lock and changes nothing.
  - An unmarked profile whose `user.js` assigns Nova on any line is refused. That covers an old block, a pre-marker copy without markers, and the user's own line, because each would write the value back and the marker would block every later clear.
- Manual (`tf-install.sh`): applies only to a profile textfox was installed in before (`textfox_installed`, decided before the backup moves files).
  - With Python 3.8 or newer, `--check-lock` runs before any change, and exit 75 aborts the install untouched. Any other check failure, for example a directory without profile files or a Python without `fcntl`, skips the reset and prints the `about:config` step.
  - After the merge, the clear runs. Without `python3`, the install completes and prints the `about:config` step.
  - A profile textfox never touched keeps any Nova value.
- Home Manager:
  - `nix/pkgs/default.nix` ships `clear-nova-pref.py`.
  - `home.activation.textfoxClearNova` runs after `linkGeneration`, for each `textfox.profiles` entry, with `${pkgs.python3}`, through `run`.
  - Exit 75 is a `warnEcho`, and the next activation retries. Messages are shell-quoted.
- `check-pref-parity.py`: four preferences. Any Nova assignment in either artifact fails, `false` or `true`.

User decisions: the manual install checks the profile lock first and aborts while Firefox runs; without `python3` it installs and prints the `about:config` step.

T24 acceptance items:

- Marker name: `textfox.nova.cleared` on every path.
- Placement: in the prefs section, before `refuse()` and force-adopt.
- Crash before the save: the immediate `savePrefFile` makes the marker durable. The exhaustive check injects a crash before that save.
- Switching delivery paths:
  - From NixOS to manual or Home Manager: the marker makes the tool a no-op.
  - From manual or Home Manager to NixOS with an old `user.js`: the autoconfig waits until `user.js` stops assigning Nova. Found by the review.

Proofs:

- `test-nixos-update.js`: 27 scenarios. Five cover the clear:
  - cleared once, marked and saved;
  - a `true` kept;
  - a refused install still clears;
  - a `user.js` that assigns Nova defers the clear and the marker;
  - a non-boolean value is left alone.

  The shim's `Services.prefs` now persists user values across runs. `savePrefFile` is a mutating step.
- `check-nixos-update-exhaustive.js`:
  - New property P6 nova-once.
  - The prefs model persists a complete run's values, and after a crash only what the script saved. `user.js` is modelled: an assigning line puts its `false` back on every load.
  - New events: the user sets Nova `false` after the marker, and the user removes the assigning `user.js` line.
  - New starts: Nova unset, the user's own `true`, a textfox `false`, a pre-T25 manual block moved to NixOS, and the user's own `false` before any clear.
  - P3 now allows a refusal to save the prefs clear.
  - Known finding F6, accepted: before the marker, a user's own `false` cannot be told from the one earlier textfox wrote, so the one-shot clear takes it.
  - Proven able to fail: a clear without the marker check (caught by P5 and P6), a clear of any value (P6), and a clear without the `user.js` guard (P6).
- `test-manual-install-files.py`: 23 cases, under `bash` and `sh`. New cases:
  - a reinstall over an old textfox clears once;
  - the install aborts untouched while the lock is held;
  - a first install leaves a Nova value alone;
  - without `python3` the install completes and names the `about:config` step.
- `test-clear-nova-live.py`, all on Firefox 160.0 build `20261104181552`, headless:
  - 11 file cases, including the `--check-lock` pair and a loose `user.js` line.
  - Manual: a pre-T25 block that Firefox ran, then the T25 `tf-install.sh`. It aborts untouched while Firefox runs, then retires the block, clears, is one-shot on reinstall, and survives two starts.
  - The user's own value afterwards is kept.
  - The Home Manager link swap.
  - A stale lock.
- `test-manual-install-live.py` (160.0): a fresh install leaves Firefox's default. A user's own Nova `true` stays effective. Firefox drops a user value equal to the default when it saves.
- `test-nixos-prefs-live.py`: the wrapped 159.0.1 under nix-portable, seeded with Nova `false`. Nova is at the default, the marker is set and persisted, the sentinel is kept, and the CSS is applied, across restarts.
- VMs (nixpkgs `/nix/store/wa9kz0gcy4wgakdv28p7i4jc7xkcbbky-source`, home-manager `/nix/store/8sp96qrpjlzhmfvhr94z8k0yq03mszk8-source`, Firefox 159.0.1):
  - `test-nixos-update-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-autoconfig-vm.sh`.
  - `test-nixos-prefs-vm.sh`: seeded `false`, expects the default and the marker persisted after a graceful stop.
  - `test-home-manager-vm.sh`: seeded `false`, activation clears, marker persisted.
- Standalone Home Manager:
  - Linux, same sources.
  - Darwin (Tart `mac_jr`, `/nix/store/lw9n2gsykqbq10cxmfwj2lnij4c7v6a2-nixpkgs`, `/nix/store/0nzi19bqisn1y1h2kha03gbdn1b7cbvl-home-manager`, `FIREFOX=home-manager`, 159.0.1): seeded `false` cleared, marker persisted.
- `check-home-manager-options.py`, `test-home-manager-live.py` (160.0), `check-option-wiring.py` (25), `check-option-parity.py` (6), `check-config-defaults.py`, `test-nixos-generated-live.py`, `test-nix-logo-live.py`, `check-home-manager-inputs.py`, the rule ledger, CSS inventory and cascade-order checks, `nixfmt-tree --ci`, `git diff --check`.
- `test-surface-paint-live.py` on Linux, Firefox 160.0: 9 surfaces, both Nova states; known-bad fails every one. No CSS changed in T25.

Re-run after the review fixes (autoconfig `user.js` guard, Home Manager quoting), all exit 0: `check-nixos-update-exhaustive.js` depth 4 (33,348 profiles, 138,656 events; P1–P6 hold outside known F5 and F6), `test-nixos-prefs-live.py`, `test-nixos-update-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-prefs-vm.sh`, `test-nixos-autoconfig-vm.sh`, `test-home-manager-vm.sh`, and standalone Home Manager on Linux and Darwin. The README changed only after these passed.

Harness fixes on the way:

- `check-home-manager.nix` and `check-home-manager-standalone.nix` passed the source tree as the package. The derivation's layout differs: the tool sits at the package root. The first Darwin run could not find it and left Nova `false`. Both harnesses now build `nix/pkgs/default.nix`, as the VM harness did.
- This host's `~/.local/bin/nix` forces `NP_RUNTIME=proot`. Under proot, a build that copies the source tree fails with `cp: setting permissions for 'textfox'` and `do not know how to unpack source archive`. Home Manager checks that build the package need a `bwrap` nix first on `PATH`. The runs used `~/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/nix-bwrap-bin/nix`, which sets `NP_RUNTIME=bwrap` and runs `nix-portable nix`.
- Expectations that pinned Nova off moved to Firefox's default: `probe-vm-preferences.py` gained `--expect-nova default` (now the default), and `check-nixos-eval.nix` asserts no Nova `pref()` and a `clearUserPref`.

Independent review (a separate agent given the diff and the spec, not this record) asked for changes. Fixed:
- the manual-to-NixOS `user.js` case;
- the `about:config` message on a failed lock check;
- README scope and the manual-copy order;
- the non-boolean value;
- quoting in Home Manager messages;
- the user's own pre-marker `false`, now recorded as F6.

Declined:
- Not writing a marker on a profile without `prefs.js`: a fresh profile's marker is what keeps a later activation from clearing a `false` the user sets afterwards.
- `run --quiet`: the flag depends on the Home Manager version.

Out of scope:
- `tf-uninstall.sh` on an older textfox leaves its persisted `false`.

Bounds:

- A pre-marker Nova `false` the user set themselves is cleared with textfox's (F6). The README says to set it again after the reset.
- On a profile with no `prefs.js`, the marker is written at once. Removing it (`about:config` reset, Refresh Firefox) lets a later run clear a user's `false`, as T19 records.
- The Windows manual path has no tool (`fcntl`). The README points to `about:config`.
- Home Manager assumes the profile directory equals the profile name, as the existing `home.file` entries already do.
- Home Manager resets every profile in `textfox.profiles`, including one textfox never ran in, because activation cannot tell. The manual path resets only a profile textfox was installed in. The README's IMPORTANT note says that updating turns Nova on, including over a user's own `false`, and how to turn it off again.

## Commit IDs after the 2026-11-14 history rewrite

Before the first push, the unpublished commits were rewritten to remove a hostname, home-directory paths and a private address from this file. Every rewritten commit got a new ID. IDs quoted in this file, `SPEC.md` and the autoconfig protocol skill were updated to the new IDs. IDs inside commit messages were not, and refer to the pre-rewrite commits. To find a quoted ID's commit, match on its subject line: `git log --format='%h %s' | grep '<subject>'`.

## T27 section titles centered on their borders

Reported by the user after installing into a throwaway profile (Firefox 159, scaled display, revamp layout, vertical tabs, browser Nova on): "navbar" was cut by the window top, "tabs" was half-covered, and "tools" sat over Nova's gear button (§B B18).

Cause:

- Every title is an absolute `::before` placed by a hand-tuned negative margin (`-16px`, `-1.75rem`, `-1rem`, `3.25rem`). Nothing tied the title to its box's border, and only "main" had a `z-index`.
- A leftover `@media -moz-pref("sidebar.revamp") { #nav-bar::before { margin: -1.35rem 0.75rem; left: 0 } }` in `chrome/navbar.css` put the revamp navbar title about 19px above the window.
- On Firefox 159, chrome painted over the part of the "tabs" title above its box; 160 did not. Measured at device pixel ratio 2, it was cut at the navbar area's bottom edge.
- "tools" lives in `sidebar-main`'s shadow root on `.buttons-wrapper`, which has `overflow: clip`. Its title was therefore placed inside the box, and Nova moves the customize (gear) button to that box's top-left.
- No painted test measured title placement. T20 recorded the tools overlap as an observation only.

Fix (user's choice: center titles on the line, `┌─ tabs ─┐`):

- Each title keeps its static position and is pinned to the top-left with `align-self: flex-start`. It is pulled up by its box's top padding, half the border and half a line: `margin: calc(-1 * (<top padding> + var(--border-width) / 2 + 0.5lh)) 0 0 8px`. Hosts that clip their overflow (bookmarks, vertical tabs, findbar, tools) stay unpositioned, so they do not clip their own title.
- `#nav-bar` is positioned and its padding is a user variable, so its title uses `top: calc(-1 * (var(--border-width) / 2 + 0.5lh)); left: 8px`.
- Every title has `z-index: 1` and `line-height: normal`.
- The stale revamp `#nav-bar::before` rule is removed.
- `.buttons-wrapper` and `#TabsToolbar` get `padding-top: 0.5lh`, so their buttons and tabs start below the title's lower half.
- Files: `chrome/navbar.css`, `chrome/tabs.css`, `chrome/browser.css`, `chrome/sidebar.css`, `chrome/findbar.css`.

Proof: `tools/firefox-chrome/test-titles-paint-live.py` (V14), headed in the disposable Sway compositor.

- In place: a title's glyphs are the pixels that change when only that title is hidden and that are not its background.
- Reference: a copy of the title (same computed font, padding, colors and text) appended at a fixed spot on top of everything, measured the same way.
- Checks:
  - Whole: the in-place glyph box is at least the reference size, less 1 device pixel.
  - On the line: the glyphs reach above the box's top border and below its bottom edge.
  - Clear of controls: the glyph box intersects no visible toolbarbutton, button, moz-button, tab, menulist or checkbox, light or shadow DOM.
- Titles:
  - Classic: navbar, bookmarks, horizontal tabs (`--tf-display-horizontal-tabs: block`, as the README says), main, sidebar, find.
  - Revamp: navbar, bookmarks, vertical tabs, main, tools, find.
- Matrix: browser Nova off and on, classic and revamp, device pixel ratio 1 and 2 (`layout.css.devPixelsPerPx`).
- Red at the old CSS (worktree at `e25b10f`, Firefox 159): every combination failed. "navbar" and vertical "tabs" were cut, titles did not straddle their borders, and "tools" overlapped `main-button` and `tools-overflow`.
- Green: exit 0 on Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525` and on Firefox 160.0 build `20261104181552`. The first full run caught the horizontal "tabs" title over the first tab at ratio 2; the strip's `padding-top` fixed it.

Harness fix (V13): `test-surface-paint-live.py` (T15) never parked the pointer. On macOS the real cursor rested on the newly centered "find" title, so the findbar border painted its hover color and the run failed on left, right and bottom, in both Nova states. T15 now moves the pointer to the window's top-left after setup and after each popup opens, as T20 does. Per V13 it was re-run on every platform.

Regression with the new CSS on Firefox 160.0:
- Linux: `test-surface-paint-live.py`, `test-surface-paint-more-live.py` (23), `test-a11y-paint-live.py`, `test-nova-layout-live.py`.
- macOS: `test-surface-paint-live.py` with the parked pointer.
- Windows: `test-surface-paint-live.py` with the parked pointer, plus the OS High Contrast Black run (all 9 surfaces readable in both Nova states; known-bad fails the rest).
- Linux T15 with the parked pointer: exit 0.

Bounds:

- The navbar title fits the window with the default `--tf-navbar-margin` top (8px) and `--tf-font-size` (14px). A smaller top margin or a larger font can push it past the window top again.
- Title paddings are written into the calc for each box. A box whose top padding textfox does not set (`#TabsToolbar`, `.buttons-wrapper`, now given `0.5lh`) depends on that value staying.
- Horizontal tabs measured with `block`, the README value. Other `--tf-display-horizontal-tabs` values place the title by their own static-position rules.
- Pointer focus and hover on titles are not measured. Firefox themes (lwtheme) and right-to-left are not in the title matrix.

## T28 boxes paint --tf-bg under Firefox themes

Reported by the user on Firefox 159 with the Nova theme `nova-ash@mozilla.org`: the "navbar" title showed as a black box on gray (§B B19).

Facts:

- Firefox 159.0's theme picker (`moz-src/browser/themes/ThemesList.sys.mjs`) offers the built-in Light, Dark and Alpenglow themes and 11 Nova themes. The Nova themes are not built in: the picker installs them from AMO (`nova-{sun,spark,flame,flare,lavender,dusk,lagoon,pine,tide,ash,smoke}@mozilla.org`, version 1.1). Spec amended first (`43e8605`).
- Each Nova theme has a `theme` (light) and a `dark_theme` variant, picked by the color scheme. Each sets `additional_backgrounds` gradients with `backgrounds_area: window` and a half-transparent toolbar color.
- Built-in Light and Dark (`firefox-compact-{light,dark}@mozilla.org`) are in-app themes: Firefox's own CSS forced to one color scheme. They set no `lwtheme` attribute and no `--lwt-*` colors, so `--tf-bg` falls back to `-moz-dialog`. Alpenglow is an lwtheme.

Cause:

- `--tf-bg` follows `--lwt-accent-color`, the theme's frame color (black for nova-ash dark).
- A theme's header and additional images are `--toolbox-background-image`, painted as body's background-image above `--tf-bg`. B16's rule cleared it only without a theme (`:root:not([lwtheme]) body`), so a Nova theme's window gradient showed under every box that leaves its background to the window: navbar, URL bar text area, tabs.
- A title paints `--tf-bg`, so on a box showing the gradient it was a dark patch.

Fix:

- `chrome/browser.css` sets `--toolbox-background-image: none` on body with or without a theme, next to `--toolbox-background-gradient: none`. textfox takes the theme's frame color as its background; the theme's toolbar color and images do not show.
- B20, found by this task's light-scheme runs: the default `--tf-border` (`var(--panel-border-color, var(--toolbar-field-background-color))`) painted (240,240,244) on `--tf-bg` (250,250,251) under built-in Light, browser Nova off. Border boxes were invisible. Every painted run before T28 had a dark scheme. User's choice: fix in T28. Default `--tf-border` is now `color-mix(in srgb, var(--tf-fg) 20%, var(--tf-bg))` in `chrome/defaults.css`, the `border.color` default in `nix/modules/options.nix`, and README (§V V16).

Probes:

- `tools/firefox-chrome/ff_themes.py`: the Nova XPIs pinned by AMO file path and sha256, fetched once into `~/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/themes` and checked on every use. A spec `ID[:light|dark]` sets `ui.systemUsesDarkTheme` before startup. `apply()` installs from file, enables the theme in the chrome context and fails unless it is in effect: lwtheme set with an accent color, or for in-app themes active with the forced scheme. The scheme must match.
- `--theme ID[:scheme]` (repeatable) and `--themes default|every` on `test-surface-paint-live.py` (T15), `test-surface-paint-more-live.py` (T20) and `test-titles-paint-live.py` (T27). `default` is compact-dark dark, compact-light light, nova-ash dark and light. `every` is the 11 Nova themes in both variants, compact-dark, compact-light and Alpenglow in both schemes (26 setups).
- T27 gains a check: the color beside each title (a 16px strip right of it, below the border line) must match the title's background within 4 per channel. It is what B19 looked like.
- The T15 judge is unchanged, so §V V13 owes no cross-platform rerun.

Red at the old CSS (worktree at HEAD; `chrome/` unchanged since `6ad43d0`; nova-ash dark, Firefox 159.0):

- T15: navbar, urlbar-text and tabs paint the gradient, not `--tf-bg`.
- T20 classic: tabs and the private window's navbar fail.
- T27 at ratio 1: navbar, bookmarks and horizontal tabs titles have background (18,17,20) on box paint (60,59,63), the B19 symptom.
- B20: T20 `--themes default` failed under compact-light, Nova off, on "indistinguishable from its background".

Green on the final CSS (Firefox 159.0 build `20261022150525`, Linux, Sway, headed):

- `test-surface-paint-live.py --themes every`: 156 runs (26 setups, both browser Nova states, defaults, sentinel and known-bad configs); 9 surfaces each paint textfox styling, known-bad fails. Exit 0. Without a theme: 6 runs, exit 0.
- `test-surface-paint-more-live.py --themes default`: 48 runs (4 setups, both Nova states, classic and revamp, 3 configs), 23 surfaces. Covers V16: built-in Light borders are no longer "indistinguishable from its background". Without a theme: 12 runs. Exit 0 both.
- `test-titles-paint-live.py --dpp 1 --themes every`: 104 runs, 624 titles whole, on the line, clear of controls and on their box's paint. Without a theme at ratio 1 and 2: exit 0.
- `test-a11y-paint-live.py`, `check-config-defaults.py`, `check-option-wiring.py` (25), `check-option-parity.py` (6), `nixfmt-tree --ci`: exit 0.

Harness limit: with `TMPDIR` set to a longer path (`~/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/tmp/t28/final`), T15 failed to connect to Marionette ("Timed out"); the same run passes with a shorter one. The likely cause is the socket path length under `paint_display`'s display directory. The default `/tmp` is short; it ran out of quota here, which is why `TMPDIR` was moved.

Bounds:

- Linux only. Themes on macOS and Windows, and GPU rendering, are not checked.
- Surfaces outside T15, T20 and T27 under themes are not checked.
- A theme's toolbar, tab and text colors are not used. `--tf-fg` is `contrast-color(--tf-bg)`; users who want the theme's text color set `var(--lwt-text-color)`.

## T33 theme gradient and theme images (issue #1)

Requested in issue #1: show a Firefox theme's art, which T28 switched off to fix B19. User decisions (2026-11-15): split by source, a scrim behind text, art behind the whole window.

Facts (Firefox release `browser/themes/shared/browser-shared.css`, Firefox 160.0 here):

- `:root` sets `--toolbox-background-image: none`; with browser Nova `var(--toolbox-background-gradient)`; `[lwtheme]` `var(--lwt-additional-images)`; `[lwtheme-image]` `var(--lwt-header-image), var(--lwt-additional-images)`; `[mini-window]` none. A theme replaces browser Nova's gradient, so the two never stack. A Nova theme's gradients are its `additional_backgrounds`, in the same list as image layers; CSS cannot split that list per layer.
- Firefox paints that list on `:root:not([theme-image-in-toolbox]) body` or `:root[theme-image-in-toolbox] #navigator-toolbox`, with `--toolbox-background-repeat/position/size`; the findbar under a theme layers it too.
- Linux's native tokens set `--toolbox-background-gradient: image(transparent)` (`tokens-platform.css`); macOS and Windows paint `linear-gradient(96deg, …50% violet… 39.84%, …50% orange… 101.72%)`.
- Style queries are on in release (`layout.css.style-queries.enabled: true`, `StaticPrefList.yaml`), with range syntax (`servo/components/style/queries/feature_expression.rs` `QueryStyleRange`, numbers compare). `:root` has no query container, so a query reads the option from body's parent.

Design (`chrome/browser.css`, `sidebar.css`, `findbar.css`, `defaults.css`):

- `--tf-theme-gradient`, `--tf-theme-images`: 0 (default) or 1. `:root { --textfox-root-art: var(--toolbox-background-image) }` keeps Firefox's own list; body still sets Firefox's variable to none, so Firefox paints nothing itself.
- `@container style(--tf-theme-gradient >= 1) { :root:not([lwtheme]) body {…} }` and `@container style(--tf-theme-images >= 1) { :root[lwtheme] body {…} }` set `--textfox-art` to that list, `--textfox-scrim` to `color-mix(in srgb, var(--tf-bg) 80%, transparent)` and `--textfox-box-bg` to transparent. Values 1 and above count as on (a clamp like `--tf-container-frame`'s); only 0 and 1 are documented.
- body and every section title `::before` paint `background-image: var(--textfox-art)` with Firefox's repeat, position and size lists, `background-attachment: fixed`, over their `--tf-bg` color, and the scrim as `box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 100vmax`. An inset shadow paints above the background, so the scrim covers the art without adding a layer that would shift Firefox's per-layer lists. A title stays opaque (hides the border line) and paints exactly the window's paint beside it.
- Boxes that painted `--tf-bg` take `--textfox-box-bg`: `.browserContainer`, `.browser-toolbox-background`, `#browser`, `#sidebar-box` and its `--sidebar-background-color`, `sidebar-main` under a theme, the findbar and its field. The sidebar splitter's `ThreeDFace` (toolkit `splitter.css`, an author sheet, so the rule needs `!important`) goes transparent under `@container style(--textfox-art-on: 1)`, a flag only the two option blocks set.
- Both 0: no image, a transparent shadow, every box `--tf-bg`; pixel-identical to `c2c4b79` (below).
- Scrim 80%: at 70% the URL bar text over Rosy Sky's darker sky reached about 4.4:1 (0 pixels at 4.5:1). At 80% every T15 text target passes over all art setups; Rosy Sky's URL bar text is the thinnest, 56 pixels at 4.5:1 against a floor of 30.
- APNG animation is shared by every user of the image, so titles and window stay on the same frame (checked below).

Probes:

- `ff_themes.py`: image themes pinned by AMO file path and sha256 (AMO API `addons/addon/{guid}`; versions match the issue): Purple starfield - Animated 1.0 (`4061627`, APNG 540×115), Rosy Sky 4.0 (`4660468`, `theme_frame` JPEG), Topo! Black 1.0 (`5117135`, PNG 480×270). The issue's profile copies were not on this machine; the files come from AMO only. `ART_THEMES` (`--themes art`) is those three and nova-ash light and dark. `ART_CONFIG` holds the config.css lines per `--art` mode; `gradient` also stands in `TEST_GRADIENT`, a visible 50% violet-to-orange gradient shaped like macOS's, because Linux paints the real one transparent. `art_expected` reads Firefox's own `--toolbox-background-image` at `:root` (never textfox's CSS) to decide whether a setup has art. `hold_still` stops animated images for capture comparisons through `windowUtils.imageAnimationMode` (the pref `image.animation_mode` does not reach chrome windows) and repaints all art once, since a region repainted later showed another frame.
- `--art none|images|gradient` on T15 (`test-surface-paint-live.py`), T20 (`test-surface-paint-more-live.py`) and T27 (`test-titles-paint-live.py`). Where Firefox has the art, window boxes are judged over it: with the box hidden the art paints, and the box must leave at least 60% of those pixels unchanged; at least one box per run must have 100 art pixels under it (a gradient can equal the frame color under a whole box, as nova-ash light's does on the left); text must reach 4.5:1 against the paint under each text pixel (the capture with the text hidden). Popups keep the `--tf-bg` judge. T20's one-frame check compares each gap with a capture whose `.browserContainer` is hidden. T27's blend check becomes: the title with its text (and text shadow) transparent paints what the capture without the title paints, across its glyph box except the border rows, and in those rows differs from it (hides the line) in 90% of the pixels. An `--art` run in which Firefox had the art nowhere fails rather than passing on the plain judge; Linux's `image(transparent)` gradient counts as no art. The surface diff of a box judged over art (not of popups, whose `--tf-bg` can sit within noise of faint art behind them) ignores noise up to 2 (Rosy Sky's 8k JPEG rasterizes 1 unit differently where a region repaints). The judge without `--art` is unchanged, so §V V13 owes no cross-platform rerun.
- `test-theme-art-live.py` (new): per setup, both browser Nova states, classic layout. Options 0 (an author sheet) must paint the same pixels as the chrome at `--baseline` (default `c2c4b79`); with the option on, pixels painting flat `--tf-bg` and the frame color must paint something else in every box (`FLAT` lists the one measured exception, nova-ash light's sidebar, where the gradient equals the frame color); the starfield must animate with the refresh driver running and across paused frames; at three paused frames (`advanceTimeAndRefresh`) every title with transparent text must equal the window without it, and must cover 90% of the border line under it. `--identity`: with no option in config.css, the window paints as `--baseline` for no theme and the default theme set, both Nova states, classic and revamp.
- Harness gaps fixed on the way: T27's reference title now copies `text-shadow` (B24); T20's known-bad rule, over art only, also counts a box that paints nothing and a border "indistinguishable from its background" (over art a box drops its `--tf-bg`; on Topo's black frame the known-bad config then paints nothing, and Rosy Sky's text shadow keeps the known-bad text legible); T20 decides art for the private window from that window.


Red (harness at `9faf97f` copied into a worktree at `c2c4b79`; Topo! Black unless named):

- `test-theme-art-live.py --art images --nova false --baseline ''`: art paints 0 of 52460 `--tf-bg` pixels in every box. Exit 1.
- T15 `--art images`: navbar, URL bar text, tabs, findbar "theme art paints 0 pixels under it". T20 `--art images --nova false --layout classic` (final harness): "theme art paints under none of sidebar, content-box, tabs, split panels, private boxes" in defaults and sentinel. Exit 1.
- T27 `--art images --nova false` on the T33 CSS with titles left out of the art rule (mutation): every title "paints a patch over the theme art" (20 to 47 pixels). Exit 1.

Green (Firefox 160.0, Linux, Sway, headed; `TMPDIR=~/.cache/tfx`, `/tmp` was near its quota):

- `test-theme-art-live.py`: 11 setups (three image themes and nova-ash light and dark, both Nova states; gradient, Nova on). Options 0 paint 0 pixels unlike `c2c4b79`; art in every box but nova-ash light's sidebar; titles cover 100% of the line under them and patch 0 pixels at 3 paused frames; the starfield animates (34645 pixels free-running, 223 between paused frames). Exit 0.
- `test-theme-art-live.py --identity`: 20 setups (no theme and the 4 default themes, both Nova states, classic and revamp), no option set, 0 pixels unlike `c2c4b79`. Exit 0.
- Both options 0: T15 `--themes every` 156 runs, exit 0; T15 without a theme, exit 0; T27 `--dpp 1 --themes every` 104 runs, exit 0; T27 without a theme at ratio 1 and 2, exit 0; T20 `--themes default` 48 runs and without a theme, exit 0.
- Images on: T15 `--art images --themes art` 30 runs, exit 0 (scrim 80%); T20 `--art images` per theme (starfield, Rosy Sky, Topo, nova-ash light and dark) 60 runs, exit 0; T27 `--dpp 1 --art images --themes art` 20 runs: only B24 (Rosy Sky navbar title overlaps the first button, 4 runs), the same as without art at `c2c4b79`; T27 `--dpp 1 --art images --themes every` 104 runs (one "captures unstable" abort at run 61, its remaining 44 rerun), exit 0.
- T15 `--art images --themes every` 156 runs (138 judged over art): 154 pass; nova-smoke light, defaults config, both Nova states fails "theme art paints under none of navbar, urlbar-text, tabs, findbar". Its gradient differs from the frame color by at most 1/255 in the navbar once the 80% scrim covers it (measured), under the 4/255 floor: there is no visible art. Exit 1; left strict.
- Gradient on: T15 `--art gradient` 6 runs, exit 0; T20 `--art gradient --nova true` classic and revamp, exit 0; T27 `--dpp 1 --art gradient` and `--art gradient --themes every` (104 runs; art judged where Firefox has it, compact Light and Dark with Nova on), exit 0.
- `check-option-wiring.py` (28 options), `check-option-parity.py` (9), `check-config-defaults.py`, `nixfmt-tree --ci` (0 changed): exit 0. nixpkgs `/nix/store/0ya4nbmac3fw13w4hmqyc41svdf7z352-source` (26.11).
- Not run: T20 `--themes every` (312 runs, about 15 hours here); options 0 there rest on T20 `--themes default`, the T15 and T27 every runs and the pixel identity runs.

Resume:

```sh
P="$HOME/.cache/textfox-firefox-live/venv/bin/python"; export TMPDIR="$HOME/.cache/tfx"
$P tools/firefox-chrome/test-theme-art-live.py            # and --identity
$P tools/firefox-chrome/test-surface-paint-live.py --art images --themes art
$P tools/firefox-chrome/test-surface-paint-more-live.py --art images --theme '{2140e74f-2373-4cd1-85dd-984632a6abc7}'
$P tools/firefox-chrome/test-titles-paint-live.py --dpp 1 --art images --themes art
$P tools/firefox-chrome/test-surface-paint-live.py --art gradient
```

Bounds:

- Linux only. The macOS and Windows gradient (`--tf-theme-gradient` with the default theme) is shown only through the Linux stand-in; macOS by eye is not checked (no Mac here).
- B24: header-image themes give titles Firefox's text shadow, which reaches 1px onto the first toolbar button (T27 fails on Rosy Sky, with or without the options). Open; needs a decision.
- Private windows: with browser Nova on and no theme, Firefox gives a private window its own lwtheme without images, so `--tf-theme-gradient` shows nothing there; T20 reads that from the private window.
- Faint art vanishes under the scrim: nova-smoke light's gradient paints within 1/255 of `--tf-bg` with images on.
- A gradient can equal the frame color over a whole box (nova-ash light at the sidebar and URL bar); nothing shows there.
- The art runs behind the whole window even when Firefox would paint it on the toolbox only (`:root[theme-image-in-toolbox]`), as decided.
- Not handled: Nova's expand-on-hover sidebar launcher (`[sidebar-launcher-expanded]`) paints its own opaque toolbox color; the selected and hovered tab, Nova's vertical-tab scroll fade and tab badges keep opaque tints; web pages paint their own background (about:blank is transparent and shows the art).

- `--tf-theme-scrim` (default 80%, clamped 0%..100%; Nix `themeScrim`) sets the wash. `test-theme-art-live.py` navbar strength (mean channel distance from options 0): starfield 0 / 7.7 / 25.4 and Rosy Sky 0 / 37 / 113 at 100% / 80% / 40%.
